**Checkbox is one item, and Checkbox Group is a part of it rather than a catalogue row.** Base UI ships `Checkbox` and `CheckboxGroup` from two subpaths, and `Checkbox.Root` works standalone, which is why Data Table's recipe already names `checkbox` and not a second item. The compound rule still applies: the group is a primitive Ultima wraps, so it sits on the same namespace. `Group` is an invented name, the same move as `ToggleGroup.Item`, wrapping the bare `CheckboxGroup` export (there is no `CheckboxGroup.Root`). `nativeButton` stays Base UI's default `false`, so a wrapping `<label>` is legal, matching Switch.

**Radio is not a catalogue item.** Base UI's docs say Radio is always placed within Radio Group, and a bare `Radio.Root` has no `checkedValue`, no name, and no roving tabindex. One `radio-group` item imports both subpaths: `Root` wraps `RadioGroup`, `Item` wraps `Radio.Root` (invented, again like `ToggleGroup.Item`), `Indicator` wraps `Radio.Indicator`.

**Textarea is an item.** There is no Base UI Textarea; `Input render={<textarea />}` is a first-party type spec, and Input's size table sets `height`, which makes that render unusable as a multiline field. `textarea.tsx` is therefore not a one-line second item: it wears its own tables, `size` as `min-height` at the same three space steps as Input's `height`, and `resize: vertical`. Auto-resize is out of v0.1. `rows` and `cols` pass through and are not axes. Default `md`. No Ultima `input` registry dependency.

**No `size` on Checkbox or Radio Group.** `size` is the three control heights. Switch already refused them, and a checkbox next to an Input of a given size is aligned by `Field.Item`'s row, not by growing the box to 40px.

**Combobox is a sibling of Select, not a variant of it, and not Autocomplete.** Base UI ships them as three primitives. One file per component means one `combobox` item exposing every Base UI part, twenty-six of them, plus `useFilter`, `useFilteredItems`, and `createItems` on the same namespace, which is Toast's manager and Alert Dialog's `Handle` again: a value, not a second item. Twenty-six is what `@base-ui/react` 1.8.0's `combobox/index.parts` exports and what the styled split below lists; the twenty-four this paragraph first carried counted two parts short. Autocomplete is the free-text primitive, fifteen of whose parts are Combobox re-exports (counted in `@base-ui/react` 1.8.0's `autocomplete/index.parts`); the v0.1 checklist never named it, and folding two Roots into `combobox.tsx` would fight the file rule, so it is out of v0.1 and is not Command either. Command stays on the v0.2 line. Ultima owns no filter algorithm: Base UI's `Collection` / `createItems` is the filter, a filter library would be an engine under ADR 0007, and none is added. Do not wrap Ultima's `input` item: `Combobox.Input` is Base UI's combobox input, and wrapping `@ultima/ui/input` would inherit Input's `height` and put `input` in every combobox install, the same reason Textarea is its own file. Do not import Select or Menu either: restating the item style and the leading indicator slot in `combobox.tsx` costs a copy of tokens already in the file, and a sibling import would put `select` in every combobox install.

**The canonical Combobox pattern is Input-as-control.** `InputGroup` is the visual box and carries `size`, the sunken surface, the strong border, the radius, the validation selector, and the ring via `:focus-within`. `Input` is borderless inside it. That inverts Input-owns-its-box, and it is the composition Trigger and Clear actually sit in. `Trigger` is a styled chevron button, not a wrapper trigger. `Field.Label` keeps the default `nativeLabel={true}` because the control is an `<input>`. `Combobox.Label` names the **Trigger**, not the Input: use it only when Trigger is the control, with `nativeLabel={false}` on `Field.Label`. Input-inside-popup, grid/`Row`, and virtualization stay on the namespace; they are not v0.1 documented examples, except a second demo that shows the Label trap. Multiple/chips get a third demo because `Chip` paints. `Combobox.Empty` and `Combobox.Status` are the empty and status announcements: styled parts, `role="status"`, always mounted. They are not the v0.1 Empty component.

**Slider is seven parts, no axes, no `tone`.** Meter's `tone` is the color of a measurement; a Slider is a value picker, and recoloring the fill would make a volume control look like a warning. Fill is accent, or the `style` slot. `orientation`, `min`, `max`, `step`, `largeStep`, and `thumbAlignment` pass through and are not axes. Range (two thumbs) is in v0.1: one `Thumb` per value is already the API, `index` is already required for SSR, and deferring the example would not remove the part. Canonical `thumbAlignment` stays Base UI's default `"center"`, which injects no script. Ultima wires no pointer or keyboard behavior of its own.

**Alert is a static callout, and the docs' `Note` is not it.** There is no Base UI Alert; it is a plain component, item `alert`, four parts. It ships in v0.1 because the checklist named it. Today's `Note` stays docs-local as a styled paragraph, decided on ULT-56 and confirmed here: it paints nothing, so deleting it the way `data-table.tsx` was deleted would put a surface on content that currently has none. The inventory sentence stands. If `Note` later paints a surface, it becomes Alert then. `tone` is the only axis, Badge's six values, default `neutral`, no `variant` and no `size`. Paint is Badge's `subtle` column, restated in `alert.tsx`; do not import Badge. No live role: `Root` is a `<div>`. No built-in glyphs: `Icon` is the caller's, omitted when unused, and `aria-hidden`. The visible text is the non-colour signal. Title is canonical in demos and defaults to `<h3>` through `useRender`, like Card, but is not Dialog's always-rendered Title.

**Alert Dialog is a separate item from Dialog, not a recipe.** Sheet is a Dialog recipe because it *is* Dialog with placement overrides. Alert Dialog is a different Base UI Root: `modal` and `disablePointerDismissal` are forced `true` and `Omit`ted from the props type, and `Popup` is `role="alertdialog"`. A recipe over Ultima's Dialog cannot produce that. Item `alert-dialog`, one file, every Base UI part. `Handle` / `createHandle` is a value, not a provider. Do not import Ultima Dialog; restate Viewport, Backdrop, Popup, Title, and Description with the same overlay tokens Dialog uses, the same reason Combobox does not import Select. It joins the overlay recipe as the seventh overlay and does not vary it. Escape closes; a backdrop click does not. Title always rendered, and Description always rendered too: the APG requires `aria-describedby` on `alertdialog`. Ultima sets neither `initialFocus` nor `finalFocus`.

**Toast is outside the overlay recipe, and Ultima owns a live-region pattern rather than an announcer.** Item `toast`, one file, every Base UI part, plus `useToastManager` and `createToastManager` on the same namespace (`createHandle` is the precedent: a value, not a second item). Do not invent `useToast()`. It portals a floating surface and is not an overlay: not modal, not anchored in the documented case, and `z.toast` already sits above `z.popup`. Calling it the eighth overlay would either force `z.popup` or add a second z to a recipe defined as one constant. Canonical demo is stacked. Anchored `Positioner` / `Arrow` stay on the namespace and are not a v0.1 documented example; `copy-button.tsx` does not become an anchored toast. `tone` is the colour axis, Badge's six values, default `neutral`, and it is not a React prop on Root: callers pass Base UI `type` on `add()` / `promise()`, Ultima styles `[data-type]`. `error` paints as `danger` so `promise()` works; `loading` paints as `neutral`; those two strings are aliases, not extra axis values. Do not wrap `add()`. `priority` stays Base UI's and is not an axis. `Toast.Provider` is a pass-through part the consumer mounts with Portal and Viewport once in their tree; Portal and Viewport stay mounted even when the stack is empty. Ultima writes no context for this component, so ADR 0006 is unchanged. Swipe is Base UI's. Proof bar item 7 is not this component.

**Progress restates Meter and owns the indeterminate loop.** Item `progress`, one file, all five Base UI parts, all styled. Product boundary already closed: Progress is task completion, Meter is a bounded measurement. They cannot import each other: registry `registryDependencies` only match `@ultima/*` specifiers, and a relative `./meter` is skipped, the same reason Slider restated track geometry and Combobox restated item styles. Restate Meter's Track (sunken, full-radius, space-step-2 height, `overflow: hidden`) and Label (text step 2, uppercase, wide tracking, subtle) in `progress.tsx`. **`tone` on Root**, Meter's five values, default `neutral`, reaching Indicator and Value through file-private context, each overridable. Slider refused `tone` because it is a value picker; Progress is a measurement of completion, so a failed upload and a finished job are tones. No `variant`. No `size`. Determinate Indicator fill is the toned colour; width comes from Base UI's inline `%`. Determinate `transitionProperty: width` over `--ult-motion-base` with `standard` easing — a motion token, not `500ms`. Root is a styled Root (column, gap), like Meter. Indeterminate is still Progress, not a Spinner recipe: keep the `progressbar` role and `'indeterminate progress'` valuetext the primitive already sets. Base UI writes no inline style when `value` is `null`; Ultima styles `[data-indeterminate]` with this file's keyframes, duration `--ult-motion-loop`, and the reduced-motion static fallback (25% width, `animationName: none`). Do not invent a `variant="indeterminate"` axis. Canonical determinate demo plus a second indeterminate demo. Proof bar item 7 is not this component.

**Skeleton, Spinner, and Empty all ship, and none has a Base UI primitive.** Badge, Card, Stat, and Code are the precedent for plain elements. The checklist named all three, and Empty sits on the page-layout line: a docs file may arrange type, and may neither paint a surface nor hand the user a control from plain elements.

**Skeleton** is item `skeleton`, one part, `Root`. A sunken rounded placeholder. Pulse keyframes in this file, `--ult-motion-loop`, static under reduced motion. No axes. Size is the `style` slot, and docs examples use space-scale heights. It paints a surface, so it is not page layout.

**Spinner** is item `spinner`, one part, `Root`. A circular looping mark drawn in CSS, not a lucide asset and not a glyph slot: Iconography stays the caller's for real icons, and this is the loading mark. `--ult-motion-loop`, static glyph under reduced motion. No axes. No `tone`. Not a Progress. Toast `type="loading"` stays a toast, not a Spinner. Do not make Spinner a Progress recipe, and do not make Progress render a Spinner.

**Empty** is item `empty`, four parts, Alert-shaped: `Root`, `Title`, `Description`, `Icon`. No `tone`, no `variant`, no `size`. Root sets a centered column, gap, and a sunken surface so docs do not. Title and Description set type. `Icon` is decorative (`aria-hidden`), same rule as Alert: the caller's, omitted when unused, not a built-in glyph. An action is **children**, a catalogue `Button`, not a fifth part and not a plain `<button>`. **Not Card** (Card is content that exists; Empty is the absence of a collection). **Not Alert** (Alert is a callout about a condition). **Not page layout** (it paints, and it hands the user a control). **Not `Combobox.Empty`** (that part stays the list's mounted `role="status"`; callers do not replace it with this item).

| Component | Styled | Passed through |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Field | `Root`, `Label`, `Description`, `Error`, `Item` | `Control`, `Validity` |
| Fieldset | `Root`, `Legend` | none |
| Checkbox | `Root`, `Indicator`, `Group` | none |
| Radio Group | `Root`, `Item`, `Indicator` | none |
| Combobox | `Label`, `InputGroup`, `Input`, `Trigger`, `Icon`, `Clear`, `Chip`, `ChipRemove`, `Popup`, `Arrow`, `Status`, `Empty`, `List`, `Item`, `ItemIndicator`, `Separator`, `GroupLabel` | `Root`, `Value`, `Collection`, `Portal`, `Backdrop`, `Group`, `Row`, `Chips`. `Positioner` (`outline: 0`) |
| Slider | `Label`, `Value`, `Control`, `Track`, `Indicator`, `Thumb` | `Root` |
| Alert | `Root`, `Title`, `Description`, `Icon` | none |
| Alert Dialog | `Viewport`, `Backdrop`, `Popup`, `Title`, `Description` | `Root`, `Trigger`, `Portal`, `Close` |
| Toast | `Viewport`, `Root`, `Content`, `Title`, `Description`, `Arrow` | `Provider`, `Portal`, `Positioner` (`outline: 0`), `Action`, `Close` |
| Progress | `Root`, `Label`, `Track`, `Indicator`, `Value` | none |
| Empty | `Root`, `Title`, `Description`, `Icon` | none |

`Field.Root` owns the vertical rhythm between the label, the control, the description, and the error, which is spacing and so the first clause. `Description` carries `margin: 0` to kill the UA `<p>` margin along with its type. `Error` carries type and `--ult-color-danger-text`. `Item` is the per-row layout inside a group. `Validity` renders no element at all, the only part in the system with no DOM, so it is passed through for want of anything to style. `Fieldset.Root` is the one styled part in the system whose styling exists mostly to undo the UA stylesheet's border, margin, and padding on `<fieldset>`. Textarea is a single-part component, so it has no row above; its root is styled, like Input. `Checkbox.Indicator` and `RadioGroup.Indicator` are the second clause: `keepMounted` defaults to `false`, so the indicator stays in the DOM through its exit transition carrying `data-unchecked`, and without `display: none` in that window the glyph flashes on uncheck. `Checkbox.Group` and `RadioGroup.Root` are grouping parts the first clause would pass through; they are styled anyway because they own the column layout and gap of a stacked set, and the system-wide unstyled `RadioGroup` in the v0 table is Menu's grouping part, not this component. The focus ring is on the control — `Checkbox.Root`, `RadioGroup.Item` — not on a wrapping label, matching Switch.

`Combobox.Popup` and `Combobox.List` are clause 2: Popup needs `width: var(--anchor-width)` and `max-width: var(--available-width)` to track the input, and List must be the scroll container (`overflow-y: auto; overscroll-behavior: contain; max-height: min(<n>, var(--available-height))` with matching block padding and scroll-padding) or a long filtered list runs off the screen and keyboard highlight has nothing to scroll. Select.List stays pass-through; Combobox.List does not copy it. Combobox has no scroll-arrow parts. `Slider.Control` and `Slider.Track` are clause 2 twice over: Control's box model is read back with `getComputedStyle` to compute the drag offset and it needs `touch-action: none` and `user-select: none` or a touch drag scrolls the page, and Track needs an explicit cross-axis size or Indicator inherits `auto` and has none. Slider.Root only groups, so it passes through. The Slider ring cannot be written `:focus-visible` on Thumb: the focusable element is a nested visually-hidden `<input type="range">`, so the ring is `:has(:focus-visible)` on Thumb. Alert is a plain component, so every part is styled. Alert Dialog's split is Dialog's: the same five styled, the same four passed through, because seven of its nine parts are Dialog re-exports and Trigger and Close are wrapper triggers. Toast.Root is a styled Root, like Tabs: it paints a surface and its stacking CSS is clause 2. Viewport is clause 2 for the frontmost-height clamp and `z.toast`. Content is `[data-behind]` opacity. Action and Close are wrapper triggers. Progress.Track is clause 2: explicit height plus `overflow: hidden`, or the fill is invisible, the same silent failure Slider.Track has. Progress.Indicator in indeterminate mode is clause 2 as well: Base UI writes no inline style, so without Ultima's keyframes the bar is an empty track. Empty is a plain component, so every part is styled. Skeleton and Spinner are single-part, so they have no row above; their roots are styled, like Badge.

### The navigation set

Breadcrumb, Pagination, and Navigation Menu, the three v0.2 components that render a navigation landmark. Settled on [Breadcrumb's parts, its separator, and the name on its landmark](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/88), [Pagination's parts, its semantics, and whether Ultima owns the page window](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/89), [Whether Pagination's controls come from Button, and what that means for a system with no className](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/91), and [Navigation Menu's parts, its two nav landmarks, and whether it is the eighth overlay](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/92), assembled on [Assemble the contracts into the spec and the catalogue](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/93), which closed the map.

**It is named for what it holds rather than for a release, and that was the assembly ticket's own question.** v0.2 is thirteen checklist lines and these are three of them, so "The v0.2 set" would have been a section named for a release while describing a fraction of it. The v0.1 set faced the mirror image and resolved it the same way: it is not "The v0.1 form set" because seven of its fourteen rows are not forms. What these three share is not a release date but a landmark — every one of them puts a `<nav>` on the page, which is why one naming rule recurs in all three rows below and why they were charted on one map. The rest of v0.2 sections itself when it is charted.

| Component | Item | Built on | Parts | Axes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Breadcrumb | `breadcrumb` | Plain elements | `Root`, `List`, `Item`, `Link`, `Separator` | none |
| Pagination | `pagination` | Plain elements | `Root`, `List`, `Item`, `Page`, `Previous`, `Next`, `Ellipsis` | none |
| Navigation Menu | `navigation-menu` | Base UI `NavigationMenu` | `Root`, `List`, `Item`, `Trigger`, `Icon`, `Content`, `Portal`, `Positioner`, `Popup`, `Viewport`, `Backdrop`, `Arrow`, `Link` | none |

`Pagination.getPages` sits on the `Pagination` namespace as a value, beside the exported `PaginationPage` type, the way Combobox carries `useFilter`, `useFilteredItems`, and `createItems`. Navigation Menu's `orientation` is Base UI's pass-through and is not an axis, the way Separator's is not.

**One landmark rule across all three, and it is the reason they are one section.** Each `Root` renders a `<nav>` and carries a default `aria-label` — `"Breadcrumb"`, `"Pagination"`, and on `NavigationMenu.Popup` `"Submenu"` — **emitted only when the caller supplied neither `aria-label` nor `aria-labelledby`**, so an override never leaves two competing names on one landmark. Distinguishing a second instance on the same page is the consumer's, stated on each docs page. None of the three is type-enforced and **the accessibility contract's type-enforced attributes were two when this was written, three since [#110](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/110), and four since [#279](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/279)**: Breadcrumb and Pagination supply defaults, so a type cannot require what already exists, and `NavigationMenu.Root` is rendered as a `<div>` when nested, which a type cannot see, so requiring the attribute would force a meaningless one onto a nested menu. `NavigationMenu.Root` joins `ToggleGroup.Root` as a documented rather than enforced case.

Pagination's is the one knowing departure from the APG. Its landmark-regions practice recommends that pagination above and below a table share one label, and axe-core's `landmark-unique` fails duplicate role plus duplicate accessible name. The sweep is what Ultima enforces, and two identically named landmarks are ambiguous in a landmark list either way, so the two take distinct names. The Data Table pagination example is the first demo that will carry both sets.

**Breadcrumb is an `<ol>` and Pagination is a `<ul>`, deliberately.** This is the one place the two plain components answer one question two ways, and the reason is in the numbering: a list's item count collides with a page's number, so "item 3 of 7" read over page 42 is worse than no count at all, and `aria-posinset` and `aria-setsize` are not permitted on `link`, so the honest count cannot be restored by hand. A breadcrumb trail is ordered and complete; a page window is a set with holes punched in it.

**The current item is one mechanism in four components now.** `Sidebar.Link`'s `active` prop, `Breadcrumb.Link`'s `active`, `Pagination.Page`'s `current`, and `NavigationMenu.Link`'s `active` all emit `aria-current="page"` with `data-active=""` beside it, and all four style it with `':is([data-active], [aria-current="page"])'`, so a router link that sets the attribute itself is styled with no prop. The first three pass it as `useRender` state; Navigation Menu's is Base UI's own prop. It is `"page"` and never `"true"`.

**Every part of Breadcrumb and Pagination is styled**, since Ultima writes every element; they have no pass-through parts. Navigation Menu's split has a row in the Styled parts table below, and `Positioner`, `Viewport`, and `List` are that rule's second clause rather than exceptions.

### The overlay set

Popover, Drawer, Context Menu, and Hover Card, the four v0.2 components that join the overlay recipe as its ninth through twelfth members, and Menubar, the strip their menus open from. Sectioned on [Where the five overlay contracts are written, and where they land in catalogue order](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/117), which is the sectioning The navigation set left to this map when it said the rest of v0.2 sections itself when it is charted.

**Menubar is the one member that is not itself an overlay**, stated under Overlays and not softened here. A set is a contract grouping and an overlay is a recipe membership, which are different claims about a component: Menubar portals nothing and floats nothing, and it is in this table because its contract is meaningless apart from the Dropdown Menus that open from it. Splitting the five across the two checklist lines that name them was the alternative and is declined on [What ships as an item, what ships as a recipe, and where each lands in the overlay recipe](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/107), which found that three-and-three split to be an artefact of how the checklist sentence was written rather than a difference between the components; it would have put Context Menu and Popover in different sections while they share a recipe, a surface, and a `z` token. The rows are in overlay order with the one exception last, and that order is the catalogue's: `apps/docs/src/components.ts` appends the five after The navigation set's three, each carrying `release: 'v0.2'`, which widens the existing disclosure and adds no `RELEASE_LABELS` key.

**Sheet is not in this table and is not in the catalogue.** It is a documented Dialog recipe with no installable unit, settled on [Sheet's recipe contract: what owns it, what it depends on, and how it differs from Sidebar's mobile menu](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/114), and it lives as a section on Dialog's page.

Each row's styled split lives in this section rather than in the Styled parts table, following The v0.1 set. Every row also has a line in the Accessibility contract, which is one table for the whole catalogue.

| Component | Item | Built on | Parts | Axes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Popover | `popover` | Base UI `Popover` | `Root`, `Trigger`, `Portal`, `Positioner`, `Popup`, `Arrow`, `Backdrop`, `Title`, `Description`, `Close`, `Viewport`; `createHandle` / `Handle` alongside the parts | none |
| Drawer | `drawer` | Base UI `Drawer` | `Root`, `Provider`, `Trigger`, `Portal`, `Backdrop`, `Viewport`, `Popup`, `Content`, `Title`, `Description`, `Close`, `Indent`, `IndentBackground`, `SwipeArea`, `VirtualKeyboardProvider`; `createHandle` / `Handle` alongside the parts | none |
| Context Menu | `context-menu` | Base UI `ContextMenu` | `Root`, `Trigger`, `Portal`, `Backdrop`, `Positioner`, `Popup`, `Arrow`, `Item`, `LinkItem`, `Group`, `GroupLabel`, `Separator`, `CheckboxItem`, `CheckboxItemIndicator`, `RadioGroup`, `RadioItem`, `RadioItemIndicator`, `SubmenuRoot`, `SubmenuTrigger` | none |
| Hover Card | `hover-card` | Base UI `PreviewCard` | `Root`, `Trigger`, `Portal`, `Positioner`, `Popup`, `Arrow`, `Backdrop`, `Viewport`; `createHandle` / `Handle` alongside the parts | none |
| Menubar | `menubar` | Base UI `Menubar` | one part, the bar itself | none |

**Popover** is the ninth overlay, settled on [Popover's parts, its styled split, and what names it](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/108). It joins the recipe unvaried: the surface, `z.popup`, and the opacity-plus-`scale(0.98)` transition are `popover.tsx`'s own restatement, as Alert Dialog's are. Base UI's own `modal` default (`false`) stands, and `openOnHover` stays available for the pattern Hover Card formalises. **It sits on `Trigger`, not on `Root`**, together with `delay` (300) and `closeDelay` (0), which Base UI documents as requiring it; `PopoverRoot` declares none of the three. Corrected on [Hover Card's name, its contract, and whether hover-only disclosure is acceptable](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/111) from the `Root` this sentence first named, verified in the 1.8.0 declarations. The older shape was right until recently, which is how the error was available to make: Base UI moved Preview Card's two delay props from `Root` to `Trigger` as a breaking change in `v1.0.0-beta.5`. Styled: `Popup` (the recipe surface), `Title`, `Description`, `Arrow`, and `Viewport`. Passed through: `Root`, `Trigger` and `Close` (the wrapper rule), `Portal`, `Positioner` (`outline: 0`), and `Backdrop`, an invisible click-catcher like Menu's and Select's, because a popover by default blocks nothing (`modal: false`) and a visible scrim belongs to the modal Dialogs. `Viewport` is the styled split's one surprise and the second clause again, at a minimum rather than Navigation Menu's size morph: when one popup serves multiple triggers, the outgoing content renders as an absolutely positioned inert snapshot while the incoming one animates, and an unpositioned container lets that snapshot ride the document flow. One declaration, `position: relative`, contains it; `Viewport`'s `data-activation-direction` and transition attributes stay unused, because Ultima morphs nothing on trigger change. The full contract is the **Popover** note under Per-component notes.

**Drawer** is the tenth overlay and the third stated variation, settled on [Drawer's parts, its styled split, and whether it varies the overlay recipe](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/113) over the facts in `docs/research/2026-09-16-base-ui-drawer.md`. **All fifteen parts ship and the compound rule does not bend**: that rule's own sentence already names `Tooltip.Provider`, a part rendering no element, so `Drawer.Provider`, `Drawer.VirtualKeyboardProvider`, and `Content`, whose whole implementation is stamping `data-drawer-content`, need no exception. **Five parts are literal Dialog re-exports** — `Trigger`, `Portal`, `Title`, `Description`, `Close` — so what Dialog's contract already says about them transfers unchanged. The two providers nest in opposite directions and only one is safe to misplace: `Provider` sits above `Root`, is optional, and fails silently; `VirtualKeyboardProvider` sits inside `Root`, throws outside it, and no-ops without a `Viewport`. Both are documented, because a type cannot see an ancestor.

**Its styled split is Dialog's five plus `SwipeArea`.** Styled: `Viewport`, `Backdrop`, `Popup`, `Title`, `Description`, `SwipeArea`. Passed through: `Root`, `Provider`, `Trigger`, `Portal`, `Close`, `Content`, `Indent`, `IndentBackground`, `VirtualKeyboardProvider`. `drawer.tsx` restates rather than importing `dialog`, the eighth component to carry that instruction, and it is Combobox's shape rather than Alert Dialog's: `Title` and `Description` are verbatim while `Viewport` and `Popup` genuinely diverge. **Four parts are second-clause**, which makes Drawer the heaviest component in the catalogue for that clause. `Popup`'s **resting** transform must read `var(--drawer-swipe-movement-x)` or `-y`, because Base UI drops its inline drag styles on release and a `transform: none` underneath snaps the panel back for a frame; its `height` must be `var(--drawer-height, auto)`, because that variable is absent unless a nested drawer is open or the drawer is exiting. `Backdrop`'s opacity must read `--drawer-swipe-progress` or the scrim does not track the finger, and it needs a zero duration under `[data-swiping]`, an attribute Base UI applies imperatively and omits from its own table. `Viewport` is the gesture root and does the placement — `position: fixed`, `inset: 0`, `z.popup`, flex alignment to the edge where Dialog's grid-centers — and takes no `overflow`, because the gesture walks from the touched node up to it, so a scroller on the swipe axis hands the gesture away.

**`SwipeArea` is the part the second clause takes back, and not for the reason it looks like.** `touch-action` is the one thing Base UI does supply here, inline and unoverridable, permitting panning on the axis the gesture does not consume; the dismissal itself needs no `touch-action` anywhere, running on a non-passive capturing `touchmove` on `document`. What Base UI supplies is **no size and no position**, so an unstyled `SwipeArea` is a zero-area element and swipe-to-open does not exist. Ultima gives it the edge strip keyed to the direction, on the reason v0 gives for styling `Menu.SubmenuTrigger`: leaving it unstyled ships a public part that renders broken.

**`Indent` and `IndentBackground` are second-clause parts that Ultima still does not style**, and Styled parts carries the boundary this draws. Styling `Indent` means shipping an app shell, which Toast's contract already declined; it wraps the consumer's entire application, and the scale, translate, radius, and colour are all decisions about content Ultima does not own. Three things make that not merely unattractive: the app-root form is unsettled by Base UI's own source and has no demo, so Ultima would be inventing the untested half; `Indent` is transformed while active and so becomes a containing block for `position: fixed` descendants, silently pinning a consumer's sticky header or toast region to the scaled page for as long as a drawer is open; and five of the nine seeded variables are registered `inherits: false`, so a consumer rule one level below the seeded element reads the initial value and stays there. **The effect is still documented**, as a `##` section on Drawer's own page carrying the copyable app-shell CSS, which is the shape [#114](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/114) fixed for Sheet on Dialog's page. It takes no overlay number and no catalogue entry and is not a third recipe under Release scope, introducing no component and composing none.

**Drawer against Sheet is a capability test rather than taste, and the catalogue ships one answer to each of two questions.** If the panel needs a gesture it is Drawer; otherwise it is Sheet. Sheet is a Dialog recipe with no installable unit and costs a consumer no item, no subpath, and no install step. Drawer is an item because it carries a gesture layer Dialog cannot be given: swipe-to-dismiss, swipe-to-open, snap points, nested stacking, the virtual-keyboard layer, and **the Android back gesture, which decides it** — that runs on `CloseWatcher`, and `CloseWatcher` appears nowhere in Dialog in the whole package. A Sheet can be made to look identical and can never answer the Android back gesture. **Base UI states the same test in its own words**, as the single usage guideline on its Drawer page: "Drawer extends Dialog: It adds gesture support, snap points, and indent effects. If you don't need these, use Dialog instead. A panel that slides in from the edge of the screen and doesn't need gesture support is a positioned Dialog." Sheet is precisely that positioned Dialog. Two silent constraints belong beside that choice: **snap points are ignored entirely unless the dismiss direction is `up` or `down`**, so a left- or right-anchored drawer cannot use them, and **`Backdrop` does not render when the root is nested** unless `forceRender` is set.

**No axes, and the edge is an attribute rather than a prop.** `swipeDirection` on `Root` is Base UI's own, defaults to `down`, and is the **dismiss** direction rather than the placement: a drawer sits on the edge it dismisses toward. Base UI then emits `data-swipe-direction` on `Popup` and `SwipeArea` with that value always present and there is no `data-side`, so Ultima styles all four cases from a per-direction table on `Viewport`, `Popup`, and `SwipeArea`, and `SwipeArea` defaults to the opposite of the dismiss direction on its own. A table keyed on a pass-through prop is not an axis, which is the rule The navigation set fixed for Navigation Menu's `orientation`, "the way Separator's is not." **Three components now place an edge-anchored panel three ways, each for a reason**: Sidebar's mobile menu hard-codes one edge as `style` overrides in its own file, Sheet hands the consumer four physical edges to write because Dialog emits no attribute to key off, and Drawer styles all four because Base UI hands it the attribute. **RTL, recorded so nobody 'fixes' it later**: `swipeDirection`'s values are physical, so Drawer's edges are physical, like Sheet's and unlike Sidebar's mobile menu, whose `inline-start` produced the bug on [#118](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/118). Drawer cannot have that bug and equally cannot flip under RTL, because the consumer picks the direction; mapping it to logical directions would fight the axis the primitive resolves physically. The full contract is the **Drawer** note under Per-component notes.

**Context Menu** is the eleventh overlay, settled on [Context Menu against Dropdown Menu: restate the styled split, or compose it](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/109). It joins the recipe unvaried and reads `z.popup`. **Nineteen parts, and seventeen of them are the same component object as their `Menu` namesake**, verified against the published `@base-ui/react` 1.8.0 tarball rather than from memory: fifteen are re-exported straight from `menu/`, `Separator` is the shared top-level `separator/` component that `Menu` also re-exports, and `Positioner` is an alias, `export const ContextMenuPositioner = MenuPositioner`. Only `Root` and `Trigger` are components of their own. **There is no `Viewport` and no `Handle` or `createHandle`**, all three of which `Menu` has, so Context Menu's namespace is nineteen where Dropdown Menu's is twenty and the imperative-handle question Popover answered does not arise here at all.

**Its styled split is Dropdown Menu's, minus the `Viewport` it does not have**, which is "Alert Dialog's split is Dialog's" a second time. Styled: `Popup`, `Item`, `LinkItem`, `CheckboxItem`, `RadioItem`, `SubmenuTrigger`, `CheckboxItemIndicator`, `RadioItemIndicator`, `Separator`, `GroupLabel`, `Arrow`. Passed through: `Root`, `Trigger`, `Portal`, `Backdrop`, `Positioner` (`outline: 0`), `Group`, `RadioGroup`, `SubmenuRoot`. **`context-menu.tsx` restates that paint rather than importing `dropdown-menu`**, and the argument is under The registry item below: every behavior a context menu has comes from `@base-ui/react/context-menu`, which hands back the identical part objects unstyled, so an Ultima import would carry appearance alone. `Positioner`'s declaration is the one place the two namespaces genuinely diverge — it re-types the anchor props with context-menu defaults (`side: 'bottom'`, `align: 'start'`, `sideOffset` `-5` and `alignOffset` `2` for root menus, `arrowPadding` always `0`) and marks `positionMethod` deprecated and inert — so reusing Dropdown Menu's part would hand a consumer Menu's prop types and a dead prop presented as live.

**`ContextMenu.Trigger` is the one part that genuinely differs, and it passes through under the first clause of Styled parts rather than under the wrapper-trigger rule.** It is a `<div>` the consumer right-clicks, wrapping the consumer's own content, so its whole job is to define a hit area, which the first clause already calls grouping. The wrapper rule exists because `stylex.props` cannot merge foreign class strings when a Button is rendered into a wrapper, and nothing is rendered into this trigger, so **the wrapper-triggers-render-the-ring list does not grow and this part is never documented as `render={<Button />}`**. It is not the second clause either: it takes no props of its own, Base UI supplies its one declaration (`-webkit-touch-callout: none`), and the 500ms long press needs no `touch-action`, because a 10px move cancels the press and scrolling is the intended escape — Drawer's finding on [#112](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/112) reached again for a different gesture. **`user-select` and any visible affordance are the consumer's**, stated on the page: Base UI's own demos give the trigger a border, an aspect ratio, and `user-select: none`, and Ultima cannot, because forcing it over arbitrary wrapped content would remove text selection the consumer wants.

**`Root` is always modal and Base UI is what forces it**, unlike Alert Dialog where the `Omit` is Ultima's. `modal` is absent from `ContextMenuRootProps`, and `MenuStore`'s selector resolves it unconditionally `true` for a context-menu parent, so `ContextMenuRootProps` is `PartProps` over Base UI's own type with nothing removed. Modality makes `MenuPositioner` render an `InternalBackdrop`, inline-styled `position: fixed; inset: 0` with `role="presentation"` and `data-base-ui-inert`; it is Base UI's element, self-styled, not a part, and Ultima neither styles nor exposes it. The public `Backdrop` stays the unstyled click-catcher Overlays already describes. **`Arrow` stays styled and the canonical root demo renders none**: the root popup anchors to a zero-size rect at the cursor with `arrowPadding` forced to `0`, so an arrow there is meaningless, but a submenu's popup anchors to its `SubmenuTrigger` like any other and an unstyled `Arrow` would ship a public part that renders broken, which is v0's reason for styling `SubmenuTrigger`. The full contract is the **Context Menu** note under Per-component notes.

**Hover Card** is the twelfth overlay, settled on [Hover Card's name, its contract, and whether hover-only disclosure is acceptable](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/111). It joins the recipe unvaried and takes the full surface rather than Tooltip's narrowed one. **The item is `hover-card` while the primitive is `PreviewCard`**, which is Naming's component-name clause below rather than an exception to it. Eight parts — `Root`, `Trigger`, `Portal`, `Positioner`, `Popup`, `Arrow`, `Backdrop`, `Viewport` — plus `createHandle` and `Handle` alongside them, exposed unchanged as Popover's are; Base UI already surfaces them under those two names, so Ultima renames nothing there even though it renames the component, and `Handle` is a class rather than a component, so it is not a ninth part. **There is no `Title`, no `Description`, and no `Close`**, the last of which Popover has.

**What it does that Tooltip does not, and the mechanical answer comes first.** Tooltip's row requires `aria-label` on its trigger matching the tooltip text, because a tooltip's content *is* the trigger's accessible name; a hover card's content is a panel, and a panel cannot be an `aria-label`, so it fails Tooltip's own enforced constraint. Conceptually it completes Base UI's trigger test, which [#115](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/115) recorded as a popover when the trigger's purpose is to open the popup and a tooltip when the trigger's purpose is unrelated. **Hover Card is the third position: its trigger has a purpose of its own, it navigates, and the popup previews where it goes.** The popup's subject is the trigger's destination.

**Styled**: `Trigger`, `Popup`, `Arrow`, `Viewport`. **Passed through**: `Root`, `Portal`, `Positioner` (`outline: 0`), `Backdrop`. **`Trigger` is styled and is not a wrapper trigger**: `PreviewCard.Trigger` renders the `<a>` itself with `href` passed through, inline in the consumer's prose, which is `Select.Trigger`'s case rather than a slot for a caller's control, so the wrapper-triggers-render-the-ring list does not grow and the ring is Ultima's own. It is also **the first link Ultima ships inside prose**, and so the one part in the system that sets `text-decoration: underline`: every other link-bearing part sets `none` because each is chrome, where a hover ground or a button shape carries the linkness, and a paragraph supplies none. With it go `text-underline-offset` at space step 1, `--ult-radius-sm` so the ring fits an inline box, and the standard focus ring. It sets **no `color`**, so it inherits the surrounding prose rather than asserting `--ult-color-text` over a muted paragraph. `render` stays open for a router link, which carries no Ultima styles and does not collide.

**`Popup` needs no `outline: none`**, the one place it departs from Popover's and Dialog's: `PreviewCard` mounts no focus manager at all, using the portal variant without tabbable logic and no `FloatingFocusManager`, so although `Popup` carries `tabIndex: -1` nothing ever focuses it and the declaration would be dead. **`Viewport` carries `position: relative`, exactly Popover's**, being the same shared viewport mechanism, optional and only for an animated swap between several triggers. **`Backdrop` is the recipe's third backdrop case**, described under Overlays: Base UI hard-codes `pointer-events: none` on it, so it is not a click-catcher but an inert styling hook, and `Root` is modal in no sense. The full contract is the **Hover Card** note under Per-component notes.

**Menubar** is the one member of this set that is not an overlay, settled on [Menubar's shape, given Base UI ships it as a container rather than a namespace](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/110). **Ultima ships one function, `Menubar`, not a namespace**, and two rules already in this document decide that rather than a new judgment: Compound components says a single-part component exports one function, and Naming mirrors Base UI's part names only where the primitive supplies them. Verified against the 1.8.0 tarball, `menubar/index.d.ts` is two lines exporting `Menubar` and its types; there is no `index.parts` file, that convention does not exist in the package, and `MenubarContext` and `MenubarDataAttributes` are internal. So there is no part name to mirror and **`ToggleGroup.Item` gains no fourth sibling**. `separator.tsx` is the file shape: one Base UI component, a bare named export, and a narrowed `orientation` with a declared default and a per-orientation style table, which is a table without being an axis, exactly as Navigation Menu's `orientation` is not one.

**The namespace alternative is declined on cost as well as on the rule.** Re-exporting Dropdown Menu's parts under `Menubar.*` names is shadcn's Base UI backend, which imports thirteen names from its own `dropdown-menu` and declares it a `registryDependency` for appearance, which The registry item below forbids. Importing `@base-ui/react/menu` directly and restating the menu paint under Menubar's names avoids the dependency and is `toggle-group.tsx`'s shape, but it costs a third verbatim copy of `dropdown-menu.tsx`'s table and gives a consumer two spellings for one menu. ToggleGroup's reasoning does not transfer: it collected two Base UI exports because the two were useless apart while standalone Toggle was deferred, and a menu already ships as `dropdown-menu`. Of the six comparable systems that ship a menubar, the four with a real namespace each own menubar behavior in three or four genuinely distinct components and alias everything past that, and Base UI owns all of that behavior inside the container, so Ultima has no part with distinct behavior to wrap.

**Its one part is styled under both clauses of Styled parts, and the second is a measured fact.** It paints, which is #107's whole ground for it being an item rather than the recipe `Form` is denied. It is also clause 2, because `modal` defaults to `true` on `Menubar`, so an open menu renders an internal fixed backdrop whose hole is cut from the **bar's own box**: `MenuPositioner` sets `backdropCutout` to the Menubar element for a menubar parent, and `InternalBackdrop` builds its `clipPath` from `cutout.getBoundingClientRect()`, so the bar's CSS box decides where clicks still land while a menu is open. Being single-part it has no split to tabulate and no row in the Styled parts table, the way Skeleton, Spinner, and Badge have none.

**The paint**: `display: flex`, `align-items: center`, and `flex-direction` from the per-orientation table; `--ult-color-surface-raised`, a hairline `--ult-color-border`, `--ult-radius-lg`, and padding and `gap` of space step 1. **`inline-size: fit-content`**, which clause 2 licenses rather than taste, because a bare `<div>` is full-width and its cutout becomes a full-width strip where clicks neither dismiss nor reach anything; a consumer wanting full-bleed chrome sets an inline size through `style`. **No shadow**: Shadow above says shadows earn their keep on the overlays, and Menubar is stated under Overlays not to be one. The radius nests exactly, since the triggers are `Menu.Trigger render={<Button variant="ghost" size="sm" />}` at `--ult-radius-md`, and 10px plus space step 1 is 12px, leaving the bar 36px tall. Tabs' `segmented` List takes the same step 1 padding and gap but grounds itself in `--ult-color-surface-sunken` because it is an inset track; a menubar is raised chrome. Ultima sets none of `modal`, `disabled`, or `loopFocus`, the way it sets neither Popover's `modal` nor any `keepMounted`. The full contract is the **Menubar** note under Per-component notes.

### The Toggle, Accordion, Avatar, and Scroll Area set

Toggle, Accordion, Avatar, and Scroll Area, the four v0.2 components that share nothing but a release. Sectioned on [Where the four contracts are written, what the section is named, and where they land in catalogue order](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/146), the second answer to The navigation set's closing sentence that the rest of v0.2 sections itself when it is charted.

**It is the one set section named by listing its members, and that is the naming rule holding rather than bending.** The rule is The navigation set's: a section is named for what it holds rather than for a release. Twice it was satisfied by a shared property because twice there was one, a `<nav>` landmark and then the overlay recipe. These four are a pressed button, a disclosure, an image, and a scroll region. They join no recipe, render no landmark, portal nothing, and none of them composes or depends on another, so there is no property to name, and listing the members is what naming a section for what it holds comes to when the members hold nothing in common. Every property name that covered all four was false of at least one: "the display set" is false of Toggle and Accordion, "the disclosure set" is false of Avatar and Scroll Area, and a name loose enough to reach all four reaches most of The v0 set as well, which is "The v0.2 set" wearing a disguise and is closed as a name for the reason The navigation set gives. **The v0 set** and **The v0.1 set** are release-named legitimately, because each holds its release whole; this section holds three of v0.2's thirteen checklist lines.

**Three of the four take a contract an existing component already carries, and that is a kinship rather than a sectioning.** Toggle restates `ToggleGroup.Item`'s paint, Accordion's `Panel` takes Collapsible's four-rule contract whole, and `ScrollArea.Viewport` copies `Table.Scroll`'s focus-ring row. Seating each beside its kin was the alternative and is declined twice over: all three kin are in **The v0 set**, whose own sentence says v0 is eighteen components and the table below is the whole list, so a v0.2 row there makes the section false; and Avatar has no kin anywhere, so the split would place three and strand one. A section per component was the other alternative, and it gives a reader four places to look for one effort's output.

**The rows are in checklist-line order, and that order is the catalogue's.** `apps/docs/src/components.ts` appends the four after The overlay set's five, each carrying `release: 'v0.2'`, which widens the existing disclosure and adds no `RELEASE_LABELS` key. Across lines the catalogue follows the checklist and within a line it follows a reason the section states, which is how the previous eight landed; no line here needs one, because two of the three name a single component and the third keeps its own order. In `apps/docs/src/__tests__/components.test.ts` the specification-order array goes from forty to forty-four and `componentsInRelease('v0.2')` from eight to twelve. Three new Base UI subpaths, `accordion`, `avatar`, and `scroll-area`, so the cost is five hand edits each and four for Toggle, whose `@base-ui/react/toggle` is already in both vitest `optimizeDeps` lists: nineteen edits, and no new kind of edit.

| Component | Item | Built on | Parts | Axes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Accordion | `accordion` | Base UI `Accordion` | `Root`, `Item`, `Header`, `Trigger`, `Panel` | none |
| Avatar | `avatar` | Base UI `Avatar` | `Root`, `Image`, `Fallback` | none |
| Scroll Area | `scroll-area` | Base UI `ScrollArea` | `Root`, `Viewport`, `Content`, `Scrollbar`, `Thumb`, `Corner` | none |
| Toggle | `toggle` | Base UI `Toggle` | one part, the button itself | `variant`, `size` |

**Accordion** ships all five parts and no axes, settled on [Accordion's contract: its parts, its styled split, its heading level, and how far it copies Collapsible](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/143) over the findings in `docs/research/2026-09-17-accordion-after-apg-change.md`. Base UI's Accordion is Collapsible run once per item under a shared value array — `AccordionItem` calls the same `useCollapsibleRoot` and `AccordionPanel` the same `useCollapsiblePanel` — so the question this contract answers is not what Accordion does but how much of Collapsible's contract survives the change of arrangement.

**Styled**: `Item`, `Header`, `Trigger`, `Panel`. **Passed through**: `Root`, whose whole job is to group, which is the first clause of **Styled parts** unqualified.

**`Accordion.Trigger` is styled, and it is the one place this contract departs from Collapsible's.** `Collapsible.Trigger` ships no styles at all because the spec composes it as `render={<Sidebar.Button />}` or `render={<Button variant="ghost" />}`, and an Ultima style on the wrapper would collide with the Button's. An accordion trigger has no such caller: it is a full-measure, start-aligned row of the accordion with the disclosure glyph at its far end, and nothing in the catalogue produces that shape to hand in, since a Button is `inline-flex`, centred, radiused, and bordered. The APG closes the other door — the button is the only element permitted inside the heading — so there is no slot beside it either. It therefore joins `Select.Trigger`, `Combobox.Trigger`, and `HoverCard.Trigger` under the sentence in **Styled parts** that takes a part back from the wrapper rule because it *is* the control rather than a wrapper around one, and it renders its own focus ring. **Its docs page says not to pass a Button through `render`**, which is the obligation a styled trigger carries and the three before it carry too.

**The same fact decides which disclosure a nested Sidebar group composes, and the answer is unchanged: Collapsible.** That group is `Collapsible.Trigger render={<Sidebar.Button />}`, and an Accordion cannot be swapped into it — its trigger paints its own full-measure row and refuses a handed-in Button by the paragraph above — so an accordion inside a sidebar panel would put accordion rows and their hairlines inside sidebar chrome. **This is the one thing Accordion's arrival says about another component, and it is a boundary rather than a nesting note**: two components do the same job and the page says which one, the way the catalogue already separates Sheet from Drawer and Tooltip from Hover Card. How an accordion *looks* inside a container is the measure rule under **Styled parts** and gets no note, on either side. `sidebar.tsx`, Sidebar's row, and the **Sidebar** subsection under Per-component notes are unchanged by Accordion's arrival, which is the sentence Toggle Group's note already carries for Toggle.

**The paint.** `Item` is one `borderBlockEnd` hairline in `--ult-color-border` and nothing else, on every item including the last, which is `Table`'s cell rule exactly: a bordered list that closes with a rule, written without a structural pseudo-class. `Header` is a reset, `margin: 0`, because a UA heading margin between every row is the defect and the trigger supplies its own type. `Trigger` is the row: `inlineSize: 100%`, `display: flex` with `alignItems: center`, `justifyContent: space-between`, and space step 4 of gap so a caret lands at the end unaided; `textAlign: start`; the reset, `--ult-font-sans` at text step 4, `--ult-font-weight-medium`, `--ult-color-text`; space step 4 of block and inline padding; a transparent ground with `--ult-color-surface-hover` on `':hover'`; reduced opacity and `cursor: default` on `':is([data-disabled])'`; a `--ult-motion-fast` transition over `background-color, color`; and the focus ring. `Panel` is Collapsible's four rules over `--accordion-panel-height`.

**`Panel` carries no padding, and that is a correctness rule rather than a taste one.** An element whose height animates to `0` cannot clip its own padding under either box model — with `content-box` the padding sits outside the animated height, and with `border-box` the used height floors at padding plus border — so a padded panel stays padding-tall when closed. The content padding belongs to a wrapper inside the panel, which is what Base UI's own demos do, and the docs page prints that pattern. No sixth part is invented for it.

**The heading level is `render`, and v0 already answered this question.** `Card.Title` "renders `<h3>` by default, changeable through `render`", and `Accordion.Header` is the same shape: Base UI's is `useRenderElement('h3', …)` with no level prop, and `render={<h2 />}` produces an `<h2>` carrying every state attribute. Radix answers it the same way with `asChild`; React Aria's `level` prop is the outlier and Ultima adds no prop a Base UI part does not have. **No Ultima wrapper fuses `Header` into `Trigger`**, which is shadcn's shape in both its backends and the move that pins the level beyond a consumer's reach.

**No glyph slot, and the caret goes inside the trigger.** This is Collapsible's answer unchanged — the caret is the consumer's glyph, rotating on `data-panel-open` — and the APG is why it cannot be beside the button: the button is the heading's only permitted child. The trigger's `justify-content` is what makes the unaided placement correct. Only shadcn supplies the glyph, and it pays for that with the `Header`/`Trigger` fusion above. **Iconography** gains nothing.

**`orientation` and `loopFocus` are omitted from `AccordionRootProps`.** Base UI's own JSDoc marks both `@deprecated` — "This prop no longer affects keyboard focus behavior" — since v1.6.0 removed roving focus from the pattern, following w3c/aria-practices#3434. `AccordionRoot` destructures `loopFocus` to discard it and puts `orientation` into state alone. This is the rule [Toggle's contract](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/142) set for `value` applied a second time, and the two are now this set's pair of items whose type surface removes a prop the primitive still accepts.

**Style on `data-panel-open`, `data-open`, `data-starting-style`, `data-ending-style`, and `data-disabled`. Everything else the parts emit is noise, and three pieces of it are actively misleading.** Rendered against the installed 1.8.0: `data-orientation` appears on all five parts and nothing in Base UI reads it; `data-hidden` and `data-closed` appear on `Item` and `Header` as well as `Panel`; `data-index` appears on `Trigger`, which the shipped docs do not list; **`data-value` on every trigger holds the root's open-value array rather than that item's own value**, so a two-item accordion with the first open renders `data-value="a"` on both triggers; and **`aria-disabled` renders on every trigger including `"false"`**, so a selector on the bare attribute matches all of them. `data-disabled` is the one to use, and it appears on `Item`, `Header`, and `Trigger` together.

**A disabled trigger stays in the tab order.** `AccordionTrigger` calls `useButton` with `focusableWhenDisabled: true`, so it keeps `tabindex="0"` and takes `aria-disabled="true"` rather than the native `disabled` attribute, which is the opposite of a disabled Button. The accessibility row records it.

**`role="region"` on every panel is Base UI's, and it is the second knowing APG departure in the catalogue.** `AccordionPanel` sets `role="region"` and `aria-labelledby` unconditionally with no prop to refuse them, while the pattern says to avoid the role "in accordions that have more than approximately 6 panels". Ultima cannot honour that without `render`, so the docs page states that the six-panel line is the primitive's default rather than Ultima's choice. Pagination's distinct landmark names are the first such departure, under The navigation set; unlike that one this is not Ultima's call to make.

**Everything else passes through at Base UI's defaults.** `multiple`, `disabled`, `value` / `defaultValue` / `onValueChange`, and `Item`'s own `onOpenChange`; `hiddenUntilFound` and `keepMounted` on both `Root` and `Panel`, resolving panel-first then root then `false`, which Ultima sets at neither level exactly as Collapsible sets neither. The value is an array whether or not `multiple` is set, and with `multiple` false pressing the open item empties it; a consumer who needs one panel always open calls `eventDetails.cancel()`, the shape the spec already records for a single-selection Toggle Group. **An item with no `value` gets a generated id**, so an initially-open accordion needs explicit values, and the docs page says so.

**`--accordion-panel-width` is seeded and unread.** `useCollapsiblePanel` measures `scrollWidth` beside `scrollHeight` and the panel writes both as inline custom properties; nothing in Base UI reads the width back, and it exists for a consumer animating a panel that opens sideways. Ultima reads height only, which is Collapsible's decision inherited, and costs nothing because the variable is seeded either way.

**On the proof bar: item 7 is not this component.** There is no `onKeyDown` anywhere under `accordion/`, the trigger is a native button whose Enter and Space are the browser's, and Ultima wires no behaviour on top. **Item 8 transfers from Collapsible whole**, over `--accordion-panel-height`: computed `transition-duration` is not `0s`, `animation-name` is `none`, and `display` is not overridden while the panel is `[hidden]`. The three silent failures are the same three, because the same hook produces them — Base UI's animation-detection warning names Collapsible and Accordion in one string. **The `hiddenGuard` transfers with its implementation intact**, applied outside the `stylex.props` merge through a `className` concatenation, because StyleX drops every earlier rule for a property once a later style sets it plainly and a consumer laying panel content out with `display: grid` through the style slot would otherwise take the guard with it and leave a closed panel on screen.

**`accordion.tsx` restates Collapsible's panel rules rather than importing them**, for **Restatement**'s reason: the behaviour is `useCollapsiblePanel`'s, below Ultima, so an import would carry appearance alone and would put `collapsible` in `accordion`'s derived registry `dependencies`. It is not one of the identical-by-intent pairs, because the custom property differs; the four rules are the same rules over a differently named variable. `collapsible.tsx` is unedited.

**Registry item `accordion`**, one file, one new Base UI subpath, `registryDependencies` `@ultima/tokens` and `@ultima/lib`, no Ultima component dependency. Five hand edits, the fifth being the `optimizeDeps.include` entry both vitest configs lack today. Its catalogue description: "Disclosure sections under one shared value, on Base UI, with the heading level left to you."


**Avatar** ships all three parts, all of them styled, and no axes, settled on [Avatar's contract: its parts, its size and shape, and what an image without a name means](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/144) over the findings in `docs/research/2026-09-17-avatar-primitive-and-prior-art.md`. It is the one component in this set with no kin anywhere in the catalogue, and the one whose primitive writes no ARIA at all in its default mode: `Root` maps `imageLoadingStatus` to `() => null`, so no part emits a status attribute, and the only attributes Base UI ever writes are on `Image`.

**Styled**: `Root`, `Image`, `Fallback`. **Passed through**: none, and Avatar is the first entry in this set whose second column is empty. `Root` looks like the grouping case the first clause of **Styled parts** sends to pass-through and is not one: it is the visible circle, carrying the ground, the radius, and the `overflow: hidden` that clips the image to it, which is paint under the first clause read plainly. Base UI's own demo puts every one of those declarations on it and calls them the consumer's.

**No axes, and the two it was offered are each a single unconditional declaration.** Every styled system in the prior art carries a `size`, and Chakra and Mantine carry a shape axis besides; Ultima takes neither, and the reason is one rule rather than two refusals.

**`size` is declined on the definition, and Skeleton already answered the question it leaves.** **Variants, sizes, and tones** defines `size` as the three control heights, and an avatar is not a control — the same sentence that refused `size` on Switch, Checkbox, Radio Group, and Field. Nothing in the prior art argues the other way on Ultima's terms: shadcn's three steps are 24, 32, and 40, Chakra runs eight named steps, and React Spectrum runs fourteen numeric ones, so "every styled system has a `size`" is a precedent for the name and for nothing under it. An avatar's real range runs from an inline mark in a table row to a profile header, which is wider than three control heights and not aligned to them. So the box is the `style` slot, which is **Skeleton**'s answer unchanged and the second component to give it: `Root` ships `inlineSize` and `blockSize` at space step 9 (32px), Base UI's demo and shadcn's default, and a caller who wants another size writes one.

**Shape is the style slot for the same reason, and the axis test is now stated rather than felt.** Chakra's `shape` and Mantine's `radius` are a fourth axis, which Ultima does not have, so a circle-or-square choice could only arrive as a `variant` of "shape and emphasis". It does not arrive at all: **an axis earns its place when the look it carries is more than one declaration, or carries conditions a caller overriding through `style` would have to restate.** That is the general form of the reason [Toggle's contract](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/142) gave for its own `variant` — StyleX merges a property one condition at a time, so replacing a shipped look means replacing its hover and pressed halves too — and it runs the other way here, because Avatar's `border-radius` and box carry no conditions at all. `style={{ borderRadius: … }}` wins the property outright and cleanly. Circle is the shipped default at `--ult-radius-full`, which is every system's default and four of the seven systems' only option.

**The paint.** `Root` is `position: relative`, `display: inline-flex` with `align-items: center` and `justify-content: center`, `flex-shrink: 0`, `inlineSize` and `blockSize` at space step 9, `overflow: hidden`, `--ult-radius-full`, a `--ult-color-surface-sunken` ground so an avatar with no image still reads as a filled mark, and `user-select: none`. It carries no border, and the ring separating overlapping avatars stays out of it, settled on [Whether an avatar group ships as a recipe, a docs composition, or nothing](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/165): the group is a documented composition on this page and its ring arrives through the `style` slot. `Image` is `position: absolute`, `inset: 0`, 100% of both axes, and `object-fit: cover`. `Fallback` is `position: absolute`, `inset: 0`, a centred flex box, `--ult-font-weight-medium`, `--ult-font-leading-none`, and `--ult-color-text-muted`.

**The initials track the box rather than a text step, and that is the system's first container query.** With no `size` axis the box is the caller's, and a `Fallback` pinned to text step 3 is right at 32px and wrong at 96px, which would make the resize the docs page recommends into two things to remember rather than one. So `Root` takes `container-type: inline-size` and `Fallback` takes `font-size: 40cqi`, about shadcn's own ratio of `text-xs` inside `size-8`, and the initials scale with whatever box the caller sets with nothing to coordinate. It is **Iconography**'s `1em` rule one step further out — a glyph tracks the text step of the part holding it, and these initials track the box of the part holding them — and it is stated in that general form so the next part whose type must follow a caller's box can cite it. No `@container` at-rule is involved: `cqi` is a unit in a value, so StyleX passes it through the way it passes `var()`.

**`Fallback` is a layout slot the caller fills, and Ultima ships no default inside it.** The prior art splits two ways: Base UI, Radix, and shadcn take initials as children, while Chakra and Mantine take a `name` prop and derive them, falling back to a generic person icon. Ultima takes the first, and **Iconography** does not grow. Three reasons, and each is sufficient. A slot that holds initials, a glyph, or anything else the caller supplies is `Alert.Icon` and `Empty.Icon`'s layout slot rather than a glyph slot, and that rule says Ultima ships no default there. A `name` prop would be a prop the Base UI part does not have, which [Accordion's contract](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/143) declined one contract earlier over `level`. And deriving initials is not a solved problem to inherit: Chakra takes the first letter of the first and last word, Mantine the first two letters, they disagree on every three-word name, and both are wrong for a mononym and for scripts where a letter is not the unit. A caller who wants a person icon renders one into the slot, and the docs page shows that beside the initials.

**The accessible name is `alt` on `Image`, documented and not type-enforced, and axe is why.** The enforcement rule under the **Accessibility contract** turns on whether an automated check would catch a missing name, which is what earned Menubar the third enforced attribute and what left Toggle documented one contract ago. It leaves Avatar documented for Toggle's reason and more plainly: axe-core 4.13.0 ships `image-alt` in the default rule set the sweep runs, its `has-alt` check returns true when the attribute merely exists, so a missing `alt` is a violation and `alt=""` is a pass. Both halves matter. The violation catches the case WAI warns about, an `<img>` with no `alt` whose filename some screen readers read aloud. The pass is what lets the attribute mean "decorative", which is the common case, since an avatar usually sits beside the name it depicts. **Three type-enforced attributes stayed the total when this was written**, and a fourth would be the first on something that is neither an interactive control nor a landmark — the shape the fourth kept, since [#279](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/279) put it on `Resizable.Handle`, a control.

**Ultima sets no default `alt`, and that is the opposite of React Spectrum's answer on purpose.** Spectrum documents avatars as decorative with an empty `alt` by default, and a default here would silently satisfy `image-alt` on a standalone avatar that genuinely needs a name, turning a failure the sweep catches into one it cannot. Breadcrumb's and Pagination's conditional landmark defaults are not a precedent against this: those supply a name that is actually correct, and nothing can tell Ultima whether a given avatar is decorative. So `alt` passes through as the native `<img>` prop it is, and the docs page states the two cases — `alt=""` where a visible name sits beside the avatar, and `alt` naming the person where the avatar stands alone.

**`Root` is never named, and an avatar beside a visible name is decorative whole.** `Root` is a `<span>` with no role, Ultima adds none, and there is nothing for a name to attach to. That leaves one composition worth stating: in a list row or a comment header the fallback's initials would be read out beside the name they abbreviate, so the decorative case puts `aria-hidden` on `Root` and the whole avatar leaves the tree in both states at once, which is the only place to put it, since hiding `Image` alone would expose the initials whenever the image failed. The `aria-hidden` Base UI writes on a `keepMounted` image until it loads is the primitive's and Ultima neither adds to it nor removes it.

**`Fallback.delay` and `Image.keepMounted` pass through at Base UI's defaults, `0` and `false`, and Ultima sets neither.** `delay` is a first-render gate so a fast image never flashes initials, and how long a consumer's connections take is theirs. `keepMounted` is the bigger one: it mounts the `<img>` immediately and reads the status off that element instead of preloading a detached copy, and it is the only mode in which `next/image` and `loading="lazy"` work at all. On the default path `render={<NextImage />}` leaves `src` on the rendered element, the preloader sees none, and `useImageLoadingStatus` sets `error` synchronously so the image never mounts; and a `loading="lazy"` attribute never defers anything, because the detached preload fetched the URL eagerly before the attribute reached the DOM. Which of those a consumer wants is a consumer's decision, the same shape as **Overlays**' rule that Ultima never sets `keepMounted` on a popup.

**Ultima ships the stacking CSS unconditionally, so flipping `keepMounted` cannot break the component.** Base UI's own doc makes the consumer stack the parts under that prop: `Fallback` before `Image` in DOM order, both `position: absolute; inset: 0`, and the not-yet-loaded image hidden. A component that shipped default-mode CSS and printed the rest on its page would hand a consumer a broken avatar the moment they reached for the prop the prop exists for. The absolute positioning above is that CSS, and it costs the default mode nothing, because there the `<img>` is simply not in the DOM until it has loaded and the `data-loading` and `data-error` selectors never match.

**The hide is `visibility`, never `display`, and that is the second clause of Styled parts.** `Image` takes `visibility: hidden` on `':is([data-loading], [data-error])'` and sets no `display` anywhere. `display: none` is the obvious way to hide it and it is the wrong one: an element with no box never intersects the viewport, so a lazy image inside a `display: none` wrapper is never loaded at all, and `keepMounted`'s whole reason for existing dies silently. That is the primitive's behaviour depending on CSS the primitive does not supply, so **it is a second-clause part, and the sentence below the Styled parts table saying none of the four is one is corrected rather than merely qualified.** What it is not is `NavigationMenu.List`'s shape, where the clause rescues a part the first clause would have passed through; `Image` paints under the first clause regardless, and the clause constrains *how* it is styled. That is `Collapsible.Panel`'s `hiddenGuard` exactly, which is why no row moves into the Styled parts table and the split stays in this section with the other three.

**On the proof bar: item 7 is not this component, and item 8 is the hide.** Ultima wires no behaviour: `onLoadingStatusChange` is the primitive's and passes through, and there is no handler, focus move, or state of Ultima's own anywhere in the file. Item 8 asserts what a screenshot cannot see, which is the whole reason the item exists: under `keepMounted` with the image not yet loaded, `Image`'s computed `display` is not `none` and its computed `visibility` is `hidden`. Item 1 is one combination, since there are no axes, so it is the no-props default alone. Item 2 is the deliberate absence — `Root` carries no role and no name, and the accessible text is `alt` when the image is loaded and the fallback's own text when it is not. Item 5 is `data-loading` and `data-error` driving that hide, and they are the only two attributes in the component that drive anything.

**An avatar group is a documented composition on this page, and it is neither an item nor a recipe.** Settled on [Whether an avatar group ships as a recipe, a docs composition, or nothing, now that Avatar has no size to push down](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/165). Every comparable system ships one as a sibling container, and three of the four push a `size` onto their children, which is the half of the job Ultima does not have, since it has no `size` axis to push. What is left is negative inline spacing, a ring, and an overflow count, and all three are `style` on parts this item already ships.

**It passes the component identity test and is still not a recipe, which is the gap this entry closes.** Every part a group needs is `Avatar.Root`, `Avatar.Image`, and `Avatar.Fallback`, one item's components, so the test under **Release scope and core coverage** reads yes, and [#141](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/141)'s second clause is untouched because a group varies no prop's liveness. What it has no answer to is which entry it satisfies. **Recipe** opens with "a release checklist entry satisfied by a documented composition": Sheet satisfies line 549, Data Table and React Hook Form each satisfy their own, and line 551 is "Avatar and Scroll Area" with no group in it. So **the identity test says what shape a composition has and the checklist says whether it is an entry at all, and a composition that passes the test with no entry to satisfy is a documented composition on the page of the component it composes.** That second requirement was never stated because every recipe so far arrived attached to an entry. Nothing is the other alternative and is declined: the ring is a real decision, and **The line between a component and page layout** exists to stop a surface being invented from raw tokens where nobody wrote one down.

**The ring is the page ground in the shape of a ring, so the one-border-width rule does not govern it.** It carries no palette step and separates two avatars by standing in for what is behind them, which is why 2px is every system's answer and not a second border width any more than `border.focus` is. It is `boxShadow: '0 0 0 2px var(--ult-color-surface)'` through the `style` slot, and the page says a group on a raised surface swaps the token for that surface's. It is not a `border`: `Root` is a fixed box with `overflow: hidden`, so a border would eat into it and shrink the image inside rather than sit outside the circle.

**The overlap is arrangement, and the stacking order needs nothing.** Every child but the first takes `marginInlineStart: 'calc(var(--ult-space-4) * -1)'`, 8px against the 32px default box, which is shadcn's own ratio and rescales with the box the caller sets. All three parts are already positioned, so paint order is DOM order and no `z-index` is written.

**The overflow count is a plain `Avatar.Root` with `+3` in its `Fallback`, and it costs no new part.** It is what Chakra and Mantine tell callers to do, the contract above already supports it with no `Image`, and `40cqi` makes the count track the box for free, so a resized group needs nothing coordinated.

**The group has no role and no name, which is `Root` is never named held rather than excepted.** React Spectrum gives its group a `label` because it gives the group a role for one to attach to; an Ultima group is an arrangement of `<span>`s with no role, and a name has nowhere to land. A group meaning "and 3 others" puts that phrase in the surrounding text, because `+3` on its own is not the sentence, which is the same move the decorative case already makes when it puts `aria-hidden` on `Root` and lets the visible name beside it do the work.

**Three demos, and no live demonstration.** The docs site has no avatar-like surface and owes none: **Release scope and core coverage** already says the site using a component is not one of the applicable checks, and a live demonstration is not part of the release gate. What is forced is the page and the demos, because `apps/docs/src/__tests__/components.test.ts` asserts every catalogue entry has an MDX page and at least one demo module. The canonical demo is an image with an initials fallback at the default circle; a second gives a broken `src` so the fallback shows, which is also how the axe sweep sees the initials and the `alt=""` case; a third is `keepMounted` with a lazy image, the mode that exists for `next/image` and the one whose CSS item 8 asserts. The page prints the square avatar, the resized avatar, and the group as `style` overrides rather than as demos of their own, because none of the three is a state the sweep needs to see: a group introduces no attribute, no handler, and no state the three demos do not already cover, so it earns no swept surface and the count stays three.

**Registry item `avatar`**, one file, one new Base UI subpath, `dependencies` `@base-ui/react` and `@stylexjs/stylex`, `registryDependencies` `@ultima/tokens` and `@ultima/lib`, no Ultima component dependency. Five hand edits, the fifth being the `optimizeDeps.include` entry both vitest configs lack today. Its catalogue description: "An image that falls back to whatever you put behind it, at whatever size you set."


**Scroll Area** ships all six parts, three of them styled, and no axes, settled on [Scroll Area's contract: its six parts, its scrollbar visibility, and the focus ring it copies from Table.Scroll](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/145) over the findings in `docs/research/2026-09-17-scroll-area-visibility-and-overflow.md` and `docs/research/2026-09-09-base-ui-docs-patterns.md`. **It is the component where Base UI writes the most inline style of any primitive in the catalogue, and that is what decides most of this contract**: an inline style beats a StyleX class outright, so every declaration Base UI already writes is one Ultima must not write, and the parts that look like they need Ultima's help mostly do not.

**Styled**: `Viewport`, `Scrollbar`, `Thumb`. **Passed through**: `Root`, `Content`, `Corner`.
