**`Root` passes through, and the bounded box is the consumer's.** Its job is to group, to hold context, and to render the scrollbar-hiding `<style>`, and Base UI sets its `position: relative` and the two corner variables inline, so the first clause of **Styled parts** sends it through unqualified. What it does not get is a size, and neither does Ultima give it one: a scroll area's height is the whole of what makes it scroll and there is no default worth guessing, so the bound goes on `Root` through the `style` slot. That is Base UI's own demo split — `width` and `height` on `Root`, `height: 100%` on `Viewport` — and it makes Scroll Area the third component whose box is the style slot, after **Skeleton** and [Avatar](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/144). **Its docs page says an unbounded Root never scrolls**, because that failure is silent and looks like a component that does not work.

**`Content` passes through, and this departs from the research's draft verdict knowingly.** The 09-09 note read it as "sets spacing, so styled", which is true of where the scrollable content's padding goes and not of who writes it. What goes inside a scroll area is unknowable, any padding Ultima picked would be wrong for someone, and it is one unconditional declaration the `style` slot reaches cleanly, which is the axis test from Avatar's contract applied to a part rather than to a prop. Base UI already sets `min-width: fit-content` inline, which is the part's whole functional contribution. This is **Accordion**'s `Panel`-takes-no-padding call for a different reason.

**`Corner` passes through, and that follows from the visibility decision rather than from the rule.** Base UI sizes and positions it inline from the measured scrollbars — `nextCornerWidth` and `nextCornerHeight` stay `0` whenever either scrollbar is hidden — so an unstyled Corner is a correctly sized invisible box that keeps the two bars from overlapping. It would need styles only to match a painted track, and the track below is transparent, so there is nothing to match. A contract that chose a filled track would style it; this one does not.

**`Viewport` is styled twice over, and the second is the one that matters.** Under the first clause it carries the focus ring. Under the second clause it carries `block-size: 100%` and `box-sizing: border-box`, and without them the viewport sizes to its own content, nothing ever overflows, `hiddenState` stays `{ x: true, y: true }`, `Scrollbar` and `Corner` both `return null`, and `tabIndex` stays `-1`. **The component degrades into a plain `<div>` and every one of those failures is invisible in a screenshot of content that happens to be short.** That is `NavigationMenu.Viewport`'s situation reached a second time, by a different route, and it is this contract's proof-bar item 8.

**The ring is `Table.Scroll`'s row exactly, minus its `overflow: auto`.** `outline: <border.focus> solid var(--ult-color-border-focus)` with `outlineOffset: var(--border-focus-offset)` on `':focus-visible'`, which is the declaration `table.tsx` already carries. The `overflow` half is not copied and the reason is deadness rather than taste: Base UI sets `overflow: scroll` inline on `Viewport`, an inline style beats a StyleX class, so a copied `overflow: auto` would compile, ship, and never apply. **`:focus-visible` rather than `:focus` is load-bearing here in a way it is not on `Table.Scroll`**: Base UI's explicit `tabIndex` makes the viewport click-focusable, which neither Chromium nor Gecko does natively and which Gecko's source calls "unnecessarily ugly", so a `:focus` ring would paint every time a reader clicked the text inside a scroll area. **The copy runs in both directions and neither direction is a dependency.** `Table.Scroll` takes `Viewport`'s tab-order rule back in return, settled on [Whether Table.Scroll stays its own part now that Scroll Area exists, or the two converge](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/166), which also declines converging the two parts: `Table.Scroll` is a `role="region"` named from its caption and `Viewport` is `role="presentation"`, so the two regions stay the two halves [#147](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/147) split them into, captioned and uncaptioned, and `table` declares nothing.

**Three things Ultima must not write, and all three are dead rather than broken.** `overflow` on `Viewport`, above. `height` or `width` on `Thumb`, because Base UI writes `height: var(--scroll-area-thumb-height)` inline for a vertical thumb and `width: var(--scroll-area-thumb-width)` for a horizontal one, which is why **`Thumb` is not a second-clause part despite reading a variable the primitive seeds**: the primitive reads it for you. And the `Scrollbar`'s placement, which Base UI writes as `position: absolute`, `touch-action: none`, `user-select: none`, and the edge insets. Verified against the installed `@base-ui/react` 1.8.0 rather than read off the docs page.

**What Base UI leaves out is the one thing `Scrollbar` needs: a cross-axis size.** The inline style sets the bar's extent along the scroll axis and nothing across it, so an unstyled `Scrollbar` is zero pixels wide, the `Thumb` inside it is invisible, and the component appears to have no scrollbar at all. That is the first clause in its strongest form — the part does not exist visually without Ultima — and it is not the second clause, because the failure is entirely cosmetic: `thumbSize` is computed from the bar's length rather than its thickness, so the measurement, the attributes, and the drag all keep working behind a bar nobody can see.

**The paint.** `Scrollbar` is space step 5 (12px) across, with space step 1 of padding, `display: flex`, and `justify-content: center`; the track is transparent. Its thickness is keyed on `':is([data-orientation="vertical"])'` and `':is([data-orientation="horizontal"])'` in one flat table, which is `separator.tsx`'s and `menubar.tsx`'s shape for a pass-through `orientation` and is **not** an axis. `Thumb` is `--ult-radius-full`, `--ult-color-surface-active` at rest, the matching cross-axis 100%, and a `background-color` transition over `--ult-motion-fast`. `Viewport` is the two structural declarations and the ring.

**One visibility mode, no `type` prop, and no `scrollHideDelay`.** The bar is visible whenever its axis overflows, and it emphasizes on `':is([data-hovering], [data-scrolling])'` by taking the thumb to `--ult-color-border-strong`. **It is a colour change and never an opacity gate**, which is the whole decision. Radix ships four modes and defaults to `hover` at 600ms, shadcn forwards that unchanged, and Base UI's own demo is a fifth mode Radix has no name for, hover-or-scrolling at `opacity: 0` with `pointer-events: none`. Ultima declines all of them on two facts rather than on taste. **`data-hovering` is never set for a touch pointer**, since Base UI excludes `pointerType === 'touch'` outright, so every hover-gated mode hides the scrollbar from every touch user at rest and shows it only during the 500ms after a scroll they have already managed without it. And **a bar at `opacity: 0` with `pointer-events: none` cannot be grabbed**, so those modes make the thumb — the component's only pointer affordance, and the reason the custom scrollbar exists at all — conditionally unusable, which is a discoverability failure dressed as restraint. Base UI's built-in behaviour is already Radix's `auto`, so this mode costs no Base UI prop and no hide CSS; the five lines that produce the fade are printed on the docs page as a `style` override for a consumer who wants it.

**A visibility axis would pass the axis test and is declined by the three-axis rule, which is the distinction between them.** [Avatar's contract](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/144) says an axis earns its place when the look it carries is more than one declaration or carries conditions a caller would have to restate, and a visibility mode is exactly that, so the test does not decide this one. **Variants, sizes, and tones** does: the axis would have to be `variant`, which is shape and emphasis, and when a scrollbar appears is neither. The test says when an axis is permissible; the three-axis rule says which axes exist.

**Everything else passes through at Base UI's defaults, and two of them are traps worth writing down.** `overflowEdgeThreshold` on `Root` (default `0`), `keepMounted` on `Scrollbar` (default `false`), `orientation` on `Scrollbar` (default `'vertical'`, so the canonical tree renders one bar and a horizontal one is the consumer's second `Scrollbar`), and the nonce through the consumer's own `CSPProvider`. Ultima sets none of them. **`overflowEdgeThreshold` gates only the four `data-overflow-*` attributes and has no effect on the gradient-fade recipe**, which reads the four `--scroll-area-overflow-*` variables, and those are written unthresholded on every measurement; a builder who wires the prop to the fade gets nothing, and the default `0` means "more than 1px from the edge" because the distance is snapped inside a `SCROLL_EDGE_TOLERANCE_PX = 1` window first. **`keepMounted` does not reproduce Radix's `always`**: on a non-overflowing axis `computeThumbPosition` clamps the thumb to `MIN_THUMB_SIZE = 16` px instead of filling the track, so the look needs `[data-has-overflow-y]` gating on the Thumb, which is the default mode again.

**The fade recipe carries a hazard the Base UI page does not state, and the docs page states it.** `ScrollArea.Viewport` calls `CSS.registerProperty` once per page for the four overflow variables with `inherits: false`, skipped on WebKit, and **that registration is page-global and permanent**: after the first scroll area mounts in Blink or Gecko, any element anywhere on the page reading those four names gets `0px` rather than a `var()` fallback, for the rest of the page's life. A descendant of `Viewport` that wants the live value writes `--scroll-area-overflow-*: inherit` on itself *and on every element between it and the viewport*. It is harmless in WebKit, so one stylesheet serves both, and it reaches only a consumer writing the fade, which is why it sits on the component page rather than being hoisted beside the CSP note.

**The CSP note is a one-line pointer, not a repetition.** **Registry and install** under **What the consumer still does by hand** already carries it and already records that `ScrollArea.Root` is the injecting part rather than `ScrollArea.Viewport` as Base UI's own page says. The page points at it in one line, which is Select's pattern for the same style tag and Slider's for its script, and this is the third occurrence.

**On the proof bar: item 8 is `Viewport`'s `block-size`, and it is the first item-8 assertion in the system that reads layout rather than a computed declaration.** In a `Root` bounded shorter than its content, the viewport's `scrollHeight` exceeds its `clientHeight` and its `tabindex` is `0` — which is the whole failure chain above caught at its head, and which the environment can actually run, since **Environment** is Vitest in browser mode against Playwright's Chromium with real layout. **Item 7 is not this component**: scrolling once focused is native, the thumb drag is the primitive's, and Ultima wires nothing on top. Item 1 is one combination, there being no axes. Item 2 is the deliberate absence, the viewport carrying no role beyond `presentation` and no name, with `Scrollbar` and `Corner` `aria-hidden`. Item 3 is the ring on `Viewport` after keyboard focus. Item 4 is the `Scrollbar` unmounting when its axis does not overflow. Item 5 is `data-hovering` and `data-scrolling` moving the thumb's colour, asserted as differing from the resting value rather than as a literal, under the standing no-colour rule.

**Three demos, two site retrofits, and no live demonstration.** The canonical demo is a bounded `Root` with a single vertical `Scrollbar`; a second overflows both axes and renders both bars and the `Corner`; a third is the gradient fade as a `style` override on `Viewport`, which is where the `inherit` hazard and the `var(…, 40px)` SSR fallback get printed. **The build effort also owes the docs site two adoptions and one deletion**, settled on [Which of the docs site's three hand-built scroll regions Scroll Area replaces, and whether that is its live demonstration](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/147) and written out under **The inventory, sorted**: the uncaptioned MDX table wrapper in `prose.tsx` and the six palette ramp grids in `routes/palette.tsx` become Scroll Areas, the shell content column in `routes/root.tsx` stays page layout and drops its `scrollbarWidth: 'none'`, and `apps/docs/src/__tests__/surfaces.test.ts` grows `scrollbarWidth` so the third prong of **The line between a component and page layout** has a gate behind it. **Neither adoption is a live demonstration**: both sit on content pages rather than in the site's chrome, so the four stay four and this page points at none.

**Registry item `scroll-area`**, one file, one new Base UI subpath, `dependencies` `@base-ui/react` and `@stylexjs/stylex`, `registryDependencies` `@ultima/tokens` and `@ultima/lib`, no Ultima component dependency and specifically not `table`. Five hand edits, the fifth being the `optimizeDeps.include` entry both vitest configs lack today. Its catalogue description: "A native scroll container with scrollbars you can see and a viewport a keyboard can reach."


**Toggle** is a single-part item, settled on [Toggle's contract: one part or a namespace, its axes, and what names it](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/142) over the item-versus-recipe finding on [#141](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/141). Its row sits last in the table and in the catalogue, which is checklist-line order; the three rows above it are still to be written.

**One function, not a namespace.** `@base-ui/react/toggle` exports one component and no parts, so **Compound components** decides it the way it decided Menubar on [#110](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/110): a `Toggle.Root` would be an invented name for a part the primitive does not have, and **Naming**'s mirroring rule reaches the case by supplying nothing to mirror. `ToggleGroup.Item` stays v0's one part name that mirrors nothing, and Toggle adds no second.

**Two axes, and they layer rather than compound.** `variant` is `outline` and `ghost`, default `outline`; `size` is `sm`, `md`, and `lg`, default `md`, at Button's three control heights. There is no `tone`: a toggle is neither a measurement nor a destructive action, which is why Switch and Toggle Group have none either, and the one colour it needs is the pressed state, which has a single meaning. `variant` sets border and colour and `size` sets type and box, so they never set the same property and the file carries two flat tables rather than Button's nested `variants[variant][tone]`. **`size` is the axis Toggle Group does not have and cannot borrow**: inside a group the ground sets the scale and the segmented control is one visual object, while a standalone toggle stands beside a Button and has to line up with it. `toggle-group.tsx` is unchanged by any of this.

**`variant` exists because the resting affordance is the real choice, and it is the one thing `style` cannot cleanly reach.** A toggle alone on a surface needs a border to read as a control; a toggle in a toolbar of them does not. A consumer overriding a single shipped look through `style` would have to restate the hover and pressed conditions as well, because StyleX merges a property one condition at a time — the rule the Sheet recipe prints for `transform` — so shipping one look and pointing at the escape hatch costs the consumer more than the axis costs the file. There is no `solid`: a toggle whose resting state is a solid fill reads as already pressed.

**The pressed state is the accent role, and that is where it departs from Toggle Group knowingly.** Pressed takes `--ult-color-accent-subtle`, `--ult-color-accent-border`, and `--ult-color-accent-text`, which is Badge's `subtle` column worn by one state rather than by a `tone` prop. Toggle Group's item answers this with neutral `--ult-color-surface-raised` over its sunken ground, and that answer does not transfer: in a group, pressed is legible because two siblings differ, while a standalone toggle has nothing to compare against and the neutral answer alone is mithril2 on mithril1, one palette step carrying a binary state. This is the resting-surface difference **Per-component notes** already sanctioned for these two tables followed to its end, and it is the Pagination-from-Button case rather than the Alert-Dialog-from-Dialog one: the two arrangements are meant to differ.

**Everything that is not the pressed state is Button's, restated.** The base table is Button's `styles.root` — the reset, `--ult-radius-md`, the hairline border, `--ult-font-weight-medium`, space step 4 of gap, `--ult-font-leading-none`, the disabled opacity and cursor, the `--ult-motion-fast` transition over `background-color, border-color, color`, and the focus ring — and `sizes` is Button's three cells unchanged, because a toggle beside a button that does not match its height is the defect. `variants` is Button's `outline` and `ghost` accent columns for the resting and hover halves, so a resting toggle and a resting button hover identically. **This is the third restatement meant to be identical rather than merely similar**, after `dialog.tsx`/`alert-dialog.tsx` and `context-menu.tsx`/`dropdown-menu.tsx`, and no test asserts it, for the reason **The registry item** gives: a check on the newest pair that the older two do not carry would be decided by which contract noticed. Whether one mechanism should cover all three is a system-wide question rather than this contract's.

**The pressed-hover nesting carries over unchanged, and it is a system rule now rather than one file's comment.** Pressed sits nested inside its own condition with its hover repeated, never as a sibling of `':hover'`; **State styling** below states the general rule. It matters more here than in a group: the pressed fill is the only thing saying the control is on, so a hover that repaints it as resting is the state disappearing under the pointer rather than a selection appearing to move.

**Its accessible name is its text, and nothing is type-enforced.** Base UI renders a `<button>` carrying `aria-pressed`, so a text toggle names itself and an icon-only one passes `aria-label`, which is Button's row exactly. A type cannot see whether children are text or a glyph, so requiring the attribute would force a meaningless name onto every text toggle, which is the reason **The navigation set** gives for leaving `NavigationMenu.Root` documented rather than enforced. The difference from Menubar, whose name *is* enforced, is that Menubar earned it because no automated check would catch a missing one, and axe's `button-name` catches this one in the demo sweep. **Icon-only Toggle is Icon-only Button's documented rule**, unchanged: `aria-label`, the consumer's glyph as the only child, and a `style` override setting `paddingInline` to the block padding so the control is square at its size. Ultima ships no glyph for it, so **Iconography** is untouched.

**`value` is omitted and the `<Value extends string>` generic is dropped**, which is [#141](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/141)'s finding written into the type surface rather than merely asserted. Measured against the installed `@base-ui/react` 1.8.0: standalone, `value` is passed to `useBaseUiId(valueProp || undefined)` and read only inside `groupContext?.setGroupValue`, so it identifies a toggle to a group that is not there. `ToggleProps` is therefore not generic, and `pressed`, `defaultPressed`, `onPressedChange`, `disabled`, `nativeButton`, `render`, and the native button props pass through as Base UI's. `ToggleGroup.Item` keeps the generic and the prop, and the two are not interchangeable at the type level, which is the honest form of the finding that they are one component wearing two prop surfaces. **The controlled prop differs the same way in the other direction**: `Toggle` reads `pressed` only when there is no group context, so the standalone type surface has the live one and the group's item has the inert one.

**Registry item `toggle`**, one file, one Base UI subpath, `dependencies` `@base-ui/react` and `@stylexjs/stylex`, `registryDependencies` `@ultima/tokens` and `@ultima/lib`, and no Ultima component dependency in either direction: `toggle-group.tsx` is not edited and is not imported. Four hand edits rather than five, because `@base-ui/react/toggle` is already in both vitest `optimizeDeps` lists.

**On the proof bar: item 7 is not Toggle and item 8 is not Toggle.** Every interaction is the primitive's, which is item 4, and the one part is styled under the first clause of **Styled parts** because it paints. Item 1 is six combinations, two variants by three sizes, with the no-props default `outline` at `md`. **Item 5 is where the nesting above is proved, and Toggle is the first test to do it**: assert that the pressed control's computed background equals its own hovered value and differs from a resting one's, which names no colour and so survives every palette regeneration under the standing rule. `toggle-group.test.tsx` asserts the second half and not the first, so the rule the comment in that file describes is currently unproven in the tree; copying the assertion there is available to a later ticket and is not required by this contract.

Each row's styled split lives in this section rather than in the Styled parts table, following The v0.1 set and The overlay set, and that table stays the v0 set plus Navigation Menu's row. The four accessibility rows go in the Accessibility contract table below, which is universal. The per-component contracts go under Per-component notes in this same order, after Menubar's. Iconography grows only where a component ships a glyph, which is each contract's to settle, and none of the four does: Accordion's caret is the consumer's, the way Collapsible's is; `Avatar.Fallback` is a layout slot the caller fills, the way `Alert.Icon` is; and Scroll Area has no indicator, icon, or arrow part at all, so there is nothing to put a glyph inside. The set is closed now that all four contracts are written, so this no longer stands on present knowledge. **Overlays is untouched by this set**, because none of the four portals or floats anything. The rows and the prose are written by [Accordion's contract](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/143), [Avatar's contract](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/144), [Scroll Area's contract](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/145), and [Toggle's contract](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/142), which is the point of unblocking them.

### The Command set

Command, the section a single contract effort produces when its output shares a definitional property with nothing else unseated. Sectioned on [Where the six contracts are written and where entries land in catalogue order](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/284), over the contract on [Command's contract and the searchable command-dialog example](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/280) and the spine on [#277](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/277).

**A set of one is the naming rule at its limit, and the two homes that could have claimed it both fail the coverage test.** The primitive kin is Combobox, but Combobox sits in The v0.1 set, whose release name is honest only because that section holds its release whole, and one v0.2 item cannot join it. The anchored `Popup` is the thirteenth overlay, which made The overlay set the stronger candidate; it is declined because that set's members are the components whose contract *is* the floating surface, while Command's contract is the filtered list and `Root open inline` renders no popup at all. What is left names itself.

| Component | Item | Built on | Parts | Axes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Command | `command` | Base UI `Autocomplete` | `Root`, `Value`, `Trigger`, `Input`, `InputGroup`, `Icon`, `Clear`, `List`, `Status`, `Portal`, `Backdrop`, `Positioner`, `Popup`, `Arrow`, `Group`, `GroupLabel`, `Item`, `Row`, `Collection`, `Empty`, `Separator`; `useFilter` and `useFilteredItems` alongside the parts | `size` on `InputGroup`: `sm`, `md`, `lg` |

**Styled**: `InputGroup`, `Input`, `Trigger`, `Icon`, `Clear`, `Popup`, `Arrow`, `List`, `Item`, `Status`, `Empty`, `Separator`, `GroupLabel`. **Passed through**: `Root`, `Value`, `Collection`, `Portal`, `Backdrop`, `Positioner`, `Group`, `Row`. The paint per part, the `filter` scorer socket, and the thirteenth-overlay `Popup` are under Per-component notes; the accessibility row is in the table below.

**The recipe adds no entry and the line stays one checkbox.** The searchable command-dialog example is the entry's documented example rather than an independent deliverable, the shape Sheet set on the overlay line, so the recipe splits off no line of its own. Its contract lives as a `## Command dialog` section on Command's page and is summarized under Per-component notes.

**Catalogue order.** `apps/docs/src/components.ts` appends `command` after `color-field`, carrying `release: 'v0.2'`, the first of the nine entries the remaining unchecked lines add. Across the nine, the specification-order array goes from forty-five to fifty-four and `componentsInRelease('v0.2')` from thirteen to twenty-two.

### The date set

Calendar and Date Picker, the two items the Zag `date-picker` machine carries. Sectioned on [Where the six contracts are written and where entries land in catalogue order](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/284), over the contract on [Calendar and Date Picker: shape, engine, and the ADR 0002 question](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/281) and the spine on [#277](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/277).

**The name is the property, the first property-named set since The overlay set.** Both items exist to let a user pick a date; both run the same machine through `@zag-js/react`, `connect`, and `normalizeProps`; and `date-picker.tsx` restates `calendar.tsx`'s grid paint rather than importing it, because the picker's grid belongs to the picker's own machine. That is a tighter bond than the overlay set's shared recipe membership — one primitive, one grid, one accessibility model — and "date" is a property no other catalogue item could join the set on.

| Component | Item | Built on | Parts | Axes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Calendar | `calendar` | Zag `date-picker` machine via `@zag-js/react` | `Root`, `Label`, `Content`, `ViewControl`, `PrevTrigger`, `NextTrigger`, `ViewTrigger`, `RangeText`, `View`, `Table`, `TableHead`, `TableBody`, `TableRow`, `TableHeader`, `TableCell`, `TableCellTrigger`, `MonthSelect`, `YearSelect` | none |
| Date Picker | `date-picker` | Zag `date-picker` machine via `@zag-js/react` | `Root`, `Label`, `Control`, `Input`, `Trigger`, `ClearTrigger`, `PresetTrigger`, `Content`, `Positioner`, `Portal`, `ViewControl`, `PrevTrigger`, `NextTrigger`, `ViewTrigger`, `RangeText`, `View`, `Table`, `TableHead`, `TableBody`, `TableRow`, `TableHeader`, `TableCell`, `TableCellTrigger`, `MonthSelect`, `YearSelect` | `size` on `Control`: `sm`, `md`, `lg` |

| Component | Styled | Passed through |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Calendar | `Root`, `Label`, `ViewControl`, `PrevTrigger`, `NextTrigger`, `ViewTrigger`, `RangeText`, `Table`, `TableHeader`, `TableCell`, `TableCellTrigger`, `MonthSelect`, `YearSelect` | `Content`, `View`, `TableHead`, `TableBody`, `TableRow` |
| Date Picker | `Label`, `Control`, `Input`, `Trigger`, `ClearTrigger`, `PresetTrigger`, `Content`, `ViewControl`, `PrevTrigger`, `NextTrigger`, `ViewTrigger`, `RangeText`, `Table`, `TableHeader`, `TableCell`, `TableCellTrigger`, `MonthSelect`, `YearSelect` | `Root`, `Portal`, `Positioner`, `View`, `TableHead`, `TableBody`, `TableRow` |

**Both files are the Zag architecture**, the prop-getter shape the ADR 0002 amendment admits as a bounded second primitive source: `Root` runs `useMachine` and `connect` and hands the api to the parts through file-private context, each part spreads its getter onto a fixed semantic element and merges `stylex.props` through Zag's `mergeProps`, and no part takes `render` because the machine owns the element. The two items stay separate because their prop surfaces and live behavior differ — `inline` is pinned on one and omitted on the other — which is the component-identity test's second clause, applied on [#277](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/277). The paint, the `hidden`-attribute item 8, and the restatement reasoning are under Per-component notes; the accessibility rows are in the table below.

**Catalogue order.** `apps/docs/src/components.ts` appends `calendar` and `date-picker` after `command`, in the checklist line's own order, each carrying `release: 'v0.2'`.

### The Button Group, Input Group, Input OTP, and Native Select set

The four catalogue items of the input-group line, contracts settled on [The input-group set contracts: Button Group, Input Group, Input OTP, and Native Select](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/278). Sectioned on [Where the six contracts are written and where entries land in catalogue order](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/284).

**The name lists the members, and the listing is the honest name.** The kinship is real: each of the four is one control assembled from pieces — a row of buttons reading as one control, a box holding an input and its addons, N slots holding one code, a native element plus a private glyph — and each carries `size` at the three control heights. Neither reaches a name: "composite" reaches Command and Date Picker too, and "size-carrying" reaches them as well, so no property picks these four out of the release. The rule The Toggle, Accordion, Avatar, and Scroll Area set set stands a second time: where the members share nothing a name can hold, the name lists them.

| Component | Item | Built on | Parts | Axes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Button Group | `button-group` | plain `Root`; `Item` a Base UI `Button` | `Root`, `Item` | `variant`, `tone`, `size` on `Root` reaching `Item` through context; `variant` and `tone` overridable per `Item`, `size` not |
| Input Group | `input-group` | plain `Root`; `Input` a Base UI `Input` | `Root`, `Input`, `Addon` | `size` on `Root`: `sm`, `md`, `lg` |
| Input OTP | `input-otp` | Base UI `OTPField` | `Root`, `Input`, `Separator` | `size` on `Root`, uniform across the slots |
| Native Select | `native-select` | plain `Root`; `Select` a `Field.Control` rendered `<select>` | `Root`, `Select` | `size` on `Select`: `sm`, `md`, `lg` |

| Component | Styled | Passed through |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Button Group | `Root`, `Item` | none |
| Input Group | `Root`, `Input`, `Addon` | none |
| Input OTP | `Root`, `Input`, `Separator` | none |
| Native Select | `Root`, `Select` | none |

**No part passes through, which is what one control assembled from pieces costs.** Every root either sets layout or wears the box, and every inner part paints. Button Group's `Root` is styled for its `inline-flex` orientation table, the positional-prop shape `separator.tsx` and `menubar.tsx` already carry and not an axis; Input Group's `Root` restates `Combobox.InputGroup`'s box with the state attributes read through `:has()`; Input OTP's `Separator` and Native Select's private chevron are each the piece no primitive ships. Defaults are `solid`/`accent`/`md` on Button Group — Button's whole column, because the restatement is meant to be identical — and `md` elsewhere. The four per-component contracts are under Per-component notes and the four accessibility rows are in the table below.

**Catalogue order.** `apps/docs/src/components.ts` appends `button-group`, `input-group`, `input-otp`, and `native-select` after the date pair, keeping the line's own order, each carrying `release: 'v0.2'`.

### The Aspect Ratio and Resizable set

The two items of the line whose recipe is Carousel, contracts settled on [Aspect Ratio, Resizable, and Carousel contracts](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/279). Sectioned on [Where the six contracts are written and where entries land in catalogue order](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/284).

**The name lists the pair for the same reason the set above lists four.** What the two share — each shapes a region's geometry rather than collecting input — is true and loose at once: "layout" would reach Sidebar and every container already seated, so the member-list rule applies a third time.

| Component | Item | Built on | Parts | Axes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Aspect Ratio | `aspect-ratio` | plain, `useRender` on `Root` | `Root` | none; `ratio` is a value prop, not an axis |
| Resizable | `resizable` | Zag `splitter` machine via `@zag-js/react` | `Root`, `Panel`, `Handle`, `HandleIndicator`; `useResizable()` alongside the parts | none; `orientation` is a pass-through prop keyed to a style table |

| Component | Styled | Passed through |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Resizable | `Root`, `Handle`, `HandleIndicator` | `Panel` |

Aspect Ratio is a single-part component and has no split row, The v0.1 set's convention: a one-part component's only part is styled by definition. `Panel` is this set's one pass-through for the opposite reason a second-clause part is styled — the splitter machine already writes `overflow: hidden` inline on every panel, an inline style beats a StyleX class, and the primitive reads its own declaration, which is Scroll Area's "do not write what the primitive wrote" arriving through a machine rather than through Base UI's inline styles. Resizable is the second entry under the ADR 0002 amendment and carries the date set's architecture: `useMachine` plus `connect` on `Root`, file-private context, `mergeProps` on every part, and no `render` prop.

**Catalogue order.** `apps/docs/src/components.ts` appends `aspect-ratio` and `resizable` after the four controls, last of the nine, each carrying `release: 'v0.2'`. Carousel adds no entry and changes no array: it is a recipe, and its contract is under Per-component notes with the other four.

### One file per component

A component is one file, `packages/ui/src/<name>.tsx`, and one registry item of the same name. The file holds the StyleX tables at module scope, then the parts, then the export. Compound components are still one file. Demos live in `apps/docs`, never beside the component.

Every file starts with `'use client'`. Base UI parts carry their own client boundary, but a plain component that uses `useRender` does not, and the directive is harmless under Vite.

**A component never imports a sibling relatively.** It imports `@ultima/ui/<name>`, which `packages/ui/package.json` exports through a `./*` subpath. Decided on What the expanded v0 catalogue changes about the proof bar and the release gate (ULT-58), because the registry build derives `registryDependencies` and rewrites import paths by matching `@ultima/*` specifiers: a relative `./dialog` is skipped by both, silently producing an item that installs a file importing a component the consumer was never told to install. Sidebar composing Dialog is the first case in v0.

### The shared lib

One registry item, `lib/component.ts`, holds the helper types every component uses. Components depend on it the way shadcn components depend on `lib/utils`. In the workspace it lives at `packages/ui/src/lib/component.ts` and a component imports it as `@ultima/ui/lib/component`; the registry build stages it beside the token sources so it installs to `@/lib/component.ts`.

```ts
import type * as stylex from '@stylexjs/stylex';

/** The only styling escape hatch: StyleX styles merged last, so they win per property. */
export type StyleProp = stylex.StyleXStyles;

/** A Base UI part's props with Ultima's style slot in place of className and style. */
export type PartProps<BaseProps> = Omit<BaseProps, 'className' | 'style'> & { style?: StyleProp };

/** A native element's props with the same treatment, for plain components. */
export type PlainProps<E extends keyof React.JSX.IntrinsicElements> = PartProps<React.ComponentProps<E>>;
```

### Props every component accepts

- `style?: StyleProp`, on every part and slot. It is the only escape hatch. Passed last to `stylex.props`, so a caller override wins per property.
- No `className`. Registry consumers own the source and edit it instead.
- `render`, on every Base UI part (passed through) and on the root of every plain component (through Base UI's `useRender`). Plain slots such as `Card.Header` are two-line functions with no `render`.
- `ref` is a plain prop under React 19. No `forwardRef`.

A Base UI part is written as `<BasePart {...props} {...stylex.props(styles.part, style)} />`. A plain root is written with `useRender({ defaultTagName, render, props: { ...props, ...stylex.props(styles.root, style) } })`.

### Compound components

A single-part component exports one function: `Button`, `Input`, `Switch`, `Textarea`, `Skeleton`, `Spinner`. A multi-part component exports one namespace object of parts, `Card.Root`, `Dialog.Popup`, matching Base UI's part names wherever a primitive exists. Parts Ultima does not style (`Dialog.Portal`, `Tooltip.Provider`) sit on the same object, re-exported unchanged, so a consumer imports one name. Flat `DialogPopup`-style exports do not exist.

### Cross-part state

Decided on Cross-part state in Ultima (ULT-52), and recorded as ADR 0006, because a hook a component exports is a name in code the consumer owns and calls. Two foundation components had already answered this differently: Tabs holds `variant` in a context private to `tabs.tsx` so `List`, `Tab`, and `Indicator` read what `Root` was given, and Meter made the caller pass `tone` to `Indicator` and `Value` separately. The rule below covers both, and Meter changes to follow it.

**A value the parts must agree on lives in context on the root. A value that can sensibly differ from part to part is a prop on each part.** That is the test an author applies. Tabs' `variant` cannot differ between its list and its indicator, so it is context. Sidebar's open state has to reach its trigger and its panel together, so it is context. A per-item `active` flag differs by item, so it stays a prop. Where a value is context and a per-part override still makes sense, the part accepts the same prop and the prop wins. Meter's `tone` on `Root` reaching `Indicator` and `Value` is the v0 case; Progress copies it.

Context is created in the component's own file with `createContext` and read with `use`. It is never a separate registry item and never a provider the consumer has to remember: the component's `Root` is the provider. One file per component means the context and every part that reads it install together, so the registry item shape does not change.

**Axis context carries a real default and never throws.** `Tabs.List` outside `Tabs.Root` renders as `underline`. A part rendered outside its root degrades rather than failing the consumer's render, and the component's docs page says which parts need the root.

**A component with shared runtime state exports a hook, `use<Component>()`.** Sidebar exports `useSidebar()`. The hook is how a consumer builds a part of their own that reads or sets the state, the flexibility shadcn's Sidebar gives through the same name. Its return type is public surface, documented on the component page beside the parts. The hook throws with a named message when called outside the root (`useSidebar must be used inside Sidebar.Root`), because a consumer calls it deliberately and a silent no-op would hide their bug. A component whose context carries only an axis, like Tabs, exports no hook.

**Shared runtime state is controlled or uncontrolled, with Base UI's names.** `open`, `defaultOpen`, `onOpenChange`, resolved per render as `open ?? internal` rather than latched at mount, so a consumer can move between the two. A second state value follows the same pattern under its own name: Sidebar's `mobileOpen`, `defaultMobileOpen`, `onMobileOpenChange`.

Base UI's primitives hold their own context across their parts. This rule says nothing about that; it governs context Ultima writes. `Toast.Provider` and `Tooltip.Provider` are that primitive context, mounted by the consumer, and they are not a violation of "never a provider the consumer mounts": that sentence is about context Ultima writes. Toast writes none. A flat Sidebar taking navigation as a data prop, so no tree exists for state to cross, was ruled out: it breaks the compound rule above and cannot take the consumer's router link through `render`.

### Styled parts

A compound component exposes every Base UI part, but most parts have nothing to paint. Apply the same styling rule to every part, including parts added after the foundation set:

**A part carries Ultima styles if it paints — background, border, shadow, or color — or if it sets its own type or spacing. A part passes through unstyled if its whole job is to portal, position, or group.** Unstyled by that rule across the system: `Portal`, `Root`, `Group`, `RadioGroup`, `SubmenuRoot`, and `Viewport`, except where a component's row below says otherwise. `Positioner` is a near-exception: it gets `outline: 0` and nothing else, following Base UI's own demos.

**A part also carries Ultima styles when the primitive's behavior depends on CSS the primitive does not supply.** Reading a variable the primitive seeds, or clipping or sizing something a transition needs. Added on Contracts for the reusable patterns the docs pulls into v0 (ULT-57), because ULT-49 found the first case where the first clause produces a component that does not work: `NavigationMenu.Positioner` and `NavigationMenu.Viewport` must carry real CSS or the menu neither resizes nor clips, and both are part names the first clause classifies as pass-through across the system. That holds whether or not Navigation Menu ever ships, because the next anchored primitive with a size morph has the same shape. `NavigationMenu.List` is the same clause for a different reason, found by running the component on [Can StyleX express Navigation Menu's size morph, and what does it cost at the emitted-CSS level](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/85) rather than by reading it: `data-activation-direction` is gated on the two triggers' rects differing on the menu's axis, so an unstyled `List` of block `<li>` elements leaves it `null` forever and `Content`'s directional slide is dead code that never runs. Its `display: flex` is load-bearing for a state rather than for appearance, which is what the clause describes. A stated exception per component was the alternative and does not scale: the table would accumulate exceptions instead of a rule. The clause covers `Collapsible.Panel`, whose height animation reads `--collapsible-panel-height`, and it is also why `Dialog.Viewport` is styled, which the first clause alone never explained. v0.2 adds `Menubar`, settled on [Menubar's shape, given Base UI ships it as a container rather than a namespace](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/110) and the first case where the primitive reads back the styled element's own box rather than a variable it seeded: `modal` defaults to `true`, and the internal backdrop an open menu renders cuts its hole from `getBoundingClientRect()` of the bar, so the bar's `inline-size` decides where clicks still land. It is single-part and therefore has no row to move here, which is the one way a second-clause example can fail the sentence above. **The clause has one boundary, and Drawer is where it was needed**, settled on [Drawer's parts, its styled split, and whether it varies the overlay recipe](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/113): it reaches a part whose own rendering, or a sibling part's behavior, breaks without the CSS, and it does not reach a part that wraps the consumer's application, where the CSS is the app shell rather than the component. `Drawer.Indent` and `Drawer.IndentBackground` are the first parts on the far side of that line, and they pass through unstyled for the reasons in The overlay set above; without them the drawer works completely and only an optional decoration is absent, which is not `NavigationMenu.List`'s situation of a shipped feature left silently dead. **`Avatar.Image` is the case that separates the clause's two shapes**, settled on [Avatar's contract: its parts, its size and shape, and what an image without a name means](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/144): it paints under the first clause anyway, so the clause does not rescue it, and what the clause supplies is a constraint on the styling it already has. Under `keepMounted` the not-yet-loaded image is hidden with `visibility` and never with `display: none`, because an element with no box never intersects the viewport and a lazy image inside one is never loaded at all — `NavigationMenu.List`'s silently dead feature reached a second time, through the obvious way to hide something rather than through leaving a part unstyled. `Collapsible.Panel`'s `hiddenGuard` is the same shape, and neither moves a row into the table below. **`ScrollArea.Viewport` is the clause's clearest case since Navigation Menu's**, settled on [Scroll Area's contract: its six parts, its scrollbar visibility, and the focus ring it copies from Table.Scroll](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/145): Base UI writes its `overflow: scroll` inline and leaves its size alone, so without Ultima's `block-size: 100%` the viewport sizes to its own content, nothing overflows, `hiddenState` stays both-hidden, `Scrollbar` and `Corner` return `null`, and `tabIndex` stays `-1`. Every one of those is invisible in a screenshot of short content, which is what item 8 of the proof bar exists to catch. It does not move a row either, because the ring already styles it under the first clause.

**Triggers that wrap the consumer's element pass through unstyled.** `Menu.Trigger`, `Tooltip.Trigger`, `Dialog.Trigger`, `Dialog.Close`, `AlertDialog.Trigger`, `AlertDialog.Close`, `Toast.Action`, `Toast.Close`, `Sidebar.Trigger`, `Sidebar.Close`, `Collapsible.Trigger`, `Popover.Trigger`, and `Popover.Close` exist to be given the consumer's own button through `render`, and `stylex.props` does not merge foreign class strings, so an Ultima style on the wrapper and an Ultima style on the Button rendered into it collide instead of cascading. Each is documented as `render={<Button />}` and ships no styles at all, not even the reset or the focus ring. `Select.Trigger` is styled, because it *is* the control rather than a wrapper around one. `Combobox.Trigger` is styled too: in the canonical pattern it is the chevron button inside `InputGroup`, not a slot for the consumer's Button. `Accordion.Trigger` is the fourth, settled on [Accordion's contract: its parts, its styled split, its heading level, and how far it copies Collapsible](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/143), and it is the one that shows the rule turns on the arrangement rather than on the primitive: it is the same `useButton` shape as `Collapsible.Trigger`, which ships no styles at all, but an accordion trigger is a full-measure row of the accordion rather than a slot for a control the consumer already has, and the APG permits nothing beside it inside the heading. `HoverCard.Trigger` is the third, settled on [#111](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/111): `PreviewCard.Trigger` renders the `<a>` itself with `href` passed through rather than wrapping a caller's element, so the collision the rule prevents cannot arise and the ring is Ultima's own. It is also the only part in the system that sets `text-decoration: underline`, because it is the only link Ultima ships inside the consumer's prose rather than inside chrome.

This qualifies the focus-ring rows in the accessibility contract below: on Dropdown Menu, Tooltip, Dialog, Alert Dialog, Toast, Sidebar, and Collapsible the ring is rendered by whatever the consumer puts in the trigger or close slot, and each docs page says the slot must hold an Ultima Button or an element carrying its own ring.

| Component | Styled | Passed through |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Dropdown Menu | `Popup`, `Item`, `LinkItem`, `CheckboxItem`, `RadioItem`, `SubmenuTrigger`, `CheckboxItemIndicator`, `RadioItemIndicator`, `Separator`, `GroupLabel`, `Arrow` | `Root`, `Trigger`, `Portal`, `Backdrop`, `Positioner` (`outline: 0`), `Group`, `RadioGroup`, `SubmenuRoot`, `Viewport` |
| Select | `Label`, `Trigger`, `Value`, `Icon`, `Popup`, `Item`, `ItemText`, `ItemIndicator`, `ScrollUpArrow`, `ScrollDownArrow`, `Separator`, `GroupLabel` | `Root`, `Portal`, `Backdrop`, `Positioner` (`outline: 0`), `List`, `Group`, `Arrow` |
| Dialog | `Viewport`, `Backdrop`, `Popup`, `Title`, `Description` | `Root`, `Trigger`, `Portal`, `Close` |
| Tooltip | `Popup`, `Arrow` | `Provider`, `Root`, `Trigger`, `Portal`, `Positioner` (`outline: 0`), `Viewport` |
| Tabs | `Root`, `List`, `Tab`, `Indicator`, `Panel` | none |
| Meter | `Root`, `Label`, `Track`, `Indicator`, `Value` | none |
| Switch | `Root`, `Thumb` | none |
| Sidebar | `Panel`, `Group`, `GroupLabel`, `List`, `Link`, `Button` | `Root`, `Item`, `Trigger`, `Close` |
| Collapsible | `Panel` | `Root`, `Trigger` |
| Toggle Group | `Root`, `Item` | none |
| Navigation Menu | `List`, `Trigger`, `Icon`, `Content`, `Positioner`, `Viewport`, `Popup`, `Arrow`, `Link` | `Root`, `Item`, `Portal`, `Backdrop` |

`Field.Control` is the third part the wrapper rule takes back from the first clause, and The v0.1 set above says why: Ultima's `Input` already *is* `Field.Control`, so styling the part would paint one element from two tables. Combobox's, Slider's, Alert's, Alert Dialog's, Toast's, Progress's, and Empty's styled splits live in that same section rather than the table below, which stays the v0 set plus Navigation Menu's row. Breadcrumb's and Pagination's splits are in The navigation set, because a plain component styles every part and has nothing to tabulate. The overlay set's five splits are in that section too, settled on [Where the five overlay contracts are written, and where they land in catalogue order](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/117): the table below stays the v0 set plus Navigation Menu's row, which is there because `NavigationMenu.Positioner`, `Viewport`, and `List` are this section's own worked example of the second clause, so the row sits beside the rule it proves. The Toggle, Accordion, Avatar, and Scroll Area set's four splits are in that section too, settled on [Where the four contracts are written, what the section is named, and where they land in catalogue order](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/146). **Two of the four are second-clause examples and no row moves for either**, which is the sentence that stood here as "none of the four" until [Avatar's contract](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/144) found the first and [Scroll Area's](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/145) the second. `Accordion.Panel` reading `--accordion-panel-height` is the case `Collapsible.Panel` above already is rather than another one. `Avatar.Image` is a real second-clause part and the clause's other shape: the rule that its hide is `visibility` and never `display` is CSS `keepMounted`'s lazy loading depends on, but the part paints under the first clause regardless, so the clause constrains how it is styled rather than rescuing it from pass-through. `ScrollArea.Viewport` is the same shape again — the first clause styles it for the focus ring, and the clause adds the `block-size: 100%` without which nothing overflows and the component silently stops being a scroll area. **The rule for moving a row is the rescue**, which is why `NavigationMenu.List` sits below and the other three do not: a component in a later set whose split the first clause gets *wrong* moves its row here and says so. The nine splits of the four sections [Where the six contracts are written and where entries land in catalogue order](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/284) writes live in those sections, and no row moves: `Command.List`'s scroll declarations are `Combobox.List`'s case a second time, `Resizable.Panel` passes through because the splitter machine writes `overflow: hidden` inline itself rather than because nothing needs painting, and the rest style under the first clause.

`Select`'s scroll arrows are the one part the rule alone would get wrong, and the per-component notes say why they are styled. `Dialog.Viewport` and `Collapsible.Panel` are the second clause rather than exceptions. `Collapsible.Trigger` is the one part the first clause would style and the wrapper-trigger rule takes back: it is a real control carrying its own `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls`, but the spec already composes it as `Collapsible.Trigger render={<Sidebar.Button />}`, which is exactly the collision the wrapper rule prevents.

The plain components (Card, Table, Stat, Code, Badge, Alert, Empty, Skeleton, Spinner, and v0.2's Breadcrumb and Pagination) have no pass-through parts: Ultima writes every element, so every part is styled. Sidebar is the plain component that does have them: `Root` only groups and provides context, `Item` is a bare `<li>`, and `Trigger` and `Close` are wrapper triggers.

**A component paints to its own measure and knows nothing about what contains it.** Settled on [Whether an Accordion inside Sidebar or Card changes either component's note](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/164). The rules above say which parts paint; this one says how far, and the answer is the box the part is given and not one step past it. Where a container's padding and a contained component's full-measure rules disagree the consumer reconciles it through the `style` slot, on whichever side owns the property in dispute. **Accordion inside `Card.Body` is the worked example**: the body carries space step 6 of `padding-inline`, so every `Item` hairline stops short of the card's edges, which is the opposite of how a bordered list usually sits in a card. The reconciliation is `Card.Body` taking `paddingInline: 0` with the panel's content wrapper taking the padding back, rather than `Accordion.Item` taking a negative inline margin, because the padding is Card's to withdraw and a negative margin only pretends the box is wider than it is. **Neither component's note names the other, and this is stated generally so that no pair ever needs one.** The catalogue is forty-four items and pairs are quadratic; a note per pair would be unwritable at this size and stale at any. **The line between a component and page layout** does not already cover this and is not stretched to reach it: that rule is about who may build a surface or a control, and two Ultima components nesting is not a case of one being built in the wrong place. Accordion's docs page prints the in-Card composition as a `style` override rather than as a demo of its own, which is the move Avatar's page already makes for the square and the resized avatar.

### Variants, sizes, and tones

Three axis props across the whole system, and no others: `variant` (shape and emphasis), `size` (the three control heights), and `tone` (which color role the component wears). A component declares only the axes it needs, and only the values it supports. The tables are `variants`, `sizes`, and `tones`; the prop unions are `keyof typeof` each table, exported as `<Component>Variant`, `<Component>Size`, and `<Component>Tone`. Defaults are declared in the destructure. There is no `cva`. Textarea is the one place `size` is not a fixed height: the same three space steps are `min-height`, so a `md` textarea starts as tall as a `md` Input and can grow. Combobox's `size` is the same three heights as Input, carried on `InputGroup`. Slider has no `size`: its track follows Meter's cross-axis size, not a control height. Alert has no `size` and no `variant`: `tone` is its only axis. Toast has the same six tones and neither of the other two, forwarded as Base UI `type` / `data-type` rather than as a prop on Root. Progress takes Meter's five tones on `Root` and neither of the other two. Skeleton, Spinner, and Empty have no axes. The input-group set, settled on [#278](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/278): Button Group carries all three on `Root` reaching `Item` through context, `variant` and `tone` overridable per item and `size` not; Input Group and Input OTP carry `size` on `Root`, uniform across what it groups; Native Select carries `size` on `Select`, the part that is the control, which is where `Select.Trigger` already carries it.

`tone` was added on Per-component contracts for the v0 set (ULT-19), for the three v0 components that let a caller choose a hue: Button, Badge, and Meter. Its values are named for the color roles, so `tone="danger"` reaches `--ult-color-danger` and `--ult-color-danger-contrast` together and the caller never names a token. A component that needs a neutral tone includes `neutral` in its own table; `neutral` is not a color role and has no `--ult-color-neutral`, so each component says which neutral tokens it uses. Slider does not take `tone`: it is a value picker, not a measurement, settled on ULT-81. Alert takes Badge's six tones and Badge's `subtle` column only, settled on ULT-82: a callout has no `solid` counterpart worth a `variant`. Toast takes the same six, settled on ULT-83, as `type` on `add()` rather than a `tone` prop on Root, so there are not two sources for one colour. Progress takes Meter's five, settled on ULT-84: it is task completion, so a failed upload and a finished job are tones the way a Meter reading is. Spinner does not take `tone`: it is a decorative mark, not a measurement.

One `stylex.create` table per axis, indexed by the prop:

```tsx
const sizes = stylex.create({ sm: { ... }, md: { ... }, lg: { ... } });

export type ButtonSize = keyof typeof sizes;
```

**The one compound.** `variant` and `size` never set the same property, so they layer. `variant` and `tone` both set color, so they cannot: whichever comes last in `stylex.props` wins the property outright, and `outline` would lose its transparent background to a tone's fill, or the tone would lose its fill to `outline`. Where a component has both axes they merge into one nested lookup, one `stylex.create` per variant holding that variant's tones, read as `variants[variant][tone]`:

```tsx
const solid = stylex.create({ accent: { ... }, danger: { ... } });
const outline = stylex.create({ accent: { ... }, danger: { ... } });
const ghost = stylex.create({ accent: { ... }, danger: { ... } });
const variants = { solid, outline, ghost };

export type ButtonVariant = keyof typeof variants;
export type ButtonTone = keyof typeof solid;

export function Button({ variant = 'solid', size = 'md', tone = 'accent', style, ...props }: ButtonProps) {
  return <BaseButton {...props} {...stylex.props(styles.root, variants[variant][tone], sizes[size], style)} />;
}
```

Every cell is written out. The tables stay small because each component declares only the tones it supports: six cells on Button, twelve on Badge, six on Alert, five on Progress. A value that depends on both `variant` and `size` still nests as a conditional inside the variant table, and the spec notes it on that component.

The local-custom-property alternative, where `tone` sets `--ult-tone-fill` and one variant table reads it, is closed: StyleX emits `create` rules that set custom properties outside the cascade layer when `useCSSLayers` is on ([#1611](https://github.com/facebook/stylex/issues/1611)), and Ultima keeps `useCSSLayers`. Revisit if that is fixed; the nested tables are a private detail of each file, so the change would not reach consumers' props.

### State styling

- Pointer states use pseudo-classes and the state tokens: `':hover'` reads `--ult-color-<role>-hover`, `':active'` reads `--ult-color-<role>-active`. Never `color-mix()` or any color derived at the use site.
- Component state uses Base UI's data attributes inside the value: `':is([data-disabled])'`, `':is([data-open])'`, `':is([data-checked])'`, `':is([data-indeterminate])'`. The `className` function form is never used, so the class stays static.
- Focus uses `':focus-visible'` and `--ult-color-border-focus`. Combobox is the first `:focus-within` ring: the visual box is `InputGroup`, focus lands on `Input`, and `:focus-visible` on the group never matches. `:focus-within` misses StyleX's camelCase lookup the same way `:focus-visible` does and lands at 3040; the ring still survives because it is an `outline`.
- Dynamic styles (function values in `stylex.create`) are allowed only for runtime numbers such as a meter width, never for variants.
- **Validation state is styled through `':is([aria-invalid="true"], [data-invalid])'`**, one condition matching both the standalone and the in-Field paths. Settled by measurement on ULT-79 against the installed `@base-ui/react` 1.8.0, because ULT-74 and ULT-76 disagreed about what the attribute even holds and neither had run the code. What it holds is `aria-invalid="true"`: the valueless form ULT-76 reported does not occur, so the selector that shipped with Input was never broken. What it does not survive is a disabled control, and that is the reason for the second half. Base UI drops `aria-invalid` on a control that is disabled while invalid and deliberately keeps `data-invalid` there, so an `aria-invalid` selector alone silently loses the danger border on a control whose error message is still rendered and still in `aria-describedby`. The two disabled paths differ and only one is a hazard: disabling the **control** keeps `data-invalid` and the error, while disabling the whole **`Field.Root`** resets the validity state, so both attributes and the message go together and there is nothing left to style. Both halves of the condition are therefore load-bearing, and neither is redundant.
- **A pointer state that must not override a component state is nested inside it, never written as its sibling.** StyleX sorts `':hover'` above a data attribute, so `':hover'` and `':is([data-pressed])'` as siblings of one property repaint a pressed control as a resting one under the pointer, which is the selection appearing to move. The pressed value carries its own `':hover'` inside it instead: `':is([data-pressed])': { default: X, ':hover': X }`. This has been the shape of `toggle-group.tsx`'s item table since ULT-57 and is stated here on [Toggle's contract: one part or a namespace, its axes, and what names it](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/142), the second file to need it, because the rule is the system's rather than either component's. It is the same shape as the media-query rule below and holds for the same reason.
- A media query is an at-rule key inside the value, and it outranks every state condition written beside it. A condition that must hold inside the query is nested inside it: `[DESKTOP]: { default: 'blue', ':hover': 'green' }`, never `':hover'` as a sibling of `[DESKTOP]`. Two components write one. Sidebar writes a breakpoint, under its per-component notes. A looping animation writes `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` to set `animation-name: none` (and the static fallback that implies), under Motion above. Transitions still never write that query: they read a duration token. The nesting rule is the system's either way.

**How StyleX actually orders these**, measured on Responsive styling in StyleX 0.19.0 (ULT-51) against this repo's installed compiler and written down here because both findings are already true of the components that shipped before Sidebar, and neither is in StyleX's documentation.

Each condition in one property value becomes its own rule carrying a StyleX priority number: the property's priority plus a contribution per condition. Under `useCSSLayers`, that number also picks the cascade layer (`Math.floor(priority / 1000)`). Within a layer, CSS specificity still applies. StyleX sorts equal-priority rules by an alphabetical comparison of the rule text; source order decides only when the matching rules also tie on specificity. Do not rely on that alphabetical tiebreak.

- **`':focus-visible'` sorts below `':hover'`, not above it.** StyleX's pseudo-class priority table spells two of its keys in camelCase, `':focusVisible': 160` and `':focusWithin': 140`, which are not CSS pseudo-class names. The real spellings miss the lookup and take the default contribution of 40, so `':focus-visible'` lands at 3040 rather than the documented 3160. Writing the camelCase form to reach 160 is not a workaround: it emits `:focusVisible` into the selector, which matches nothing. The practical order for the conditions Ultima writes is `:focus-visible`, `:focus-within`, and `:is([data-*])` tied at 3040, then `:hover` at 3130, then `:active` at 3170. Ultima's ring survives this only because it is an `outline` and the hover state sets background and color, so the two never contend for a property. A component that ever wants a focused appearance to beat a hovered one on the same property nests the focus condition inside the hover one rather than trusting the order.
- **A transition cancelled with the `transition` shorthand silently never wins.** Measured in Chromium on [Can StyleX express Navigation Menu's size morph, and what does it cost at the emitted-CSS level](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/85). Base UI's own demos write `&[data-instant] { transition: none }`, which compiles to priority 2040 and therefore lands in an *earlier* cascade layer than the `transition-duration` longhand at 3000, so the cancel loses and the transition runs anyway — reported at `0.2s` with `data-instant` present. Written as `transitionDuration: { ':is([data-instant])': '0s' }` it compiles to 3040, the same layer as every other `:is([data-*])`, and reports `0s`. **Ultima writes the longhand, never the shorthand**, and this holds for every Base UI demo rule that cancels a transition rather than only for Navigation Menu's. `width`, `height`, and `max-width` sit at 4000, a later layer than all `transition-*` at 3000, which is why a size morph's dimensions are never in contention with its timing.
- **A bare media query at 3200 outranks all of them, `:hover` included.** That is what the nesting rule above is protecting against, and it is why the rule is stated as nesting rather than as ordering: there is no order of sibling keys that makes a hover inside a breakpoint work.
- **The current unplugin pipeline lowers `:dir(rtl)` to language matching.** Measured on [#118](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/118) with StyleX 0.19.0 and Lightning CSS 1.33.0. Babel preserves `':is([data-starting-style]:dir(rtl))'` at priority 3040, alongside the base state's 3040. Both land in the same cascade layer; the RTL selector has higher CSS specificity and wins even with source order reversed when native `:dir()` survives. Nesting `':dir(rtl)'` inside the state raises the StyleX priority to 3090, still in that layer. The failure happens afterward: `@stylexjs/unplugin` runs Lightning CSS with `browserslist()` targets, and this checkout's default query lowers both forms to a list of RTL `:lang()` selectors. Those selectors miss a page with `dir="rtl"` and no matching language. Chromium measured the candidate Sidebar popup at `translateX(-256px)` under that condition, despite the popup matching native `:dir(rtl)`. Modern Chrome-only targets preserve the selector, but changing browser targets or the consumer pipeline is outside this ticket. Sidebar keeps its physical mobile slide and narrows its RTL claim.

<details>
<summary>Reproduce #118's compiler measurement</summary>

Run from the repository root after `pnpm install`. This uses the Babel options in `stylex.options.ts` and the same Lightning CSS target resolution as the unplugin:

```sh
node --input-type=module <<'JS'
import { createRequire } from 'node:module';
import { resolve } from 'node:path';
import { stylexOptions } from './stylex.options.ts';
const tokens = createRequire(resolve('packages/tokens/package.json'));
const ui = createRequire(resolve('packages/ui/package.json'));
const unplugin = createRequire(ui.resolve('@stylexjs/unplugin'));
const { transformSync } = tokens('@babel/core');
const plugin = tokens('@stylexjs/babel-plugin');
const { transform, browserslistToTargets } = unplugin('lightningcss');
const browserslist = unplugin('browserslist');
const source = `import * as stylex from '@stylexjs/stylex';
export const styles = stylex.create({ popup: { transform: {
  default: 'translateX(0)',
  ':is([data-starting-style])': 'translateX(-100%)',
  ':is([data-starting-style]:dir(rtl))': 'translateX(100%)',
  ':is([data-ending-style])': 'translateX(-100%)',
  ':is([data-ending-style]:dir(rtl))': 'translateX(100%)'
} } });`;
const { metadata } = transformSync(source, {
  filename: resolve('packages/ui/src/rtl-measure.js'),
  babelrc: false, configFile: false,
  plugins: [[plugin, stylexOptions({ dev: false })]]
});
for (const [, rule, priority] of metadata.stylex) console.log(priority, rule.ltr);
const css = plugin.processStylexRules(metadata.stylex, { useLayers: true });
console.log(css);
for (const query of [undefined, 'last 2 chrome versions']) {
  const { code } = transform({
    filename: 'stylex.css', code: Buffer.from(css),
    targets: browserslistToTargets(browserslist(query))
  });
  console.log(query ?? 'defaults', code.toString());
}
JS
```

The installed compiler emitted these rules, in this order inside `@layer priority1`:

```css
.xbryuvx{transform:translateX(0)}
.x11l5x2q:is([data-ending-style]){transform:translateX(-100%)}
.xni10eo:is([data-starting-style]){transform:translateX(-100%)}
.x1sps7nv:is([data-starting-style]:dir(rtl)){transform:translateX(100%)}
.x1vs1xg5:is([data-ending-style]:dir(rtl)){transform:translateX(100%)}
```

The default rule has priority 3000; all four state rules have priority 3040. Lightning CSS then replaces `:dir(rtl)` in both RTL rules with `:is(:lang(ae), :lang(ar), :lang(arc), :lang(bcc), :lang(bqi), :lang(ckb), :lang(dv), :lang(fa), :lang(glk), :lang(he), :lang(ku), :lang(mzn), :lang(nqo), :lang(pnb), :lang(ps), :lang(sd), :lang(ug), :lang(ur), :lang(yi))` for the default targets. The Chrome-only run keeps `:dir(rtl)`. Browser targets can change as Browserslist data updates; this records the installed versions on 2026-09-16.

</details>

### Tokens in component code

Token groups are imported from the tokens file and read by their literal key: `color['--ult-color-surface']`. No local aliases, no raw values. The registry rewrites the import path on install.

### What a component may assume about the consumer's CSS

Nothing. StyleX rules sit in a cascade layer, so any unlayered consumer reset beats them. Each component therefore sets its own `box-sizing`, `margin`, `appearance`, `font-family`, and `line-height` on its root. The install flow tells consumers that their global resets must sit inside an `@layer`.

### Focus ring

One rule across the system, written inline in each component beside its base reset:

```ts
':focus-visible': { outline: `${border.focus} solid ${color['--ult-color-border-focus']}`, outlineOffset: border.focusOffset },
```

`outline` rather than `box-shadow`, because an outline survives Windows forced-colors mode. The offset is the same everywhere, dense forms included. Which parts render it is fixed per component in the contract below: every part that takes keyboard focus directly gets the ring; Menu and Select items are highlighted through `data-highlighted` with `--ult-color-surface-hover` and render no ring; Dialog, Menu, and Select popups set `outline: none`, since Base UI focuses them only as a fallback container and the popup itself is the visible signal.

### Accessibility contract

Decided on Accessibility contract per v0 component (ULT-18) for the fourteen foundation components, with Sidebar's row added on ULT-53 and ULT-55, Collapsible's and Toggle Group's on ULT-57, Field's and Fieldset's on ULT-79, Checkbox's, Radio Group's, and Textarea's on ULT-80, Combobox's and Slider's on ULT-81, Alert's and Alert Dialog's on ULT-82, Toast's on ULT-83, and Progress's, Skeleton's, Spinner's, and Empty's on ULT-84. Base UI supplies the roles, ARIA state, keyboard handling, and focus management for every interactive primitive, following the WAI-ARIA Authoring Practices. Ultima adds nothing and removes nothing there. What Ultima owns is the accessible name, the visible focus ring, and the element choice for the plain components. The table records both halves so a builder does not re-implement what the primitive gives. Every component in The v0 set has a row, and so does every component in The v0.1 set, The navigation set, The overlay set, The Toggle, Accordion, Avatar, and Scroll Area set, and the four sections [#284](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/284) writes — The Command set, The date set, The Button Group, Input Group, Input OTP, and Native Select set, and The Aspect Ratio and Resizable set — whose three navigation rows were settled on [#88](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/88), [#89](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/89), [#91](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/91), and [#92](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/92). Command's row is settled on [#280](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/280). Field and Fieldset are in the table too, not only the controls: a component that paints no control still owes a builder its element and the question of what names it, and leaving the two out left the table at twenty-nine rows for a thirty-one-item catalogue. The rest of the new sets' rows were settled on their contract tickets — Calendar's and Date Picker's on [#281](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/281), the four input-group rows on [#278](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/278), and Aspect Ratio's and Resizable's on [#279](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/279) — each before [#284](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/284) seated it.

| Component | Element or primitive | Name source | Focus ring on | Keyboard | Enforced by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Button | Base UI `Button` (`<button>`) | Text content; a Button with no text passes `aria-label` | Root | Base UI | Docs |
| Input | Base UI `Input` (`<input>`) | Consumer `<label htmlFor>`, `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby`, or a `Field.Label` | Root | Native | Docs |
| Switch | Base UI `Switch` | Wrapping `<label>`, `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby`, or a `Field.Label` | Root | Base UI (Space, Enter) | Docs |
| Select | Base UI `Select` | `Select.Label`, `aria-label` on Trigger, or a `Field.Label` with `nativeLabel={false}` | Trigger | Base UI (arrows, typeahead, Escape) | Docs |
| Dropdown Menu | Base UI `Menu` | Trigger text, or `aria-label` on Trigger | Trigger | Base UI (arrows loop, Enter, Space, typeahead, Escape, Tab closes) | Docs |
| Dialog | Base UI `Dialog` | `Dialog.Title`, always rendered; `Dialog.Description` optional | Close, and any focusable content | Base UI (Tab loops, Escape closes, focus returns to trigger) | Docs |
| Tabs | Base UI `Tabs` | Tab text | Tab | Base UI (arrows, `activateOnFocus` and `loopFocus` defaults) | Base UI |
| Tooltip | Base UI `Tooltip` | `aria-label` on `Tooltip.Trigger`, matching the tooltip text | Trigger's rendered element | Base UI (focus opens, Escape closes) | Types: `'aria-label'` required on `Tooltip.Trigger` |
| Meter | Base UI `Meter` | `Meter.Label` | None (not focusable) | None | Docs |
| Table | `<table>` parts; `Table.Scroll` a `<div role="region">` whose `tabindex` is `0` while an axis overflows and an explicit `-1` when it fits | `Table.Caption` optional on a bare table and required under `Table.Scroll`, which it names through `aria-labelledby` | `Scroll`, when used | Static; native scrolling once `Scroll` has focus | Native, Docs |
| Stat | `<div>` root, `<span>` label and value | `Stat.Label`, before `Stat.Value` in DOM order | None | Static | Native |
| Code | `<code>`, or `<pre><code>` for the block variant | Content | None | Static | Native |
| Badge | `<span>` | Content; color never carries meaning alone | None | Static | Docs |
| Card | `<div>` parts | `Card.Title` renders `<h3>` by default, changeable through `render` | None | Static | Native |
| Separator | Base UI `Separator` (`<div role="separator">`) | Optional accessible label from the consumer | None | Static | Base UI |
| Sidebar | `<nav>` on `Panel`; `<ul>`, `<li>`, `<a>`, `<button>`, `<h3>` on the rest; Dialog around `Panel` below the breakpoint | `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby` on `Sidebar.Panel`, which the mobile popup reuses; `aria-label` on the elements rendered into `Sidebar.Trigger` and `Sidebar.Close` | `Link`, `Button`; `Trigger`'s and `Close`'s rendered elements | Native (Tab, Enter, Space); Collapsible for group disclosure; Dialog for the mobile menu (Tab loops, Escape closes, focus returns to the trigger) | Types: one of `'aria-label'` or `'aria-labelledby'` required on `Sidebar.Panel` |
| Collapsible | Base UI `Collapsible` | `Trigger` text | `Trigger`'s rendered element | Base UI (native button: Space, Enter; no arrows, no Escape) | Docs |
| Toggle Group | Base UI `ToggleGroup` and `Toggle` | `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby` on `ToggleGroup.Root`; text or `aria-label` on each `Item` | `Item` | Base UI (roving tabindex, arrows along the orientation, Home and End, Space and Enter toggle) | Docs |
| Field | Base UI `Field` (`<div>` root; `Label` a `<label>`, `Description` a `<p>`, `Error` a `<div>`) | None of its own. `Field.Label` names the control in the slot, and `Description` and `Error` describe it through the `aria-describedby` merge | None | Static; every interactive element inside a Field is a component that brings its own | Docs |
| Fieldset | Base UI `Fieldset` (a real `<fieldset>`; `Legend` a `<div>`) | `Fieldset.Legend`, which `Fieldset.Root` points `aria-labelledby` at; a required group puts the word "required" in it | None | Static | Docs |
| Checkbox | Base UI `Checkbox` (`<span role="checkbox">` plus a hidden input); `Checkbox.Group` a `<div role="group">` | Wrapping `<label>`, `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby`, or a `Field.Label`. A group is named by `Fieldset.Legend` | `Root` | Base UI (Space, Enter) | Docs |
| Radio Group | Base UI `RadioGroup` and `Radio` (`<div role="radiogroup">`; each `Item` a `<span role="radio">` plus a hidden input) | `Fieldset.Legend`, or `aria-label` / `aria-labelledby` on `Root`; wrapping `<label>` or `Field.Label` on each `Item` | `Item` | Base UI (roving tabindex, arrows move and select, looping; Home and End off; Space selects) | Docs |
| Textarea | Base UI `Input` rendered as `<textarea>` | Consumer `<label htmlFor>`, `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby`, or a `Field.Label` | Root | Native (Enter inserts a newline and does not submit) | Docs |
| Combobox | Base UI `Combobox` (`<input role="combobox">` in the canonical pattern) | Consumer `<label htmlFor>`, `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby`, or a `Field.Label`. `Combobox.Label` names the Trigger, not the Input | `InputGroup` (`:focus-within`); Trigger when it is the control | Base UI (arrows, Enter, Escape, typeahead on Trigger) | Docs |
