| Slider | Base UI `Slider` (`Root` a `<div role="group">`; each Thumb a nested `<input type="range">`) | `Slider.Label`, or `aria-label` on each Thumb; a `Field.Label` with `nativeLabel={false}` | Thumb, via `:has(:focus-visible)` | Base UI (arrows, RTL-aware; Shift+arrow and PageUp/PageDown for `largeStep`; Home and End) | Docs |
| Alert | `<div>` parts; `Title` an `<h3>` by default | Visible text (Title, or Description when there is no Title); color never carries meaning alone | None | Static | Docs |
| Alert Dialog | Base UI `AlertDialog` (`Popup` `role="alertdialog"`) | `AlertDialog.Title`, always rendered; `AlertDialog.Description`, always rendered | Close, and any focusable content | Base UI (Tab loops, Escape closes, backdrop click does not, focus returns to trigger) | Docs |
| Toast | Base UI `Toast` (Viewport `role="region"`; each Root `role="dialog"` or `"alertdialog"`) | Viewport `aria-label="Notifications"`; Title labels each Root | Action's and Close's rendered elements | Base UI (F6 focuses the viewport; swipe dismisses) | Docs |
| Progress | Base UI `Progress` (`<div role="progressbar">`) | `Progress.Label` | None (not focusable) | None | Docs |
| Skeleton | `<div aria-hidden="true">` | None; the container it stands in for is `aria-busy` | None | Static | Docs |
| Spinner | `<div aria-hidden="true">` | None; the busy container is `aria-busy`; start/end speech uses the live-region pattern | None | Static | Docs |
| Empty | `<div>` parts; `Title` an `<h3>` by default | Visible text (Title, or Description when there is no Title) | None | Static | Docs |
| Breadcrumb | `<nav>` on `Root`; `<ol>`, `<li>`, `<a>` on the rest | `aria-label="Breadcrumb"` on `Breadcrumb.Root` by default, emitted only when the caller supplied neither `aria-label` nor `aria-labelledby` | `Link` | Native (Tab, Enter) | Docs |
| Pagination | `<nav>` on `Root`; `<ul>`, `<li>`, `<a>` on the rest | `aria-label="Pagination"` on `Pagination.Root`, same conditional default | `Page`, `Previous`, `Next` | Native (Tab, Enter) | Docs |
| Navigation Menu | Base UI `NavigationMenu` (`Root` a `<nav>`, a `<div>` when nested; `Popup` a second `<nav tabIndex={-1}>`; `Trigger` a `<button>`; `Link` an `<a>`) | `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby` on `Root`, the consumer's; `Popup` defaults to `aria-label="Submenu"`, same conditional rule; each Trigger by its text | `Trigger`, `Link` | Base UI (arrows move between triggers through `CompositeRoot`; ArrowDown horizontal, ArrowRight/ArrowLeft by direction, opens; Escape and outside press dismiss; focus returns to the trigger unless the close reason was hover, outside press, or focus-out) | Docs |
| Popover | Base UI `Popover` (`Popup` a `role="dialog"`; `Trigger` a wrapper control carrying `aria-haspopup="dialog"`, `aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`) | `Popover.Title` when rendered, else `aria-label` / `aria-labelledby` on `Popup` by the caller; the trigger never names the popup | `Trigger`'s rendered element; any focusable content in the popup gets `outline: none` on `Popup`, like Dialog's | Base UI (Tab leaves the popup, Escape closes, outside press dismisses, focus returns to the trigger; `modal` defaults to `false`) | Docs |
| Drawer | Base UI `Drawer` (`Popup` a `role="dialog"` with `aria-labelledby` and `aria-describedby`, inside a focus manager that restores focus) | `Drawer.Title`, always rendered; `Drawer.Description` optional — Dialog's row, because both parts are Dialog's own components | `Close`, and any focusable content; `outline: none` on `Popup`, like Dialog's | Base UI (Tab loops, Escape closes, focus returns to the trigger, a backdrop press and a swipe both dismiss, and the Android back gesture closes it through `CloseWatcher`) | Docs |
<!-- Base UI's Drawer page carries no accessibility section and no keyboard table, so this row is the only written statement of the contract rather than a restatement of one. -->
| Context Menu | Base UI `ContextMenu` (`Popup` a `role="menu"`; `Trigger` a `<div>` region that is not focusable and emits no role and no ARIA) | `aria-label` on `Popup` by the caller; there is no trigger text to inherit and no labelling relationship to the trigger | Nothing. The trigger is not focusable and carries no Ultima styles; items show `data-highlighted` instead, as Menu's and Select's do | Opening is pointer-only: right click, or a 500ms long press. Once open, Base UI (arrows loop, Enter, Space, typeahead, Escape, Tab closes) | Docs |
| Hover Card | Base UI `PreviewCard` (`Trigger` an `<a>` carrying only its own `id`; `Popup` a `<div tabindex="-1">` with no role and no `id`) | **None, deliberately.** The trigger is a link named by its own text, and the popup has no role, so there is nothing to name; see the bullet below | `Trigger`, and it is Ultima's own ring rather than a caller's element | Base UI (hover opens after 600ms for a mouse or pen only, `:focus-visible` opens it at the same delay, the pointer may travel into the popup, Escape and outside press close it, never opens on touch) | Docs |
| Menubar | Base UI `Menubar` (a `<div role="menubar">` with an auto-generated `id` and `aria-orientation` always emitted, including the redundant default) | `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby` on `Menubar`, required by the types as a union; no child part can name it, because its children are Menu roots | Nothing on the bar. The ring is whatever the consumer renders into each trigger, the wrapper-trigger rule reaching Dropdown Menu | Base UI (a roving tab stop across the triggers, Arrow Right and Left or Down and Up by orientation, Home and End, looping by default; no typeahead across triggers, and Tab leaves the bar rather than closing a menu) | Types: one of `'aria-label'` or `'aria-labelledby'` required on `Menubar` |
| Accordion | Base UI `Accordion` (`Header` an `<h3>`, changed through `render`; `Trigger` a `<button>` with `aria-expanded` and, while open, `aria-controls`; `Panel` a `role="region"` with `aria-labelledby` set unconditionally) | `Trigger` text names the button, and the button names the panel | Trigger | Base UI (native button: Space, Enter; no arrows, no Escape, no roving tabindex since the APG change); Tab moves between triggers | Docs |
| Avatar | Base UI `Avatar` (`Root` a `<span>` with no role; `Image` an `<img>`; `Fallback` a `<span>`). The primitive writes no ARIA in its default mode and one attribute under `keepMounted` | `alt` on `Avatar.Image`, the consumer's; `Avatar.Fallback`'s own text when the image is absent, still loading, or failed. `Root` is never named, having no role to name. An avatar beside a visible name is decorative whole: `alt=""` and `aria-hidden` on `Root` | None (not focusable) | Static | Docs |
| Scroll Area | Base UI `ScrollArea` (`Root`, `Viewport`, and `Content` all `role="presentation"`; `Scrollbar` and `Corner` `aria-hidden`; `Viewport` carries an explicit `tabindex` of `0` while either axis overflows and `-1` when neither does) | **None, and none is needed.** Every part is presentational, so there is no role to name. Unlike `Table.Scroll`, Ultima does not give the viewport `role="region"`: the role is Base UI's to supply and this contract adds none. A consumer who wants a named region passes `render` | `Viewport`, on `:focus-visible` | Native scrolling once the viewport has focus. Base UI adds no key handler. `Scrollbar` and `Thumb` are pointer-only | Docs |
| Toggle | Base UI `Toggle` (a `<button>` carrying `aria-pressed`, rendered through `useRenderElement` rather than as a composite item when there is no group) | Text content; an icon-only Toggle passes `aria-label` | Root | Base UI (Space, Enter); a native tab stop, not Toggle Group's roving tabindex | Docs |
| Command | Base UI `Autocomplete` (`<input role="combobox">`; `List` a `role="listbox"`; `Item` a `<div role="option">`) | Consumer `<label htmlFor>`, `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby`, or a `Field.Label` with the default `nativeLabel={true}`; the primitive ships no `Label` part. In the command-dialog recipe the Input takes `aria-label` | `InputGroup` (`:focus-within`); `Trigger` when it is the control. Items show `data-highlighted`, no ring | Base UI (arrows move the highlight, Enter activates the highlighted item, Escape closes the popup in the anchored arrangement; in `inline` mode there is no popup, so Escape is the host surface's — in the recipe, Dialog's) | Docs |
| Calendar | Zag `date-picker` machine run `inline` through `@zag-js/react` (`Table` a `role="grid"`; `TableHeader` a `<th scope="col">`; `TableCell` a `role="gridcell"`; `TableCellTrigger` a `<button>`; week-number cells `role="rowheader"`) | `Calendar.Label` naming the grid, or `aria-label` / `aria-labelledby` on `Table` | `PrevTrigger`, `NextTrigger`, `ViewTrigger`, `TableCellTrigger`, `MonthSelect`, `YearSelect` | Zag (arrows move the focused day, Page Up and Page Down change the month, Home and End the week, Enter and Space select) | Docs |
| Date Picker | Zag `date-picker` machine through `@zag-js/react` (`Input` an `<input>`; `Trigger` a `<button>` with `aria-haspopup="grid"`, `aria-expanded`, and `aria-controls`; `Content` `role="application"` with `aria-roledescription="datepicker"` and `tabIndex={-1}`; the grid parts as Calendar's) | `DatePicker.Label` naming `Input`, or `aria-label` / `aria-labelledby` on `Input`; `Trigger` is named by the machine's translated `aria-label`, so its glyph is decorative | `Control` (the `:focus-within` ring, Combobox's shape — the box is `Control`, focus lands on `Input`), `Trigger`, `ClearTrigger`, `PresetTrigger`, `PrevTrigger`, `NextTrigger`, `ViewTrigger`, `TableCellTrigger`, `MonthSelect`, `YearSelect`; `outline: none` on `Content` | Zag (typing parses through `parse`; Escape closes; the grid keys as Calendar's; focus management is the machine's) | Docs |
| Button Group | Plain: `Root` a `<div role="group">`; `Item` a Base UI `Button` (`<button>`) | `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby` on `Root`, documented rather than enforced for `ToggleGroup.Root`'s reason; each `Item` by its text or `aria-label` | `Item` | Native (Tab between items, Space and Enter activate; no roving tabindex, which is what separates a group of actions from a toolbar and the whole row's difference from `Toggle Group`'s) | Docs |
| Input Group | Plain: `Root` a `<div>` with no role; `Input` a Base UI `Input` (`<input>`) inside the box; `Addon` a `<div>` | The inner input's own: `<label htmlFor>`, `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby`, or a `Field.Label` | `Root` on `:focus-within`, Combobox's `InputGroup` row | Native (the control inside is a plain input) | Docs |
| Input OTP | `@base-ui/react/otp-field` (`Root` a `<div role="group">` merging the field's `aria-labelledby` and `aria-describedby`; each `Input` a real `<input>`; `Separator` the standalone `<div role="separator">`) | `Field.Label` or `<label htmlFor>` names the field through the first slot, the primitive ignoring `aria-label` there; slots 2 through N take `aria-label="Character N of M"`, the consumer's, with axe's `label` rule the backstop | `Input`, on the focused slot | Base UI (arrows and Home/End move between slots RTL-aware, Backspace and Delete clear and step back, paste distributes; one tab stop for the whole field through roving `tabIndex`) | Docs |
| Native Select | Plain: `Root` a `<div>` wrapper; `Select` a `<select>` rendered through Base UI `Field.Control`; the chevron is private, `aria-hidden`, and `pointer-events: none` | `<label htmlFor>`, `aria-label`, `aria-labelledby`, or a `Field.Label` — Input's row, the field reaching the control by registration | `Select` | Native (Space and arrows open the platform listbox) | Docs |
| Aspect Ratio | `<div>` through `useRender`; a `<figure>` through `render` | None. The frame has no role; the media inside carries its own name (`alt`) or is decorative | None | Static | Docs |
| Resizable | Zag splitter machine via `@zag-js/react` (`Root` and `Panel` plain `<div>`s; `Handle` a `<div role="separator">` with `tabindex="0"`, `aria-orientation`, `aria-valuenow`/`aria-valuemin`/`aria-valuemax`, and `aria-controls` naming the two adjacent panels; `HandleIndicator` a `<div>`) | `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby` on `Resizable.Handle`, required by the types as a union — no child part can name a resize boundary | `Handle` | Zag (arrows move the boundary by `keyboardResizeBy` pixels, 1 by default and 10 with Shift; Home and End to the panel limits; Enter toggles a collapsible panel; F6 and Shift+F6 cycle the handles) | Types: one of `'aria-label'` or `'aria-labelledby'` required on `Resizable.Handle` |

Rules the table compresses:

- **Types enforce four things, and Menubar is the first addition since v0.** `Tooltip.Trigger` requires `'aria-label': string`, because Base UI wires nothing between a tooltip and its trigger for assistive technology. `Sidebar.Panel` requires one of `'aria-label'` or `'aria-labelledby'`, as a union, because a page with a sidebar has at least two navigation landmarks and an unnamed one fails the APG's unique-label rule; the type cannot see which the author prefers, so it accepts either and rejects neither. Every other name source varies with context (a visible label, a labelling element, a child part), and a type cannot see children or siblings, so those are documented rules. Requiring `aria-label` on Input would steer authors to the worst of their three options. `ToggleGroup.Root` has the same shape as `Sidebar.Panel` — no child part can name it, so the name can only be an attribute — and stays a documented rule anyway: an unnamed second navigation landmark is a hard APG failure with a named rule behind it, while an unnamed `role="group"` is a weaker recommendation that no automated check flags. `NavigationMenu.Root` is the third documented case and has a reason of its own: Base UI renders it as a `<nav>` at the top level and a `<div>` when nested, so a type that required the attribute would force a meaningless one onto a nested menu, and a type cannot see nesting. Breadcrumb's and Pagination's roots supply defaults, and a type cannot require what already exists. **`Menubar` is the third enforced attribute**, added on [Menubar's shape, given Base UI ships it as a container rather than a namespace](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/110) as the same union as `Sidebar.Panel`'s, and it is the case that shows the absence of an automated check is an argument for a type rather than against one. **`Resizable.Handle` is the fourth enforced attribute**, added on [Aspect Ratio, Resizable, and Carousel contracts](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/279) as the same union as `Menubar`'s: a resize boundary is a control that never has text and no sibling or child can name it, so the name can only be an attribute, and no axe rule looks for a name on `separator`. `ToggleGroup.Root` stays documented on two grounds, that an unnamed `role="group"` is a weak recommendation and that nothing flags it, and only the second holds for a menubar: the APG's menubar pattern requires a name with no opt-out, while axe-core 4.13.0 has no menubar rule, so a documented rule would have no backstop at all where `Sidebar.Panel` has both a type and `landmark-unique`. Structurally it is `Sidebar.Panel` exactly — no child part can name it, and a type cannot see whether a labelling element exists, so it accepts either attribute and rejects neither — and it escapes both reasons the documented cases exist, since a menubar has no nesting case that would make the attribute meaningless and no sensible default, because `"Menubar"` would only restate the role where `"Breadcrumb"` names the navigation. Base UI's own canonical demo and shadcn's wrapper both ship a nameless menubar, which is what a documented rule was going to achieve. **Toggle is the fourth documented case and it is the cleanest**, settled on [Toggle's contract: one part or a namespace, its axes, and what names it](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/142): a text toggle names itself and only an icon-only one needs `aria-label`, a type cannot see whether children are text or a glyph, and requiring the attribute would put a redundant name on every text toggle. It is Button's situation exactly, and it is the case where the Menubar argument runs the other way, since axe-core's `button-name` flags a nameless toggle in the demo sweep and a documented rule has its backstop. **Avatar is the fifth documented case and the one with the strongest backstop of any of them**, settled on [Avatar's contract: its parts, its size and shape, and what an image without a name means](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/144): `alt` on `Avatar.Image` is documented, and axe-core 4.13.0's `image-alt` is in the default rule set the sweep runs, is tagged `wcag2a`, and decides the case by whether the attribute exists at all — its `has-alt` check returns `virtualNode.hasAttr('alt')`, verified against the installed package. So a missing `alt` is a violation, which catches the case WAI warns about where a screen reader reads the filename, and `alt=""` is a pass, which is what lets the attribute mean decorative. **Ultima defaults it to neither**, and that is the one place this row departs from React Spectrum, the only surveyed system to document a default: `alt=""` applied for the caller would silently satisfy `image-alt` on a standalone avatar that needs a real name, turning a failure the sweep catches into one it cannot. Breadcrumb's and Pagination's conditional landmark defaults are not a precedent against that, because those supply a name that is correct, and nothing can tell Ultima whether a given avatar is decorative. A required `alt` would also be the first enforced attribute on something that is neither an interactive control nor a landmark. **Four type-enforced attributes are now the total**, `Resizable.Handle` being the first on a control that is neither a trigger nor a landmark but a `separator` the APG promotes to a widget.
- **Wrapper triggers render the ring, not Ultima.** `Dropdown Menu`, `Tooltip`, `Dialog`, `Alert Dialog`, `Toast`, `Sidebar`, `Collapsible`, and `Popover` name a Trigger or Close in the focus-ring column, and those parts pass through unstyled under Styled parts above, so the ring comes from the element the consumer renders into the slot. The docs page for each says the slot must hold an Ultima Button or an element carrying its own ring. `Select.Trigger` is styled by Ultima and renders its own ring. Combobox's canonical ring is `:focus-within` on `InputGroup`; Slider's is `:has(:focus-visible)` on `Thumb`, because the focus target is the nested range input.
- **Icon-only Button** is a documented rule, not a component: no `IconButton` and no `iconOnly` prop in v0.
- **Input works without Field, and adding Field costs it nothing.** Standalone, its validation state is the consumer's `aria-invalid`, styled with `--ult-color-danger-border`. Inside a `Field.Root` it needs no new prop, no context read, and no wrapper: Base UI's `Input` *is* `Field.Control`, so it registers itself as the field's control and receives `aria-labelledby`, `aria-describedby`, `aria-invalid`, and the state attributes automatically. **There is exactly one source of validation state**, because the two paths are mutually exclusive in practice: a consumer inside a Field never writes `aria-invalid`, since Base UI owns it, and a consumer outside one has no Field to own it. The failure mode the contract rules out is the other shape, a Field-level `invalid` prop that Ultima forwards to the control by hand; Ultima forwards nothing, and `Field.Root`'s `invalid` is Base UI's own pass-through for an engine, per Forms. The one edit Field's build ticket makes to a shipped file is widening Input's selector to `':is([aria-invalid="true"], [data-invalid])'` — see State styling for why.
- **A field's error reaches assistive technology through `aria-describedby`, never `aria-errormessage`, and Ultima emits neither itself.** Base UI merges the `Field.Description` and `Field.Error` ids into one `aria-describedby` on the control and sets `aria-invalid` when the field is invalid, so the whole convention is inherited and the contract's usual split applies unchanged: Base UI supplies it, Ultima adds nothing and removes nothing. `aria-errormessage` is ruled out by evidence rather than taste, on ULT-76: none of six surveyed systems generates it, the string appears zero times in Base UI's published package, there is no WCAG technique for it at all, and it still fails on VoiceOver with Safari as of 2025-12-24. Announcement order is Base UI's, which is mount order, so a description that was present at first paint is announced before an error that appears later regardless of their order in the markup. Ultima does not reorder it.
- **`Field.Error` is not a live region, and must not become one.** Two independent reasons, and each is sufficient. Base UI's `Form` focuses the first invalid control on submit, which is a change of context under WCAG's own definition, which takes the error out of SC 4.1.3's scope entirely. And `aria-describedby` pointing at a `role="alert"` element loses the description on VoiceOver macOS, VoiceOver iOS, and Orca — and `Field.Error`'s id is precisely what `aria-describedby` points at, so a live region there is actively harmful rather than merely redundant. This agrees with the live-region pattern settled on ULT-83, with the mechanics measured first on ULT-77. **The reason is conditional on the submit path**, so the condition is part of the contract: the documented examples wrap fields in `Form`, and `validationMode` stays Base UI's default `onSubmit`. A consumer who validates on blur without moving focus is back inside 4.1.3 and owns a live region themselves, which the Field page says.
- **`Field.Label` is a real `<label for>`, and Base UI associates the name twice.** The control also carries `aria-labelledby` pointing at the same label. For a control that is not a labelable element, the label takes `nativeLabel={false}`, which drops `htmlFor` and leaves `aria-labelledby` to carry the name alone; without it the `for` points at a `<button>` or a `role="group"` and brings label-activation and hover behaviors the control should not inherit. `Select.Trigger` needs it. Canonical Combobox does not: the control is `Combobox.Input`, an `<input>`, so the default `nativeLabel={true}` stands; the Label-trap demo, where Trigger is the control, takes `false`. Slider needs it: `Slider.Root` is `role="group"` and is the field's control. Ultima passes the prop through and each control's docs page says whether it needs it. It is not type-enforced: which controls need it varies with what the consumer renders into the field, a type cannot see a sibling, and the contract's type-enforced attributes stay the total.
- **Required marking is the native attribute and nothing else.** `required` passes through to the control, and Base UI adds `aria-required` where the control is not a native input. Ultima ships no asterisk, no marker part, and no `necessityIndicator` prop: a visible marker is label text, and label text is the consumer's, which is the same line Icon-only Button sits on. W3C's own recipe puts "(required)" in the label text for exactly this reason. One documented exception, because the support data forces it: `aria-required` on a radio group fails on TalkBack entirely, so a required **group** puts the word "required" in its legend, which the Fieldset docs page says. A single control needs no such workaround.
- **A grouped control is named by `Fieldset.Legend` and gets one error for the whole group.** `Fieldset.Root` renders a real `<fieldset>` named through `aria-labelledby`, and a group-level `Field.Error` reaches the group through the same `aria-describedby` merge. **No per-item error**: `Field.Item` carries a label and a description and never an error, following both Primer implementations, because an individual checkbox or radio is not independently invalid. Two things are accepted knowingly rather than inherited silently. `Fieldset.Legend` renders a `<div>`, not a `<legend>`, which trades the forms-mode guarantee — screen readers in forms mode read `<legend>` and not a sibling `<div>` — for a legend Ultima can set to the type scale; the name still reaches assistive technology as a real accessible name on a real `<fieldset>`, which is what makes the trade affordable. And Base UI sets `aria-invalid` on a checkbox group's `role="group"`, which that attribute's Used in Roles list excludes; it is permitted on `radiogroup` and not on `group`. Ultima does not strip it, because the contract says Ultima removes nothing from what the primitive emits, but it is recorded here as a known deviation rather than discovered later by an audit. The documented grouping is Fieldset wrapping the group as children, so the `<fieldset>` stays in the tree; `Fieldset.Root render={<RadioGroup.Root />}` would replace it with the radiogroup and is not the shape.
- **Indeterminate is `aria-checked="mixed"`, which Base UI emits from the `indeterminate` prop.** Ultima does not set the native `indeterminate` property; that lives on the hidden input Base UI owns. The dash glyph is the visible signal, and colour is not. Data Table's select-all is a controlled `indeterminate` on a standalone Checkbox, because the header and the body rows are not one `Checkbox.Group`. `parent` plus `allValues` on `Checkbox.Group` is the form-list recipe for a select-all that *is* in the same group.
- **`Combobox.Empty` and `Combobox.Status` stay mounted.** They are `role="status"` polite live regions, and Core-AAM only maps text changes *inside* a live region; inserting the region itself is silent. The empty state is this part, not the v0.1 Empty component. Callers pass children into `Empty`; they do not replace it with a catalogue item.
- **A Slider's value is `aria-valuetext` on the nested range input, not a live region on `Value`.** Base UI locale-formats it by default and the prop is overridable per thumb. `Slider.Value` is an `<output htmlFor>` with `aria-live="off"` so a drag does not spam. Ultima does not wrap a formatter. The live-region pattern under Live regions below is not this part. A multi-thumb slider needs a distinct `aria-label` per thumb: the group label alone does not distinguish them.
- **Dialog always renders a Title.** Base UI sets `aria-labelledby` only when one exists. A design with no visible heading hides the Title through the `style` slot rather than omitting it. `modal` stays Base UI's default (`true`). Sidebar's mobile menu is the one stated exception: its popup takes the `aria-label` or `aria-labelledby` that `Sidebar.Panel` was given and renders no Title, because the name already exists on the `<nav>` inside it.
- **Popover's Title is optional, and the popup name is a documented rule.** Settled on [Popover's parts, its styled split, and what names it](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/108). A popover is a transient panel whose subject is usually its trigger, and no surveyed system requires a popover title, so this is not Dialog's always-rendered row and no type enforces it: a name a type cannot see (a `Title` child) cannot be required alongside the alternatives, and an unnamed `role="dialog"` is the checkable failure, which is why enforcement is the docs page and the axe sweep over its demos rather than a required prop. A popover with no visible heading takes `aria-label` on `Popup`.
- **A context menu must not be the only way to reach its actions.** Settled on [Context Menu against Dropdown Menu: restate the styled split, or compose it](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/109), and it follows from the row above rather than from taste. `ContextMenu.Trigger` emits no role and no `aria-*` attribute of any kind, never subscribes to the store's trigger props, and is not focusable, while `Menu.Trigger` sets `aria-haspopup`, `aria-expanded`, and `aria-controls`; verified in the 1.8.0 source, and #115's finding about Tooltip and PreviewCard emitting no ARIA reached a third time. With no focusable trigger and no keyboard opener, a keyboard-only or assistive-technology user has no path to the menu, so every action a context menu offers is also reachable from a visible control, and the canonical demo shows that pairing rather than a bare right-click target. Base UI's own documentation states the same rule. It is the one thing Context Menu's page carries that no other page does.
- **Context Menu's popup name stays documented and is not type-enforced, and the submenu is the reason.** Requiring `aria-label` on `ContextMenu.Popup` is tempting on `Tooltip.Trigger`'s reasoning, since nothing else can supply a name. It is declined on `NavigationMenu.Root`'s reasoning instead: a submenu's popup is a `ContextMenu.Popup` too, under `SubmenuRoot`, and Base UI names it from its `SubmenuTrigger` like any other submenu, so a required attribute would force a meaningless one onto every submenu and a type cannot see that nesting. Context Menu joins `ToggleGroup.Root` and `NavigationMenu.Root` as a documented case. The enforced total was two when this was written, three since [#110](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/110) required a name on `Menubar`, and four since [#279](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/279), which turned on Context Menu's own distinguishing fact: a menubar has no nesting case that would make the attribute meaningless, and a submenu popup does.
- **A Dropdown Menu inside a Menubar is a different composition, and Menubar's row governs it.** Settled on [Menubar's shape, given Base UI ships it as a container rather than a namespace](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/110). Dropdown Menu's row above is unchanged, because it describes the component standing alone, and this bullet carries what the composition changes so a builder does not have to read the primitive to find out. `Menu.Trigger` opts into being a composite item only inside a bar, so there its trigger takes `role="menuitem"` where a standalone one has no role, takes the roving `tabIndex` of `0` or `-1`, has its `modal` prop ignored and warned about because the bar owns modality, gains hover-open automatically while any menu in the bar is open, loses popup hover-close, defaults its popup to `bottom` and `start` rather than `bottom` and `center`, returns focus to its trigger on every close except an outside press, ORs its `disabled` with the bar's, and opens on `mousedown` while closing on `click`. Arrowing or hovering to the next trigger switches menus without closing: the outgoing one closes as a sibling rather than a dismissal, and the bar latches `data-has-submenu-open` through the swap. All of it is the primitive's, including the three menubar-specific requirements the APG names, so a consumer writes none of it.
- **Hover Card is not a labelled region, and its empty name cell is a decision rather than an omission.** Settled on [Hover Card's name, its contract, and whether hover-only disclosure is acceptable](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/111). Read from the 1.8.0 source, `PreviewCard` emits `aria-hidden` on `Arrow` and `role="presentation"` on `Backdrop` and on `Positioner`, and nothing else: its trigger has no `aria-expanded`, `aria-haspopup`, `aria-controls`, or `aria-describedby`, and its popup has no role and no `id`, so **there is no trigger-to-popup association at all**. Tooltip is identical, and `role="tooltip"` appears nowhere in the package. This is the precise form of the claim [#115](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/115) made as "no ARIA at all": **neither emits any ARIA that names, gives a role to, or associates the popup with its trigger**, and a grep scoped to one component's directory misses the positioner role, which comes from a shared helper. Popover is the contrast that draws the line: it emits `aria-haspopup="dialog"`, `aria-expanded`, and `aria-controls` on its trigger and `role="dialog"` with `aria-labelledby` and `aria-describedby` on its popup, so Popover is an ARIA dialog while Tooltip and Hover Card are visual only. Naming Hover Card's popup would mean Ultima giving it a role, which the opening paragraph of this section forbids. **Tooltip's own row needs no correction**: it names `aria-label` on the trigger as the source, never claimed a role, and the enforcement bullet above already says Base UI wires nothing between a tooltip and its trigger.
- **A component whose content is reachable only through some inputs is never the only path to that content.** One rule for two components: Context Menu, whose gap is the keyboard, settled on [#109](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/109), and Hover Card, whose gap is touch and the screen reader, settled on [#111](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/111). For Hover Card the primitive already satisfies WCAG 1.4.13 on all three counts — Escape dismisses through a document listener without moving hover or focus, a safe polygon lets the pointer travel onto the content, and no timer hides it — and **1.4.13 does not require focus-triggering**, which is advisory there and delegates to 2.1.1. **2.1.1 is the criterion that governs**, and its normative note is the whole answer: the requirement is only that keyboard users have a way to perform comparable actions and operate the content, not that every pointer gesture have a keyboard equivalent. So the card's content existing at the link's destination discharges 2.1.1, and **anything unique to the card fails it at Level A**. One finding makes that duplication load-bearing rather than polite: Base UI's guidance says the content is not screen-reader navigable, but nothing hides it — there is no `aria-hidden` and the positioner's inert option only sets `pointer-events: none` — so a keyboard user who Tabs to the link opens the card and its content enters the accessibility tree as orphaned, unlabelled text at the end of the document. Ultima cannot repair that without adding ARIA to the primitive, so the duplication is what makes the orphaned text harmless. **Nothing here is type-enforced.** Requiring a non-optional `href` on the trigger is tempting and is declined: `render` is granted on every Base UI part and [#91](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/91) kept it open for a caller's router link, so a required `href` would either forbid `render` or be satisfied by a dummy whenever it is used. The rule is that content also exists at another URL, which no type can express, so the docs page and its demo are the enforcement. Four type-enforced attributes remain the total.
- **Drawer's gestures lose a keyboard user nothing, and the one exception is a part rather than the component.** Settled on [Drawer's parts, its styled split, and whether it varies the overlay recipe](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/113). Every gesture has a non-gesture equivalent already in the part list: swipe-to-dismiss is also `Close`, also Escape, also a backdrop press; the Android back gesture is also Escape; snap points are a pointer refinement of a panel that opens and closes without them. So Drawer does **not** join Context Menu and Hover Card under the rule above. **`SwipeArea` does.** It is swipe-to-*open* and has no keyboard or pointer equivalent of its own, so a drawer whose only opener is a `SwipeArea` cannot be opened by keyboard at all: **ship a `Trigger` alongside any `SwipeArea`**, and the canonical demo shows both. One further thing the page owes a reader: `VirtualKeyboardProvider` writes `overflow-anchor`, `padding-bottom`, and `scroll-padding-bottom` as inline styles onto whichever of the consumer's elements it finds scrollable, computed from the existing values and restored on cleanup, and it reads `prefers-reduced-motion` itself to choose its scroll behavior.
- **Alert is not a live region.** `Root` is a `<div>` with no `role="alert"` and no `role="status"`. A callout that was in the page at first paint is not an interruption, and `role="alert"` on it is the APG misuse; a SPA navigation that mounts one would assertively interrupt. This is the Alert half of the agreement ULT-83 closed: do not put `role="alert"` on the catalogue Alert to make Toast's pattern simpler. A consumer who needs ARIA19 on a dynamically inserted validation summary uses `Root`'s `render` to add `role="alert"` themselves. Ultima's documented examples do not. `Icon` is `aria-hidden` and decorative; the visible text is the non-colour signal, the same rule as Badge. An icon-only Alert fails that rule. Title is canonical in demos and is not Dialog's always-rendered Title.
- **Alert Dialog always renders a Title and a Description.** Title for the same reason Dialog does. Description because the APG requires `aria-describedby` on `alertdialog` pointing at the alert message; Dialog's Description stays optional. Hide either through the `style` slot rather than omitting it. `modal` and `disablePointerDismissal` are not props: Base UI forces both `true` and `Omit`s them. Escape closes; a backdrop click does not. Ultima sets neither `initialFocus` nor `finalFocus`. The default first-focusable stands, so the accessibility contract and the destructive demo put the least-destructive action first in DOM (or the caller passes `initialFocus`) and a Delete confirm does not land on Delete. Proof bar item 7 is not this component: Ultima wires no focus of its own.
- **Toast inherits Base UI's live regions and does not replace them.** Viewport is `role="region"` with `aria-label="Notifications"`, `aria-live="polite"`, `aria-atomic={false}`, `aria-relevant="additions text"`, and `tabIndex={-1}`. F6 focuses it. Each Root is `role="dialog"`, or `"alertdialog"` at `priority: 'high'`, with `aria-modal={false}`. High-priority toasts announce through the primitive's sibling visually hidden `role="alert"` clone holding `title` and `description`; the visible Root is `aria-hidden` until the viewport is focused. Ultima does not restyle, wrap, or replace that clone, and does not build announce-only on the accidental path where a high-priority add with no Root still speaks. Portal and Viewport stay mounted when the stack is empty; a consumer who gates Portal on `toasts.length` defeats the region, because it has no SSR output and Core-AAM only maps changes inside an already-mounted region. Action and Close are wrapper triggers; the ring is the Button in the slot. Colour never carries meaning alone: Title (or Description) is the non-colour signal, same rule as Alert.
- **Progress inherits the primitive's `progressbar`.** Root is `role="progressbar"` with `aria-valuemin`, `aria-valuemax`, `aria-valuenow` (omitted when indeterminate), `aria-valuetext` (defaulting to `'indeterminate progress'` in that mode), and `aria-labelledby` from Label. Value stays `aria-hidden`. Ultima adds no live role. Item 2 and item 4 assert those attributes, not an announcement. Proof bar item 7 is not this component.
- **Spinner and Skeleton are decorative by default.** Each Root is `aria-hidden="true"`. The container being replaced or waited on takes `aria-busy="true"`. Start and end of a wait that needs speech uses the documented live-region pattern (pre-mounted `role="status"`, mutate once), not the glyph. Do not put `role="status"` or `role="alert"` on Spinner or Skeleton; a looping live region announces forever. Optional visible text sits next to Spinner as the caller's, not a looping name. Skeleton is not a `progressbar`.
- **Empty is not a live region.** `Root` is a `<div>` with no `role="alert"` and no `role="status"`, the same agreement Alert closed. Title is an `<h3>` by default through `useRender`. Visible text is the name; Icon is decorative. A retry Button in children is the control. Do not announce Empty as a live region when it was in the page at first paint.
- **Table** parts are `Table.Root` `<table>`, `Table.Head` `<thead>`, `Table.Body` `<tbody>`, `Table.Row` `<tr>`, `Table.HeadCell` `<th scope="col">` (scope overridable), `Table.Cell` `<td>`, `Table.Caption` `<caption>`, and the optional `Table.Scroll`. A consumer who needs horizontal scroll wraps `Table.Root` in `Table.Scroll` rather than writing the region themselves, decided on Contracts for the reusable patterns the docs pulls into v0 (ULT-57). A scroll region is tabbable and therefore needs a focus ring, which is Ultima's to own, and the docs' own tables are the first three callers. `Table.Scroll` is the one part in v0 that takes the ring while being neither a control nor a popup; `ScrollArea.Viewport` has the same shape and copied this row on [Scroll Area's contract: its six parts, its scrollbar visibility, and the focus ring it copies from Table.Scroll](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/145), with two stated differences and a third that runs the other way. **It does not copy `overflow: auto`**, because Base UI writes `overflow: scroll` inline and an inline style beats a StyleX class, so the copied declaration would ship and never apply. **It is not a named region**: `Table.Scroll` is an element Ultima writes entirely, so its `role` and its `aria-labelledby` are Ultima's to choose, while `ScrollArea.Viewport`'s `role="presentation"` is Base UI's and this contract adds nothing to it. **And `Table.Scroll` was the worse-behaved of the two in two ways, of which one is now corrected and the other is not a defect.** It set `tabIndex={0}` unconditionally, so a table that fits was still a tab stop, where `ScrollArea.Viewport` drops to `-1` when neither axis overflows, which is what Chromium and Firefox both do natively and what accessibilityinsights recommends; **that is now conditional, settled on [Whether Table.Scroll stays its own part now that Scroll Area exists, or the two converge](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/166)**, and the row immediately above this one already said `when the table overflows`, so the code had drifted from the contract rather than the contract being wrong. It renders `role="region"` unconditionally and that stays unconditional: a landmark that enters and leaves the accessibility tree as the viewport resizes is worse than a stable named one, and what makes the landmark correct is the name rather than the overflow. **A caption is therefore what makes `Table.Scroll` correct, settled on [Which of the docs site's three hand-built scroll regions Scroll Area replaces, and whether that is its live demonstration](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/147)**, and the uncaptioned case belongs to Scroll Area, whose viewport is `role="presentation"` and adds no landmark. Measured against the installed axe-core 4.13.0: `region` carries `type: 'landmark'` whether or not it is named, `landmark-unique-matches` matches on that type alone, and `landmark-is-unique` keys on role plus lowercased accessible text with `null` for an absent name, so two unnamed regions on one page agree on both keys and the second is a violation. **The two parts do not converge**, settled on [Whether Table.Scroll stays its own part now that Scroll Area exists, or the two converge](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/166): the split [#147](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/147) drew rests on `Table.Scroll` being a named landmark, which a `ScrollArea` composition cannot be, so converging would spend the one capability the split is made of. `table` gains no `registryDependencies` entry and no CSP note.
- **Native keyboard-focusable scroll containers change nothing Ultima owes, and both scroll regions say so rather than leaving a builder to assume otherwise.** Settled on [Scroll Area's contract: its six parts, its scrollbar visibility, and the focus ring it copies from Table.Scroll](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/145). Chromium ships the feature from 132 after rollbacks in 127 and 130, Firefox has had it since 2004, and WebKit has nothing — bug 190870, filed 2018, still open — so a scroll region without an explicit `tabindex` is unreachable by keyboard in Safari. The feature also never engages on either of Ultima's regions, because both carry an explicit `tabindex` and Blink's `IsKeyboardFocusableSlow` lets an explicit `tabindex` decide outright. Where the browsers differ from Base UI is in the other direction: Chromium drops a native scroller from the tab order when it contains any keyboard-focusable descendant and neither engine makes one mouse-focusable, while `ScrollArea.Viewport` is a tab stop regardless of its children and is click-focusable in both. That last one is why both regions ring on `':focus-visible'` rather than `':focus'` — a `:focus` ring would paint every time a reader clicked the text inside the region.
- **Code** blocks wrap long lines rather than scroll, so the block needs no `tabIndex`.
- **Plain components are static.** No `tabIndex`, no key handlers. Sidebar is the exception that proves it: its interactive parts are native `<a>` and `<button>` elements whose behavior is the browser's, and its `Trigger` sets `onClick`, `aria-expanded`, and `aria-controls` and nothing else. Site navigation is a list with disclosure buttons, never `role="tree"` or `role="menu"`, on the APG's own caution.
- **The current item is one mechanism in four components.** `Sidebar.Link`'s `active`, `Breadcrumb.Link`'s `active`, `Pagination.Page`'s `current`, and `NavigationMenu.Link`'s `active` each emit `aria-current="page"` with `data-active=""` beside it, and each styles it with `':is([data-active], [aria-current="page"])'`, so a router link that sets the attribute itself is styled with no prop. The first three pass it as `useRender` state; Navigation Menu's is Base UI's own prop. It is `"page"` and never `"true"`.
- **A navigation landmark carries an Ultima default name, emitted only when the caller named nothing.** Breadcrumb, Pagination, and `NavigationMenu.Popup` each do this, and the whole rule is in The navigation set. A second instance of the same component on one page is the consumer's to distinguish, which for Pagination knowingly departs from the APG's advice that pagination above and below a table share one label, because axe's `landmark-unique` is what the sweep enforces.
- **Pagination's ends are disabled and never removed, and never with the native attribute.** `Previous` and `Next` emit `aria-disabled="true"` and `data-disabled=""` and Ultima swallows the activation, because native `disabled` drops focus to `<body>` at the ends. That is a real bug MUI shipped a refocus workaround for in August 2026 and one Ultima's natively-disabled Button reproduces; keeping the element focusable means there is nothing to work around. The attribute pair is also the only form that works for both `<a>` and `<button>`, since an anchor has no `disabled` attribute and an anchor without `href` is not focusable. This is the first use of proof bar item 7 since v0.1.
- **Every Navigation Menu Trigger stays in the tab order.** `tabIndex: 0` is applied *after* the composite's roving `-1`, so roving does not govern the tab order here. A builder coming from Toolbar or Toggle Group would assume the opposite, which is why it is stated rather than left to the primitive.
- **Reduced motion.** Transitions are handled entirely by the motion tokens dropping to 1ms. Every transition reads a duration token, so Base UI's transition-aware unmount still fires. Looping animations cannot use that collapse: `--ult-motion-loop` becomes `0s` under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, and the component sets `animation-name: none` (plus the static fallback) under that query, under Motion above. That is the one reduced-motion exception to "components never write a `prefers-reduced-motion` query." The pattern still does not generalise to a breakpoint, because a breakpoint is the condition rather than a value inside one; Sidebar is the component that writes a breakpoint query, under its per-component notes.
- **Forced colors.** No `@media (forced-colors)` rules in v0. Native elements, `outline` rings, and state on real elements degrade on their own. The exception is `Switch.Thumb`, a `<span>` that would lose its fill, so it carries a hairline `border` so it stays visible.

Each component's docs page carries an Accessibility section restating its row. Automated checking is in the Testing section below: each row becomes assertions in that component's test file, and axe runs over every docs demo in both color modes.

### Live regions

Decided on Toast's contract, and whether Ultima owns a live-region pattern (ULT-83), with the mechanics measured on Live regions: what Base UI gives, and what a status announcement actually requires (ULT-77) and recorded in `docs/research/2026-09-11-live-regions.md`. Ultima owns a documented pattern, not a catalogue component and not a shared announcer. Base UI ships none. A LiveRegion item would paint nothing, which is why Form has no item, and `Combobox.Status` is off-label as a general region.

**The region's element is in the tree before the text changes.** Core-AAM §3.8.2 only maps changes *inside* a live region; inserting the region itself is silent. Never hide it with `display: none`, `hidden`, `aria-hidden`, or conditional rendering of the region. Mount it empty. A visually hidden region is clip-hidden (`clipPath: inset(50%)` and a 1px box), not `display: none`.

**Ultima-written UI uses `role="status"` with explicit `aria-atomic="true"`.** `role="alert"` is for a form error or equally imperative message, and Field.Error is already not that path. Identical consecutive text is not a change: append Word Joiner U+2060, matching Base UI and the docs copy button.

**Toast inherits Base UI's regions.** Viewport is the polite stack region. High-priority toasts announce through the primitive's `role="alert"` clone. Catalogue Alert stays a `<div>`. Field.Error stays not a live region. Combobox.Empty and Combobox.Status stay mounted `role="status"`. Slider.Value stays `aria-live="off"`. Progress inherits `progressbar` and is not a live region. Spinner and Skeleton are `aria-hidden`; they are not live regions. Empty is a static `<div>`, like Alert. Start and end of a wait that needs speech uses this pattern, not the Spinner.

**Copy-button stays docs-local** and keeps its own region; it does not import Toast. Data Table's row-count announcement, when that recipe ships, follows this pattern rather than installing an announcer.

**Enforcement.** Announcements are not assertable. axe-core has no live-region rules, and its `region` rule skips `alert`, `log`, and `status` subtrees. The docs surface check cannot see a missing region. Copy-button's mutation test is the v0.1 check for the one non-Toast caller. Toast's test file asserts Viewport attributes, that Portal and Viewport are present with an empty stack, the high-priority clone's `role="alert"` / `aria-atomic`, and the stacking CSS under proof bar item 8. Settled on ULT-85: no docs-source lint for missing live regions, no new CI job, and no ninth proof-bar item. Live-region structure is items 2 and 4 in that component's file. The canonical Toast demo mounts Provider, Portal, and Viewport so the empty Viewport is in the axe sweep; open and high-priority toast axe stays in `toast.test.tsx`.

### Overlays

Dialog, Dropdown Menu, Select, and Tooltip share one popup recipe. Sidebar's mobile menu is the fifth overlay and composes Dialog rather than adding a primitive, decided on Mobile navigation: reuse Dialog, or promote Drawer into v0 (ULT-55): it keeps Dialog's surface, backdrop, `z.popup`, and opacity fade, and replaces the `scale(0.98)` transform with a slide from the inline start over `--ult-motion-base`. Combobox is the sixth, settled on ULT-81: it joins the recipe on `Popup` rather than varying it, with no aligned-item default and so none of Select's `<style>` injection. Alert Dialog is the seventh, settled on ULT-82: it joins the recipe rather than varying it, restating Dialog's surface, visible backdrop, `z.popup`, and enter/exit in `alert-dialog.tsx` rather than importing Dialog. Dismissal is behaviour, not a recipe variation: Escape closes, a backdrop click does not. Sidebar's mobile menu remains the only stated variation on the recipe in v0, and it lives in `sidebar.tsx` as `style` overrides, so Dialog itself is unchanged. Toast, settled on ULT-83, is outside this recipe and is not an overlay at all, which is why the eighth is Navigation Menu below rather than Toast. It restates the overlay surface tokens on Root without the overlay enter/exit, and Viewport reads `z.toast` rather than `z.popup`. Navigation Menu is the eighth, settled on [Navigation Menu's parts, its two nav landmarks, and whether it is the eighth overlay](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/92): it portals a floating surface, wears the surface, and reads `z.popup`, so it is inside the recipe rather than outside it the way Toast is, and it is the second stated variation after Sidebar's mobile menu and the first in v0.2. It keeps the surface, `z.popup`, and the opacity fade, and **drops `scale(0.98)` and `transform-origin`**, because a transform fighting an animated `width` and `height` is the one combination the size morph cannot survive and the origin is inert without a transform. In their place `Popup` transitions `width` and `height`, `Positioner` transitions `top, left, right, bottom`, and `Content` carries a directional slide keyed on `data-activation-direction`, all over `--ult-motion-base` rather than the recipe's `--ult-motion-fast`, because a panel resizing is Sidebar's collapse gesture rather than a popup appearing. The variation lives in `navigation-menu.tsx`, so the recipe itself is unchanged. The v0.2 overlay set takes the recipe to twelve, settled on [What ships as an item, what ships as a recipe, and where each lands in the overlay recipe](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/107) in checklist order: Popover is the ninth, Drawer the tenth, Context Menu the eleventh, and Hover Card the twelfth. Popover, Context Menu, and Hover Card join unvaried — each portals an anchored surface, wears it, and reads `z.popup` — with Context Menu's backdrop an invisible click-catcher like Menu's, and Hover Card taking the full surface rather than Tooltip's narrower case, because a preview card is a panel rather than a one-line label. **Drawer is the third stated variation**, after Sidebar's mobile menu and Navigation Menu. It keeps the surface, Dialog's visible backdrop, and `z.popup`, and **drops `scale(0.98)` and `transform-origin`**: [What Base UI 1.8's Drawer actually is, and what it demands of the consumer's app shell](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/112) measured that `Popup`'s resting `transform` must read `var(--drawer-swipe-movement-*)` or the panel snaps back for a frame on release, so a scale would contend with the gesture on the one property the gesture owns, which is Navigation Menu's situation with a swipe in place of a size morph. The origin was inert here regardless, because Drawer has no Positioner and Dialog never seeded the variable either. In the scale's place it slides from its edge over `--ult-motion-base` rather than the recipe's `--ult-motion-fast`, because a panel crossing the viewport is Sidebar's mobile menu rather than a popup appearing, and the variation lives in `drawer.tsx`, so the recipe is again unchanged. **It also adds two things the recipe has never had**, settled on [Drawer's parts, its styled split, and whether it varies the overlay recipe](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/113), which is what makes it a variation rather than a member. **A third motion state, mid-gesture**: the recipe has an enter and an exit, and Drawer has a during, `[data-swiping]`, where the backdrop's transition duration is zero so the scrim tracks the finger. **And a velocity-scaled exit**: `Popup` and `Backdrop` both take `transition-duration: calc(var(--drawer-swipe-strength) * var(--ult-motion-base))` on the ending style, where the strength lands between 0.1 and 1 after a flick release and is exactly 1 for every other close, so one rule covers a flick, a slow release, and a click on `Close`. The slide itself is keyed on `data-swipe-direction` rather than on a side prop, since Base UI always emits it. **Menubar is outside this recipe and is not an overlay at all**, the sentence Toast already has and more plainly than Toast: it portals nothing, floats nothing, carries no backdrop, and is not dismissible. The overlays a menubar holds are the Dropdown Menu popups inside it, which are already the recipe's second member; the bar restates the overlay surface tokens without the enter and exit and reads no `z` at all. **Sheet takes no number**, because it introduces no component. It is the documented general form of the variation Sidebar's mobile menu already is — Dialog's surface, backdrop, `z.popup`, and opacity fade, with the scale replaced by a slide from an edge over `--ult-motion-base` — written as `style` overrides at the call site rather than in a file of its own. That leaves twelve overlays, three stated variations, and two components outside the recipe. Command is the thirteenth, settled on [#280](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/280): its anchored `Popup` joins the recipe unvaried, as Combobox's did as the sixth, and the `inline` arrangement renders no popup and makes no second claim. Any further overlay the docs needs follows the same shape: reuse the tokens and the transition, and document the placement and motion in its contract.

- **Surface.** `--ult-color-surface-raised`, a hairline `--ult-color-border`, `--ult-radius-lg`, and `--ult-shadow-md`. An overlay always sets a border and a shadow together, never a shadow alone. `z.popup`.
- **Transition.** `transform-origin: var(--transform-origin)`, which Base UI's positioner seeds before Floating UI measures; `Dialog.Popup` has no positioner and never seeds it, so the declaration is inert there and harmless. Transition `opacity` and `transform` over `--ult-motion-fast` with the `enter` easing; `[data-starting-style]` and `[data-ending-style]` both sit at `opacity: 0` and `transform: scale(0.98)`, with the `exit` easing on the closing side. Tooltip is the one narrower case: `--ult-radius-sm`, `--ult-shadow-sm`, and text step 2.
- **Backdrop.** Dialog, Alert Dialog, and Drawer have a visible one: `--ult-color-surface-overlay` over a `--ult-filter-backdrop` blur, the scrim fading opacity alone over `--ult-motion-base`. Drawer's opacity reads `--drawer-swipe-progress` rather than resting at 1, so the same scrim follows the finger. Base UI renders no backdrop when the root is nested inside another dialog root, and an app shell counts: `Sidebar.Root` wraps its children in a `Dialog.Root` for the mobile menu, so every overlay inside an Ultima app shell loses its scrim unless the caller passes `forceRender`, which the docs demos do. Menu's, Select's, Combobox's, Popover's, and Context Menu's backdrops are invisible click-catchers and stay unstyled; Context Menu's was settled on [#109](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/109) and belongs in this list. **Hover Card's is a third case**, settled on [#111](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/111): Base UI hard-codes `pointer-events: none`, `user-select: none`, and `role="presentation"` on it, so it cannot receive a click at all and dismissal comes from the document-level listeners instead. It is an inert styling hook rather than a catcher, it stays unstyled too, and `PreviewCard.Root` is modal in no sense, with no focus trap, no scroll lock, and no outside `aria-hidden`.
- **Reduced motion** needs no extra rule for enter and exit. Every transition duration is a token that already collapses to `1ms`, and Base UI's transition-aware unmount still fires at that duration. Looping animation is not this recipe; it is `--ult-motion-loop` and the `animation-name: none` exception, under Motion.
- **`keepMounted` is never set by Ultima.** Base UI's default (unmount when closed) stands, because how much closed DOM a page carries is the consumer's decision and the transition does not need the popup mounted. A consumer who wants it passes it through.

### Forms

Decided on What Ultima documents as its form integration (ULT-78) and measured on Form integration measured: Base UI Form against React Hook Form and TanStack Form (ULT-75), with the evidence in `docs/research/2026-09-11-form-integration.md`. It sits here rather than under Field's per-component notes because it reaches every form component on v0.1, which is why it was settled before Field's own contract rather than after.

**The documented integration is Base UI's `Form` and `Field` over native constraint validation, and it costs a consumer nothing.** `Form` is a primitive ADR 0002 already adopted, so naming it adds no install step, no registry dependency, and no caveat to Registry and install. Ultima ships no `form` item of its own: `Form` renders a `<form>` and paints nothing, which under Styled parts leaves it a component with no styled part, so an item wrapping it would cost a file and a name and return neither. The examples import it from `@base-ui/react/form` beside the Field a consumer installed, which costs that consumer nothing, because `field.tsx` has already put the package in their tree. It is also the only candidate in the measurement that supplies any ARIA at all: `Field` sets `aria-labelledby` from the label, accumulates one `aria-describedby` from the description and the error message, and sets `aria-invalid` when the field is invalid and not disabled. The three libraries measured beside it emit no `aria-*` attribute of any kind, verified against their published builds rather than inferred. And `Form` already ships the mechanism How comparable systems wire a field's label, description, and error message (ULT-76) found unanimous across six systems: its submit handler validates every registered field, focuses the first invalid control, and calls `preventDefault` before the consumer's `onSubmit` runs. That focus move is why `Field.Error` needs no live region, which matters because it renders a bare `<div>` with no role and would announce nothing on its own.

**A form library is an engine, so ADR 0007 already decides its shape and this is not a new category.** All three measured candidates pass that ADR's headless gate: none emits a `className` or a `style`, and the DOM any of them can render is a single optional `<form>` element. None supplies a role, ARIA state, keyboard handling, or focus management, which is the whole of what separates an engine from a primitive, so ADR 0002's scope never came into question here either. What the ADR needed was its scope stated more generally, recorded as an amendment on it: 0007 was written for a composition that *needs* an engine, and no v0.1 entry needs one.

**The engine Ultima writes a recipe for is React Hook Form.** Four things decide it over the runner-up. It has zero runtime dependencies and publishes CJS, ESM, and UMD, so Registry and install keeps exactly one ESM-only caveat rather than gaining a second. Both Base UI's handbook and shadcn/ui document it with working demos, so the recipe follows a primary source rather than inventing a shape. It carries `shouldFocusError` in its own options, so an engine-driven submit keeps the focus move the baseline depends on instead of re-owning it. And its per-field `fieldState` maps onto `Field.Root`'s `invalid`, `dirty`, and `touched` with nothing left over. Schemas are a second install, `@hookform/resolvers` plus the validator, and the recipe says so at its install step. TanStack Form is the runner-up and the cleaner engine by the gate, rendering no DOM whatsoever; it is declined on six transitive packages and on `@tanstack/form-core` importing `@tanstack/devtools-event-client` at module scope, which puts a devtools client in every production graph, and the measurement records no focus-the-first-invalid option of its own. It is the one to revisit if built-in Standard Schema support without an adapter package ever becomes the deciding cost. Formisch is declined on cost rather than on the gate: it is ESM-only with a required `valibot` peer, which would make two ESM-only caveats where there is one and put a schema library in the consumer's mandatory install rather than in their choice.

**Each rule is declared in exactly one place.** With an engine in play there are two live validation passes, and they co-operate rather than fight: an external `invalid` ANDs with Base UI's own verdict rather than replacing it, and `Form` still validates every registered field before the consumer's handler runs. What that does not survive is the same rule written twice, which produces two messages for one mistake. So a constraint the platform has an attribute for — `required`, `minLength`, `maxLength`, `pattern`, `step` — is declared on the control and belongs to Base UI, and everything the platform has no attribute for belongs to the engine: cross-field rules, schema validation, and async rules. Async is the clean case for that split. Base UI documents that an async `validate` cannot stop a submit under `validationMode="onSubmit"`, and the engine's own submit handler, which runs after Base UI's synchronous gate has passed, can.

**No Ultima file imports a form library, and Field's contract is what keeps it that way.** `item()` in `scripts/build-registry.ts` derives a `registry:ui` item's `dependencies` from its staged source's import specifiers alone, with no way to declare one away and no way to mark one optional, so a single import in `field.tsx` would reach Textarea, Checkbox, Radio Group, Combobox, and Slider through the one component each of them sits inside. Field instead exposes `invalid`, `dirty`, and `touched` as pass-through Base UI props from the day it ships, which is the whole of what an engine needs from it, so the recipe costs Field nothing later and every v0.1 item's `dependencies` stay what that component's own imports put there.

**The worked examples live on Field's page.** Field is what every other form component composes, so a submission example on each of their pages would be the same example written out again. That page carries two: the platform example, which demonstrates submission, validation, and the required, disabled, and invalid states with no dependency at all, and the recipe, which is the whole contract because it has no installable unit, so it names its component dependencies, states its install step, and carries the same checks a component would. Every other form component's page demonstrates its own disabled, required, and invalid states inside a `Field` and no submission of its own. The cost lands on the docs site rather than on a registry item: a live demo of the recipe means `apps/docs` installs the engine as a devDependency, the first time a demo has needed one. Settled on ULT-85: those applicable checks are the live demo in the axe sweep and the documented states on the page. `field.test.tsx` and `fieldset.test.tsx` assert association (`aria-describedby` merge), `aria-invalid` / `data-invalid`, and that Error has no live role. They do not import `@base-ui/react/form` to re-prove the primitive focusing the first invalid control, and they do not import `react-hook-form`. Item 7 is not Field: Ultima wires no submit. A static invalid Field demo is how the sweep sees `aria-invalid` and the error text.

**The shadcn precedent is worth half copying, and the half to refuse is the one v0.1 is graded on.** Their `field` item carries `dependencies: ["cn"]` and no form library, with four separate framework guides written over it, which is the same split as this decision and the opposite of the Data Table precedent where they ship no item at all. What not to copy is the guides. Across all four, `aria-describedby` appears zero times, the word `required` appears zero times, `aria-invalid` is hand-written by the consumer at every call site, and none of them has an accessibility section despite three of the four promising accessibility in their opening line. Those are precisely the states this milestone's line names. Ultima starts ahead of that because the description and error wiring come from `Field` rather than from the consumer, and the examples are graded on it because a recipe's example is covered by the applicable checks.

**Next.js changes nothing about the contract.** One documented path has to work in both supported targets, and the platform path does, because it is a `<form>` and a submit handler in either. A server action is a variation on the same components rather than a second integration: `Form` takes field-keyed server errors through its `errors` prop and clears each one on change, which is the prop a `useActionState` result feeds. It is documented as a variation on the Field page and carries no checks of its own. The engine recipe is client-side and identical in both targets.

### Iconography

Decided on Iconography in v0 (ULT-21). Base UI ships no icon set, and its glyph slots come in two shapes, corrected here on the assembly ticket because this section said all four v0 slots were empty containers and two of them are not. `Menu.CheckboxItemIndicator` and `Menu.RadioItemIndicator` are genuinely empty, and so are v0.1's `Checkbox.Indicator`, `Radio.Indicator`, and `Combobox.ChipRemove`; `Menu.SubmenuTrigger` has no trailing affordance of its own. `Select.Icon`, `Select.ItemIndicator`, `Combobox.Icon`, `Combobox.ItemIndicator`, and `Combobox.Clear` each render a default text glyph, `▼`, `✔️`, or `x`, measured in `@base-ui/react` 1.8.0. That is a fallback rather than an icon set, and it changes nothing Ultima does: every one of those slots takes `children`, so the override below replaces an empty container and a default glyph the same way. The foundation components fill those five slots. New components document any additional built-in glyphs in their contracts and follow the same private SVG convention. **A glyph slot need not be a Base UI part.** `Breadcrumb.Separator` and `Pagination.Ellipsis` are elements Ultima writes whose whole content is a glyph Ultima supplies, settled on [#88](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/88) and [#89](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/89), and each takes `children` as a replacement the same way. Neither is `Alert.Icon` or `Empty.Icon`, which are layout slots the caller fills and for which Ultima ships no default at all.

**No registry item declares an icon dependency.** Not `lucide-react`, not any other set. Ultima ships the glyphs in the table below, and a declared dependency would put a package in every consumer's tree for a handful of paths, pick their icon library on their behalf, and leave anyone already standardized on another set carrying two. Adding one later is additive, so this is cheap to reverse if a consumer ever asks.

**Glyphs are inline SVG, private to the component file that uses them.** Not exported, and not a shared `lib/icons` item. One file per component is the load-bearing rule and the registry rewards a self-contained item, so each file carries its own. The check glyph is written out five times rather than pulling a lib item into every component install; the dot is written out three times; the chevron-down three times; the chevron-right three times; the x twice in one file.

| Glyph | Slot | File |
| --- | --- | --- |
| chevron-down | `Select.Icon` | `select.tsx` |
| check | `Select.ItemIndicator` | `select.tsx` |
| check | `Menu.CheckboxItemIndicator` | `dropdown-menu.tsx` |
| check | `Checkbox.Indicator` | `checkbox.tsx` |
| dash | `Checkbox.Indicator`, when indeterminate | `checkbox.tsx` |
| dot | `Menu.RadioItemIndicator` | `dropdown-menu.tsx` |
| dot | `RadioGroup.Indicator` | `radio-group.tsx` |
| chevron-right | `Menu.SubmenuTrigger`, trailing | `dropdown-menu.tsx` |
| chevron-down | `Combobox.Icon` | `combobox.tsx` |
| check | `Combobox.ItemIndicator` | `combobox.tsx` |
| x | `Combobox.Clear` | `combobox.tsx` |
| x | `Combobox.ChipRemove` | `combobox.tsx` |
| chevron-right | `Breadcrumb.Separator` | `breadcrumb.tsx` |
| ellipsis | `Pagination.Ellipsis` | `pagination.tsx` |
| chevron-down | `NavigationMenu.Icon` | `navigation-menu.tsx` |
| check | `ContextMenu.CheckboxItemIndicator` | `context-menu.tsx` |
| dot | `ContextMenu.RadioItemIndicator` | `context-menu.tsx` |
| chevron-right | `ContextMenu.SubmenuTrigger`, trailing | `context-menu.tsx` |
| chevron-down | `Command.Icon` | `command.tsx` |
| x | `Command.Clear` | `command.tsx` |
| calendar | `DatePicker.Trigger` | `date-picker.tsx` |
| chevron-left | `DatePicker.PrevTrigger` | `date-picker.tsx` |
| chevron-right | `DatePicker.NextTrigger` | `date-picker.tsx` |
| x | `DatePicker.ClearTrigger` | `date-picker.tsx` |
| chevron-left | `Calendar.PrevTrigger` | `calendar.tsx` |
| chevron-right | `Calendar.NextTrigger` | `calendar.tsx` |

`select.tsx` and `dropdown-menu.tsx` are the only two files in v0 with built-in glyphs. `checkbox.tsx` and `radio-group.tsx` join them in v0.1, settled on ULT-80: `Checkbox.Indicator` and `Radio.Indicator` are genuinely empty containers, so Iconography applies, and the dash is a second glyph in the checkbox slot rather than a restyled check. `combobox.tsx` joins on ULT-81: `Icon` and `ItemIndicator` have the same default-text-glyph shape as Select's, replaced the same way, and `Clear` / `ChipRemove` take a private x (`ChipRemove` is the genuinely empty slot). `children` replace the slot, same override as Select. Alert does not: settled on ULT-82, `Alert.Icon` is a layout slot the caller fills, not a Base UI glyph slot Ultima owns, so the table does not grow. Toast does not either: settled on ULT-83, Action and Close are wrapper triggers, so there is nothing to put an X inside, the same reasoning as Dialog's close. Spinner does not: settled on ULT-84, the loading mark is CSS in `spinner.tsx`, not a glyph slot and not a lucide asset. `Empty.Icon` is Alert's layout slot again: the caller's, omitted when unused, so the table does not grow. Progress has no glyph slots. The three components the docs application added ship none. Sidebar settled that on Sidebar's public parts (ULT-53): its `Trigger` and `Close` pass through unstyled, so there is nothing to put a hamburger or an X inside, by the same reasoning as Dialog's close below, and a nested group's caret is the consumer's glyph rendered into `Sidebar.Button`, rotating on Collapsible's `data-panel-open`. Collapsible and Toggle Group settled it on Contracts for the reusable patterns the docs pulls into v0 (ULT-57), for the same reason in Collapsible's case and because a toggle's content is entirely the caller's in Toggle Group's. The navigation set adds three files in v0.2. `breadcrumb.tsx` and `pagination.tsx` carry the first glyph slots that are not Base UI parts: `Breadcrumb.Separator` is a markup node rather than the CSS pseudo-element the APG recommends, declined on two costs rather than on taste — StyleX styles only the element its class sits on, so the APG's own `li + li::before` has no expression there at all, and a rotated border or a text character cannot carry this section's 24x24, `stroke-width: 1.5` geometry — and `Pagination.Ellipsis` is that same shape a second time. `navigation-menu.tsx` joins on [#92](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/92): `NavigationMenu.Icon` ships a Base UI default `'▼'` text character that Base UI's own docs page does not mention, replaced exactly as `Select.Icon`'s is. **It does not rotate on `data-popup-open`**, against the maintainers' demo, on the reason Select's notes already give — one less transition to keep in step with the popup's — which is strictly stronger where the popup is running a size morph, a position transition, and a directional slide at once; the Trigger's own `data-popup-open` carries the open state. `context-menu.tsx` joins on [#109](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/109) and takes all three of `dropdown-menu.tsx`'s glyphs, because it restates that file's paint rather than importing it: the check, the dot, and the trailing chevron-right, which stays non-overridable there as it is in v0 because `SubmenuTrigger`'s children are the item's label. **Avatar does not grow the table either**, settled on [Avatar's contract: its parts, its size and shape, and what an image without a name means](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/144): `Avatar.Fallback` holds whatever the caller puts behind the image, usually initials, so it is `Alert.Icon`'s and `Empty.Icon`'s layout slot rather than a slot whose whole content is a glyph Ultima supplies, and a caller who wants a person mark renders their own into it. The two systems that ship one, Chakra and Mantine, reach it through a `name` prop Ultima does not add. `date-picker.tsx` and `calendar.tsx` join on [#281](https://github.com/frankieramirez/ultima/issues/281), the first glyph slots on a Zag primitive: the machine renders nothing inside its triggers, the same shape as `Breadcrumb.Separator`'s empty container, so each takes a private default — the calendar mark on `DatePicker.Trigger`, chevrons on `PrevTrigger`/`NextTrigger`, and an x on `ClearTrigger` — decorative in every case, since the machine's `translations` supply the trigger's accessible name, and `children` replace, same override. `MonthSelect` and `YearSelect` keep native appearance and take no glyph. A component added after v0 settles its glyph slots with its contract and follows the same private SVG convention.

**Geometry.** A 24×24 `viewBox`, `fill="none"`, `stroke="currentColor"`, `stroke-width: 1.5`, round caps and joins. The weight matches Phosphor's regular, which the docs demos use: Phosphor draws on a 256 grid with the stroke pre-expanded into filled geometry at 16 units, about 1.5px optical at 24px. Ultima keeps a 24×24 `viewBox` anyway, because hand-drawing these glyphs on a 256 grid buys nothing a reader can see. The seam is placed deliberately: a consumer's icon set is unknowable and a consumer who dislikes the chevron edits four lines in a file they own, while a mismatch on the docs pages is visible on every component page and fixable by nobody but us. `currentColor` means a glyph inherits its part's color and never names a token, which keeps the rule in Tokens in component code intact. The dot is a filled `<circle>`, the one exception to `fill="none"`. The dash is a stroke, not a filled rectangle. The x is two strokes.

**Size is `1em`, with `flex-shrink: 0`.** A glyph tracks the text step of the part holding it, so the 14px menu item and the 16px Select trigger get proportional chevrons with no coordination between them. No icon step joins the space scale, there is no `--ult-size-icon`, and no glyph gets a `size` axis. Phosphor's `IconContext` defaults to `size: "1em"` and `color: "currentColor"`, so the demo icons and Ultima's own glyphs size and color by the same mechanism with nothing to reconcile.

**Overriding.** A slot whose whole content is a glyph renders the default and takes `children` as a replacement:

```tsx
<BaseSelect.Icon {...props} {...stylex.props(styles.icon, style)}>{children ?? <ChevronDown />}</BaseSelect.Icon>
```

`Menu.SubmenuTrigger` is the exception. Its children are the item's label, so its chevron is appended after them and is not overridable in v0.

**The indicator slot.** Select reserves a leading space step 6 (16px) slot with a space step 4 (8px) gap on every item. Dropdown Menu checkbox and radio items reserve that slot at the trailing edge instead. All menu labels share the same left padding; submenu chevrons also sit at the trailing edge.

**Dialog's close affordance ships no glyph.** `Dialog.Close` passes through unstyled and is handed the consumer's own element, so there is nothing for Ultima to put an X inside.
